# Dross — Minecraft Forge mod

## Project summary
- Minecraft **1.20.1**, Forge **47.4.10** (recommended build), Java **17** (Temurin), Gradle 8.8 via the wrapper.
- Mod ID `dross`, display name `Dross`, base package `com.reg21meme.dross`, main class `Dross.java`.
- Mappings: `official` (Mojang names). Mod metadata: `src/main/resources/META-INF/mods.toml`, filled from `gradle.properties`.
- **GeckoLib 4.8.4** (Forge 1.20.1, `geckolib_version` in `gradle.properties`) draws and animates the boss models. It's a required dependency: players need it installed too (`mods.toml` lists it). `build.gradle` remaps its mixin refmap in the dev runs (`mixin.env.*` properties) and turns off its example mobs (`geckolib.disable_examples`).
- GitHub: https://github.com/Reg21meme/dross-mod (branch `main`).
- The user is a **beginner**. Explain changes in plain language. **Never commit or push unless the user asks.**

## Commands (Windows, run from the project folder)
- Build: `.\gradlew.bat build --console=plain` (output jar: `build/libs/dross-<version>.jar`)
- Run the game: `.\gradlew.bat runClient --console=plain`. This blocks until the game window closes. The user often closes the game themselves, which is normal.
- Game logs: `run/logs/latest.log` (and `debug.log`). Crash reports: `run/crash-reports/`.
- If a first-time setup fails with `NoSuchFileException` under `.gradle/caches/forge_gradle/maven_downloader`, that is a flaky parallel download. Re-run the same command.
- To test commands in-game, create the world with **Allow Cheats: ON**.
- Headless checks: `.\gradlew.bat runGameTestServer --console=plain` runs every GameTest without a game window. Add `-PgameTestWorld=<new folder>` for a brand-new world (the folder under `run/` must not exist yet; otherwise it reuses `run/world`). Confirm the `All N required tests passed :)` line: a datapack load failure can still exit 0.
- GameTests live in `src/main/java` (files named `*GameTest.java`) but **stay out of the mod jar**: `build.gradle`'s `jar` task excludes them and the test-only helpers `dimension/TerrainProbe`, `dimension/TerrainSurvey` and `enchant/RisenTestWait`. A new test-only helper class goes on that list, and game code must never use a test class (the jar would break).

## Rules for every agent
1. Forge 1.20.1 / Java 17 APIs only. Use `DeferredRegister` for all registrations. Watch out for newer-version APIs that don't exist here: no `ResourceLocation.fromNamespaceAndPath` (use `new ResourceLocation(Dross.MODID, "path")`), no data components, no NeoForge classes, no `neoforge.mods.toml`.
2. **Shared files: only ADD your own entries.** Never rewrite, reorder, rename or remove another area's entries. Shared files are:
   - `Dross.java`: add one line per thing you need registered on the mod event bus (for example `ModBlocks.BLOCKS.register(modEventBus);`). If `modEventBus` doesn't exist yet, add `IEventBus modEventBus = context.getModEventBus();` once at the top of the constructor.
   - Registry classes in `com.reg21meme.dross.registry`: `ModBlocks`, `ModBlockEntities`, `ModItems`, `ModEntities`, `ModParticles`, `ModCreativeTabs`, `ModEnchantments`, `ModLootModifiers`, `ModSounds`, `ModTreeDecorators`, `ModDensityFunctions`, `ModFeatures`, `ModPlacementModifiers`, `ModEffects`, `ModStructures` (create one if it doesn't exist yet, holding only the `DeferredRegister` and your entries).
   - `ModCreativeTabs`: the Dross tab is grouped by kind, in this order (the user's request): 1. blocks, 2. quest items, 3. trophies, 4. spawn eggs, 5. weapons and tools, 6. enchanted books (Necromancy, then Deathforged), 7. materials (Raw Moonsilver, Moonsilver Ingot, Stormite Shard), 8. crops (Frostroot, Snow Lichen, Rimeberries). Add a new item at the end of its group; the comment in the file lists the groups.
   - `src/main/resources/assets/dross/lang/en_us.json`: add keys only.
   - `DrossColors.java` (`com.reg21meme.dross.DrossColors`): add constants in your own area's section, and change only your own area's values.
3. **Stay in your own area** (see the table below). If you need something from another area, don't build it. Report it as "Needs from <area>: ...".
4. Prefer your own `@Mod.EventBusSubscriber` classes inside your package over adding event code to `Dross.java`.
5. After changes, run `.\gradlew.bat build --console=plain` and fix any errors **in your own area**. If the error is in another area's code, report it instead.
6. Finish with a short, beginner-friendly explanation: what you added, which files, and how to test it in-game.
7. Don't commit.
8. **Colors come from `DrossColors`.** Never hard-code a color in feature code (particles, glows, text styles, spawn eggs). If you need a new color, add a labeled constant in your area's section of `DrossColors`. Some older code still has hard-coded colors; move it onto `DrossColors` when you next change that code.
9. **`/dross` command names** say what kind of thing a command is about and which one, so they stay clear as more bosses and places arrive (world-builder adds them to `command/DrossCommand`; if you need one, report it as "Needs from world site"):
   - `goto <place>`: teleports (`goto cathedral`, `goto hub`, `goto trader`, `goto sewer`, `goto lake`). A kind of place names its kind first: `goto biome <name>` (one per Dross biome) and `goto lair <boss>` (`goto lair colossus`, `goto lair ratking`).
   - `showcase <kind> [<boss>]`: test showcases (`showcase boss_designs colossus`, `showcase boss_designs ratking`, `showcase boss_designs rimefall`, `showcase rats`, `showcase boss_sounds ratking`, `showcase trophies colossus`, `showcase trophies ratking`, `showcase trophies huntsman`, `showcase rimefall_mobs`, `showcase mob_sounds rimefall`, `showcase bossbars huntsman`, `showcase boss_sounds huntsman`, `showcase frostfolk_villages`, `showcase frostfolk_igloos`). A new boss adds one literal under each kind it has; a boss's minions get their own kind (`showcase rats`).
   - `goto lair huntsman` (the nearest Huntsman's village), `goto village [<x> <z>]` (the nearest hunter village) and,
     a test tool for now, `locate villages` (the 5 nearest, with [go] buttons); `showcase frostfolk` (the Frostfolk, Rimefall's
     villagers; never call them hunters).
   - `boss <action> <boss>`: boss test tools (`boss summon colossus`, `boss arena colossus`, `boss summon ratking`, `boss arena ratking`, `boss summon huntsman`, `boss arena huntsman`), and `boss arena bedrock` (a spawn-proof test arena for any boss).
   - `trader <action>`: the trader (`trader sendhome`).
   - `quest <action>`: quest progress (`quest status`, `quest reset`, `quest complete`).
10. **Making a new boss (or mini-boss): follow [`BOSS_GUIDE.md`](BOSS_GUIDE.md)** step by step: questions up front, a separate linked copy if other sessions are working, a Python generator in `tools/<boss>/` reusing the golem kit, checking every preview yourself, the Java and showcase commands, GameTests, the in-game photo check (`tools/photo_booth/`), the hand-over, and the docs after the user's pick. **Every boss's previews go in its own folder, `boss-previews/<biome>/<boss>/`**, in the folder of the biome it lives in (`cinder_wastes/cinder_colossus/`, `blightwood/rat_king/`, `rimefall/`; the user's request, October 18) (minions in a subfolder); nothing goes straight into `boss-previews/`.

Client-only code (renderers, particle providers, book screens) goes in a `client` subpackage of the area, for example `com.reg21meme.dross.villager.client`.

## The plan
The full design, including the **early-game quest** from `EARLY_GAME_PLAN.md`, which replaces the old test quest (nether star for a compass, netherite ingot lights the bare frame at 0,0, arrival next to an auto-built return portal).
- **[built]** means it's in the game now.
- **[to build]** means it's planned but not in the game yet. Step 14 (Rimefall's animals and the Huntsman's fight) is [to build]. Before it: the Cinder Colossus fight (step 8), the Rat King (step 9: Gnawrick 1C, his rats, sounds, fight and trophies) and the Rage Wand (step 10) are built and the user has tested them in-game; new work goes here first.

The Dross is a **mid-to-late game** place: players reach it after proving they survived dangerous places, not after beating every boss. It should work well alongside other mods. The only way in is the castle portal and the only way out is the hub, so the Dross stays dangerous.

### The look: electric blue [built]
- The Dross's color is **electric blue** (deep cobalt / electric blue, clearly different from vanilla's cyan soul fire). It replaced the old orange everywhere: portal texture, portal particles, screen swirl, frame, the trader's teleport particles and glow, the spawn egg spots, the risen undead's puffs and wisps, and the arrival title.
- Every color is a labeled constant in `DrossColors`. The palette is main `0x2E6BFF`, deep cobalt `0x0B2A9E` and highlight `0x7FA8FF`, kept in a shared "Palette" section of `DrossColors`. Each area points its own constants at it.
- A glow given through a scoreboard team can only use a chat color (`ChatFormatting.BLUE` is the closest); nothing uses one now. The trader's glow uses the exact RGB.
- **Not recolored:** the Admin Sword texture (parked for removal) and the soul tooltip text (stays gold).

### Steps
1. **Dross dimension** (`dross:dross`) [built]: real terrain (below), permanent night (`fixed_time` 18000), normal hostile mob spawn rates.
   - Zombies spawn in full netherite armor + netherite sword. Skeletons spawn in full netherite armor and keep their bows. Nothing is enchanted (including the bow). None of this gear drops on death. Risen undead (step 6) are skipped by this rule: their gear comes only from Deathforged.
   - Slimes, creepers and spiders are removed from the spawn list. **The Cinder Wastes** have their own list: only the Colossus's stone and lava golems (step 8). **Blightwood** has no zombies, skeletons or endermen either: only rats and witches (step 11). **Rimefall** has its own list too: only its animals, moose and Pond Skaters, and its few Rimehorn herds placed with the land (step 14).
   - Players see the dimension named **"The Dross"** (lang key `dimension.dross.dross`).
   - **Terrain** [built] (Terrain v1, October 2026; design `tools/terrain/TERRAIN_DESIGN.md`, tuning guide `tools/terrain/README.md`, report `TERRAIN_REPORT.md`): Minecraft's noise generator, set up in data files written by `tools/terrain/worldgen.py` (every tuning number a labeled constant). A 2D height field: no caves, overhangs or floating land (the only underground spaces are Blightwood's sewers, step 11). Rolling hills, cliff steps (9 and 18) and knolls in rocky areas, Cinder mesas, and the Gravecrags' snow-capped ridged peaks (about y 230-260). Five biomes, regions about 600-670 blocks across: **Silverwood Forest** (about 42%, always round the hub), **Cinder Wastes**, **Blightwood**, **Rimefall** and **The Gravecrags** (never within about 750 blocks of the hub). (There was a sixth, the **Nether Scar**, until October 17, 2026: the user had it removed. Its land went to the biomes round it without moving any other border, and its four blocks, Scarred Netherrack, Scar Roots, Soulwick and Scar Vent, are kept as building blocks that no longer generate.) Each biome has its own ground cover, features (trees, plants, ponds, lava pools, giant mushrooms, dead trees, lichen, boulders), fog/sky/water colours, particles and sounds; ores (coal, gold nuggets, Moonsilver, Stormite) lie under every biome. New worlds only: the old flat `dross_flats` biome was removed (October 17 cleanup).
   - **Blocks** [built]: 38 terrain blocks and 3 materials (listed in "Terrain v1" below), plus the sewers' Sewer Sludge and Blight Vent (step 11).
   - **Sky, fog and light** [built] (`dimension/client/`): night fog in each biome's colour, a blue glow over the night sky, a slight blue tint on sky light, no clouds, thicker fog in Blightwood; every part switchable and self-disabling on error.
   - [built] The hub at Dross 0,0 is a safe zone (step 5).

2. **Dross portal**
   - **Frame** [built]: a custom block, `dross:dross_portal_frame`, which **can't be crafted or mined** (no recipe, no drops, unbreakable, blast-proof). For testing, it has a block item that is only available in the creative **"Dross"** tab (or `/give`); it still has no recipe, drops nothing and can't be broken in survival. Flint and steel can't light it, and normal obsidian frames stay normal purple nether portals.
   - **Frame look** [built]: an **original** texture (dark stone with electric blue cracks), no longer based on Mojang's obsidian.
   - **"The Dross is leaking out"** [built]: frame blocks give off drifting electric blue particles and a low ambient sound. This applies to every frame, including the unlit castle frame.
   - **Lighting** [built]: throwing the **Rift Key** (step 7) into the empty middle of a valid frame (along X or Z) **uses up the key** and lights the portal.
     - If that frame is already lit, the key isn't used: it pops back out, with a portal cooldown so it doesn't drift into the Dross.
     - A netherite ingot no longer lights anything.
   - **No portals inside the Dross** [built]: the Rift Key does nothing there, and lighting a nether portal (flint and steel or fire on obsidian) is blocked there. Otherwise a nether portal would be a second way out.
   - **Portal look** [built]: Minecraft's nether portal texture copied and recolored **electric blue** (still a recolor, see Parked), blue particles, and a blue in-portal screen swirl.
   - **Stays open**: once lit, the castle portal stays open permanently, for everyone.
   - **Travel** [built]:
     - Every Dross portal outside the Dross leads to the **hub at Dross 0,0** (step 5).
     - The hub's exit portal always leads to the **castle portal** (`PortalSite`), wherever the player came in.
     - No more auto-built return portals, and no saved return-portal lookup.
     - All entities can travel. Never place an entity inside blocks or above a void drop.

3. **The castle (portal site)**
   - **The castle** [built]:
     - Placed once per world, at the site location, on flattened ground (reuse the existing site-placement logic). The frame is unlit and fully unbreakable.
     - If `data/dross/structures/portal_castle.nbt` exists, that template is placed. The user builds it in creative and saves it with a structure block as `dross:portal_castle`.
     - Otherwise the **Fallen Cathedral** [built] (shrine design No. 1, the user's pick; `world/PortalCathedral`, `ShrineDesign.buildFitted`, `world/shrine/GroundFit`, `world/shrine/TreeFelling`): its 3x5 frame opening (along X) sits on the site column (see **Location**), front facing south. Its 61x87 footprint is fitted gently into the (already nearly flat) ground:
       - the footprint is levelled at the median natural height by cutting or filling a few blocks; small dips and caves just under the ground are filled (never deeper than 8 / 16 blocks); no big foundations;
       - **staircase blend** [built]: around the footprint, a column d blocks out is never more than d above or below the build level, so the ground steps at most 1 block at a time back to the natural terrain (no cliffs or walls of dirt); one column is never changed by more than 8 blocks (`MAX_RING_CHANGE`); new ground keeps each column's own top layer (grass, podzol, sand over sandstone, snow);
       - **trees** [built]: only trees that touch the footprint or a reshaped column are removed, and always whole (logs, leaves, vines, bee nests, snow on them); leaves no remaining log holds up go too, other trees are left alone. Huge mushrooms and ice spikes follow the same rule.
       - **built a step a tick** [built] (`PortalCathedral.Building`, `ShrineDesign.startFitted` / `FittedBuild`, `GroundFit.Fitting`, `TreeFelling.Felling`; the Dross hub uses it too): felling the trees (the slow part, cut into steps of at most about 40 ms, `GroundFit.STEP_MILLIS`), levelling, sealing, snow, the template (one step) and the frame check each run on their own server ticks, with exactly the same result as all at once (`world/shrine/SteppedFitGameTest`); `buildFitted` still does it all at once. Needed right away (`PortalSiteBuilder.ensurePlaced`), the rest is done at once; if the server stops after the first tree is removed, the rest is finished before the world is saved.
     - Only if the cathedral can't be built (template missing, or its frame fails the check; an ERROR is logged), the small placeholder ruined shrine (`PortalShrine`, vanilla stone bricks, 4×5 frame along X) is built instead.
     - The template must contain exactly **one** complete Dross frame with an empty opening (either direction). The code finds it and stores its bottom corner **and axis**.
     - Structure blocks save at most 48×48×48 in 1.20.1, so a bigger castle needs several pieces.
   - **Location** [built] (`world/SiteRules`, `SiteSearch`, `SiteCheck`, `PortalSitePlacer`, `SiteVillages`; all rules are labeled constants in `SiteRules`): chosen once when a new world starts and saved in `PortalSiteData`.
     - A random spot **3,000–10,000 blocks from world spawn** in an allowed biome: plains, sunflower plains, desert, taiga, old growth pine/spruce taiga, snowy taiga, forest, flower forest, birch forest, old growth birch forest, dark forest, snowy plains, ice spikes. Never ocean, river (frozen ones too), beach or swamp, and no water under or within 4 blocks of the footprint.
     - The natural ground under the footprint must vary by **5 blocks or less** (`MAX_VARIATION`; a few small dips, up to 1% of the columns (`SMALL_DIP_SHARE`), don't count). No village (possible village starts, then the real village bounding boxes) within the margin around the blending ring.
     - Background search with the world generator's own height and biome maths (no chunks generated), then the best spot is checked in the real generated world (chunks loaded with tickets); a failed spot is remembered and the next tried. If nothing passes, the rules relax step by step with a WARN (6 → 8 → any dry-land biome at 8 → a last-resort steep level at 12), so a world always gets a site. Placing takes about a minute after a new world starts.
     - Before it's built, the `PortalSite` getters report the planned spot (a guess 3,000 blocks east of spawn in the first moments); `PortalSiteBuilder.ensurePlaced` (used by leaving the Dross) builds it right away if needed.
     - **Location in chat** [built] (`world/SiteAnnouncer`, lang key `dross.site.location`): when the site is placed (any build path, sent from `PortalSitePlacer.Job.placed`), everyone online gets "Dross portal site is at X, Y, Z" (`getOpeningCenter`), and each player gets it again whenever they join a world where it's placed. Plain text.
   - **`PortalSite` API is axis-aware** [built]: `getFramePos` (bottom corner), `getFrameAxis`, `getOpeningCenter` (middle block of the opening's bottom row) and `getOpeningWidth`. The compass, `/dross goto cathedral` and the hub's exit portal use them.
   - **Leaking** [built]: once, when the castle is placed, within about 16 blocks:
     - flowers and grass become dead bushes or are removed,
     - some grass blocks become coarse dirt,
     - stone bricks become cracked stone bricks.

     The particles and sound come from the frame blocks (step 2).
   - **`/dross goto cathedral`**: teleports the player in front of the frame, facing it (axis-aware). Before the site is placed it builds nothing and fails with "The Dross portal site hasn't been placed in this world yet." (like `/dross goto trader`). `world/PortalSiteGameTest` checks the join message and this command headlessly.
   - **Grand shrine designs** [built]: 10 big ruined landmarks were made as candidates to replace the small placeholder shrine; two are kept. Each has one unlit 5x7 frame (opening 3x5) at its heart and the Dross leaking out around it: glowing blue cracks (blue glass over sea lanterns, lapis), dead ground and plants, broken frame shards that leak dust.
     - The two kept: 1 Fallen Cathedral (61 wide, 87 deep, 55 tall) and 2 Shattered Spire (75 wide, 73 deep, 61 tall).
     - Each is a structure template (`data/dross/structures/shrine/<id>.nbt`) made by `tools/shrines/` (Python; see its README). Its front faces south and its ground is layer 2, two layers reach underground. Change a design in Python, then re-run the generator and `gen_java.py`.
     - **The user picked No. 1, the Fallen Cathedral, for the Overworld portal site (above) and No. 2, the Shattered Spire, for the hub in the Dross (step 5).** The other eight (3 Sunken Temple, 4 The Colosseum, 5 Overgrown Keep, 6 Fallen Colossus, 7 Monolith Ring, 8 Dead World Tree, 9 Riven Ziggurat, 10 Old Observatory), their templates and previews, and their showcase (`/dross showcase portal_shrines`) were removed (October 17, 2026).

4. **Dross trader** (villager) [built]: spawns once per world, in his own **hut at the edge of the closest village (to world spawn) that has room for it**. He wears skin 2, **The Archivist** [built] (see "Skins" below). He can be hurt and knocked back but **can never die**, because he only ever spawns once: at half health or less he teleports home and fully heals.
   - **Which village and when** (`TraderSpawner`, `VillageSearchPlan`): at world start he lists every possible village chunk within 5,000 blocks of spawn. He takes the **5 nearest villages within 1,000 blocks** (a 2,000 x 2,000 square), generates all 5 at once in the background (chunk tickets, 180 s timeout), and builds at the closest one with a good spot. If none fit, the next 5 farther out, the square growing by 1,000 blocks at a time up to 5,000. The log says which village was picked and why (one line per village checked). He spawns once the hut is built, which can take a while after the world opens.
   - **Emergency:** only if no village within 5,000 blocks has a spot (for example Generate Structures is off), the chapel is built on the nearest flat spot near world spawn (any biome, door facing spawn, no path) with a WARN in the log. `TraderSpawnData.isInVillage()` is false then.
   - **Hut: the Rift Chapel** (`villager/hut/RiftChapel`, the user's pick; `TraderHut.DESIGN`; the other nine candidate designs were removed on October 17, 2026), 9 wide x 13 deep x 18 tall. A small stone chapel: deepslate base, stone brick walls with lapis blocks here and there, buttresses, tall blue windows, a steep deepslate-tile roof, a blue rose window over the dark oak door, an open belfry with a soul lantern, and a spire topped with an end rod and a copper lightning rod. Inside: pews, blue banners, soul lanterns on chains, and behind the altar a broken piece of a real Dross portal frame (unbreakable, leaks blue dust and hums; it's incomplete, so it can't be lit). He stands in the aisle.
     - It sits in the village, right next to its houses (about 3 blocks from the nearest village piece), never overlapping or touching any house, street, decoration or path block (2-block margin around every village piece). Its door faces the nearest street, and it prefers flat ground. Small bumps are levelled: the floor may sit up to 3 blocks below the highest ground (bumps cut, grass put back) and up to 3 above the lowest (dips filled), choosing the least earthwork; the doorstep stays within 1 block of the floor.
     - A **3-wide path** in the village's own path material (dirt path; smooth sandstone in desert villages) follows the ground from its door to the nearest street. It only replaces natural ground.
     - The lightning rod draws natural lightning within 128 blocks to the spire (harmless, and it shields the village).
   - **Behavior:** he wanders within 50 blocks of his hut (X and Z) and opens his door. If he's more than 50 blocks out, falls more than 4 blocks, or is more than 3 blocks below the surface (not counting his hut, whose footprint is saved on him as `HutBox`, or village buildings), he teleports to his spot in the hut (the chapel's aisle) with the enderman sound and enderman-style particles (the portal's electric blue particles). If he's riding a boat, minecart or anything else, he gets off first (the vehicle stays where it was) and anything riding him is thrown off. He can't use portals.
   - **Glow (finding aid):** while any player is within his 50-block area, he glows electric blue with an outline visible through walls.
   - **Quest and dialogue:** see step 7.
   - **Shop:**
     - The old test trades (nether star → compass, the Admin Sword, Necromancy I–IV) are gone.
     - After a player completes the quest he sells **Necromancy (its lowest level)** for 32 emeralds + book and **Deathforged I** for 24 emeralds + book. Unlimited uses, no restock, no XP.
     - The offers are the same for everyone, but the trading screen only opens for players who completed the quest (received the Rift Key).
     - His key-forging line ends by pointing to the shop: "...be careful what you let out. I've got some books that can help you in there." (`dross.trader.dialogue.key_forged`).
     - **Dross Guide Book**: for players who have the "Enter the Dross" advancement, he also sells the Dross Guide Book for 3 books (no emeralds; a labeled constant). Unlimited uses, no restock, no XP. Players without that advancement don't see this offer.
   - **Spawn egg:** "Dross Trader Spawn Egg" in the creative Dross tab (electric blue spots). An egg trader treats the spot he was spawned at as his home, with the same 50-block area, glow and teleport-home rules. Having no hut footprint, he counts the 5x5 round that spot as his hut for the underground rule (`EGG_HOME_HALF` in `DrossTrader`).
   - **Test tools:** `/dross goto trader` teleports you to him (even if his area isn't loaded) and `/dross trader sendhome` sends him home. His entity ID is `dross:dross_trader`; `@e` selectors only find him while his area is loaded.
   - The old test-only join message (`TestingJoinMessage`, "[Dross test] ...") is removed.
   - **Location in chat** [built] (`villager/TraderAnnouncer`, lang key `dross.trader.location`): when the real trader spawns (village chapel or emergency chapel, sent from `TraderSpawner.buildAndSpawn`), everyone online gets "Dross trader's hut is at X, Y, Z" (`TraderSpawnData.getPos()`), and each player gets it again whenever they join a world where he has spawned. Spawn-egg (and `/summon`) traders never send it. Plain text; matches the site's message (step 3). `villager/TraderAnnouncerGameTest` checks it headlessly.
   - **Skins** [built]: 4 original electric blue skins on the vanilla villager model (`TraderSkins`), keeping their numbers from the ten candidates the user picked from: 1 Hooded Watcher, 2 The Archivist, 3 Ragged Seeker, 8 Blind Oracle.
     - **The Archivist** [built]: **the user picked skin 2, The Archivist, for the quest trader** (`TraderSkins.QUEST_TRADER`). Every trader wears it (the real trader and spawn-egg traders alike); the renderer uses it directly, so traders carry no skin number. The vanilla villager placeholder is retired.
     - Skins 3 and 8 are worn by the hub's survivors (step 5). Kept for later (parked): skin 1 for a Dross enemy.
     - The six candidates that weren't picked (4 Rift Regent, 5 Shard Hermit, 6 Wayfarer, 7 Stargazer, 9 Rift Warden, 10 Alchemist), the skin showcase (`/dross showcase trader_skins`, with its showcase traders) and the hut showcase (`/dross showcase trader_huts`) were removed (October 17, 2026).
     - The textures are drawn by `tools/skins/draw_skins.py`, which also renders the previews in `skin-previews/` (`all_skins.png` shows all four).

5. **Arriving in the Dross** (through the portal only, not `/execute in`)
   - **The hub** [built]:
     - Built once per world at Dross 0,0. Its center is a constant, `DrossHub.CENTER`, in the portal area.
     - **Built in the background** (`portal/DrossHubPlacer`), so creating a new world never waits for it: when the world has started, its chunks (the footprint, the ground round it and a bit more) get chunk tickets and the world generator's threads make them while the game runs; then it's surveyed, planned and built a step a server tick (`DrossHubBuilder.Build`: the spire through world-builder's `ShrineDesign.FittedBuild`, its trees felled in steps of at most about 40 ms, then levelling, sealing, snow, the template (the longest tick, about 0.1 s) and the frame lit, each on its own tick), and saved. The log gives each kind of step's time and the longest one tick's share. Anything that needs it before it's finished (an arrival through a portal, `/dross goto hub`, `DrossHub.layout`) finishes the rest at once, so nobody ever gets a half-built hub; `DrossHub.whenBuilt` waits for it instead. If the server stops before the first tree is removed (nothing in the world has changed yet), it's simply built the next time the world starts; after that, the rest is finished at once before the world is saved.
     - If `data/dross/structures/dross_hub.nbt` exists (the user's own structure-block design), that template is used. Otherwise the **Shattered Spire** [built] (shrine design No. 2, the user's pick), fitted onto the terrain like the cathedral (`portal/DrossHubBuilder`: `GroundFit` survey and plan at the median height, a staircase blend at most 1 block per step over 16 blocks, touching trees removed whole): its 3x5 frame (along X) has its middle column on `DrossHub.CENTER`, the gate faces south, and the frame is lit as the **exit portal** (saved in `DrossHubData` with its width and height).
     - Only if the spire can't be built or lit (an ERROR is logged), the old **placeholder** is built: an 11×11 platform with a lit 4×5 exit portal (at the centre if nothing was built; in front of the gate, clear of the spire, if the spire stands but its frame failed).
     - Players arrive inside the spire's hall, on the top step in front of the exit portal, facing the gate. If the exit frame is ever broken, they land on that same saved spot if it's safe.
     - Note: one of the spire's frame shards floats about 57 blocks above Dross 0,0, so anything that looks for "the ground" at 0,0 with a heightmap lands on it. Use the saved exit instead.
     - **Safe zone:** no **natural** hostile mob spawning within **48 blocks** (X and Z) of the hub center. Mobs can still walk in from outside. (`dimension/DrossHubSafeZone`)
   - **The survivors** [built] (`villager/Survivor*`, entity `dross:survivor`): **the Blind Oracle** (trader skin 8, the mentor) and **the Ragged Seeker** (skin 3, the student), both "he"; never called guards. Spawned once per world on the spire's dais beside the arrival step (`DrossHub.layout`). They walk together in and around the spire, never more than 20 blocks outside its footprint; exactly like the trader they can be hit but never die (at half health they heal and pop back to their spots), and hostile mobs and Necromancy risen undead ignore them. Right-click: their next lore line in chat (`dross.survivor.mentor|student.line.1-5`). No trades.
   - **Arrival sequence**, in `portal/DrossArrival.java`. Times are in ticks after arrival (20 ticks = 1 second):
     - **Title** [built]: at tick 20, "The Dross" (from `dimension.dross.dross`) fades in like `/title` (0.5 s in, 3.5 s on screen, 1 s out). Shown every time. The 1-second delay keeps it from being hidden behind the "Loading terrain" screen. It's electric blue (`DrossColors`).
     - **Four-note piano sequence** [built] ("dun, dun, DUN, dunnn"): note block harp, every time, only the arriving player hears it, on the Master volume. Notes start with the title: E, D, C# 0.4 s apart, then a 0.5 s gap and low F# (pitch 0.5, the lowest note block note). Volumes 0.6, 0.6, 0.8, 1.0.
     - **Advancement "Enter the Dross"** (`dross:entered_the_dross`) [built], **the main achievement**: "Step through the blue portal.", challenge frame (purple), Dross Portal Frame icon, in the Dross tab (step 7).
       - Granted through Quest's grant helper (`minecraft:impossible` trigger) 2 seconds after the last note, so its fanfare doesn't clash with the piano. If the player leaves the Dross before then, it's granted on their next arrival.
     - **Dross Guide Book** [built]: on the player's **first arrival only** (a flag that survives death), they get the **Dross Guide Book**: a custom item, readable like a written book, with its own original texture and placeholder text explaining the dimension (5 pages, lang keys). If their inventory is full, it drops at their feet. Given through `QuestProgress.giveGuideBookOnFirstArrival`. Later copies can be bought from the trader (step 4).

6. **Necromancy and Deathforged** (enchantments, `com.reg21meme.dross.enchant`) [built]. All tuning numbers are labeled constants at the top of their files.
   - **Necromancy** (swords only, I–V): "Hits have a chance to raise the undead to fight for you."

     | Level | Chance per hit | Max alive | Soul capacity | Types |
     |---|---|---|---|---|
     | I | 15% | 1 | 25 | zombie, skeleton |
     | II | 20% | 2 | 50 | zombie, skeleton |
     | III | 25% | 4 | 100 | + husk, stray, drowned |
     | IV | 30% | 6 | 250 | + chicken jockey, spider jockey, skeleton horseman |
     | V (admin, testing only) | 100% | 10 | infinite | same as IV |

   - **Hits:** only a melee hit with the sword in the main hand on the **main target** of a swing (sweep hits never roll). Hitting a player, the Dross trader or your own risen undead never raises any.
   - **How many:** if none of yours are alive, a success raises the full max; otherwise half the max, rounded up (I: 1, II: 1, III: 2, IV: 3, V: 5), never over the max ("alive" counts per player). Each costs 1 soul; a jockey/horseman takes 1 slot but costs 2 souls (with only 1 soul left, a normal undead rises instead). Not enough souls: raise as many as the souls allow. Each one's type is random from the types the level unlocks.
   - **Souls:** killing any zombie, skeleton or variant (anything that is a kind of zombie or skeleton) with the sword stores 1 soul, up to the level's capacity; risen undead give none. An electric blue wisp flies from the mob into the player's hand. Souls are stored on the sword itself (item tag), so each sword has its own count and it moves with the sword. Tooltip: "Souls: X / Y" ("Souls: ∞" at V; V never spends souls), in gold.
   - **Risen undead** (real vanilla mobs, owner stored in their persistent data) fight as a **pack**. A free risen undead picks, in order: a mob attacking or targeting its owner (within 16 blocks), the owner's target (hit within the last 5 s), a mob that hurt it, then any fight one of the owner's other risen undead within 16 blocks is in; otherwise it follows the owner. One that's already fighting keeps its target until it dies or becomes invalid; when a fight ends it helps allies still fighting before following. A target that is dead, dying or removed is dropped right away, even the mob whose hit raised them dying on that same hit, and any walk toward it stops at the same moment, so skeletons lower their bows instead of walking to the empty spot (`RisenTargetGoal` sets and clears every target itself and ends the fight with `endFight`; `RisenTargetGameTest` checks both headlessly). They never target or hurt players (arrows included), the Dross trader, villagers, wandering traders, or their owner's other risen undead (even if the owner hits a villager or wandering trader). The owner's own hits (sweep included) never damage their risen undead. **The player's allies leave them alone** (`RisenAllies`; the user's request, October 17): iron golems (village or player-built), snow golems and tamed wolves (anyone's) never target, chase, get angry at or hurt a risen undead (or a risen mount), whoever owns it; their own target searches skip risen undead, so a golem still finds a real monster standing behind them; an ally already fighting one drops it (target, anger, last attacker, its walk) within half a second (`CHECK_INTERVAL_TICKS` 10). The risen undead keep their rules: they never pick a golem or a wolf on their own (only their pack AI may set one as their target, so a zombie the golem hurts can't call risen zombies onto it), still defend their owner from one attacking the owner and still join in when the owner hits one; it just won't hit back. Rage Wand exception: an ally and a risen undead raged on different teams may fight (`RisenAllyGameTest` checks all of this headlessly). Whenever a risen undead damages a mob (arrows included), that mob switches its target to the risen undead that hit it, including zombies, skeletons and their variants, which normally ignore other undead. Risen drowned always fight on land, day or night. On Peaceful, Necromancy does nothing and no souls are spent. They crumble in a puff of electric blue particles after 60 seconds, or early if the owner logs out, dies or changes dimension, or if their chunk is reloaded from a save. No drops, no XP, no item pickup, no portals, no conversions (zombie→drowned, skeleton→stray). Vanilla burning rules (helmets protect, husks don't burn).
   - **Deathforged** (swords only, I–X; does nothing without Necromancy on the same sword): "Your risen undead come armored. Higher levels forge stronger gear." Armor (all types) / sword (zombie types only): I leather / none, II leather / wooden, III gold / gold, IV chainmail / stone, V iron / stone, VI iron / iron, VII diamond / iron, VIII diamond / diamond, IX netherite / netherite, X netherite / netherite + Strength I and Speed I for their whole life (particles hidden, no glow). Nothing is ever enchanted and nothing drops. Skeleton types always get a plain, unenchanted bow; without Deathforged: no armor. Mounts get no gear. The table is `GEAR_BY_LEVEL` in `DeathforgedEnchantment`; `DeathforgedGearGameTest` checks every level headlessly.
   - **Rules for both:** treasure only (never from the enchanting table, loot chests, fishing or normal librarians). Books work at an anvil. **Deathforged I–V** combine normally with vanilla XP costs (I+I→II … IV+IV→V), capped at V (V+V gives nothing); if both items carry Deathforged and either is VI–X, no result (a single VI–X book still goes onto a sword with no Deathforged). **Necromancy** never levels up at an anvil: two same-level Necromancy books (or a book onto a sword with that level) give no result, but two swords may combine; the result keeps the (higher) Necromancy level, only Deathforged combines (I–V rules above), and the souls from both swords add up, capped at the result's capacity. A combine that changes nothing (no enchantment gain, repair or rename) gives no result. Necromancy can't be combined with Smite. Rarity keeps Deathforged X under the anvil's "Too Expensive!" limit.
   - **Getting them:**
     - After the quest the trader sells Necromancy (lowest level) and Deathforged I (step 4).
     - Deathforged books drop only from the netherite-wearing zombies and skeletons of the Dross (`DrossMobGear.hasDrossGear`), when a player hit them recently: 5% per kill + 1% per Looting level; level weights I–X: 25, 20, 15, 12, 9, 7, 5, 4, 2, 1.
   - **Advancement "Rise!"** (`dross:rise`): "Raise the undead with Necromancy.", challenge frame (purple), zombie head icon, granted the first time a player raises undead, through Quest's grant helper. It's in the Dross tab (step 7), parent "Enter the Dross".
   - **Creative "Dross" tab:** the Admin Sword, the Dross Compass (a hidden marker tag; once it's in a player's inventory its target is set to the portal site), and enchanted books for every level of both enchantments (these stay even after the trader stops selling them). The tab is grouped by kind, in this order (the user's request, `registry/ModCreativeTabs`): **blocks** (the portal frame, then the 38 terrain blocks by biome), **quest items** (Weathered Letter, Dross Compass, Rift Key, Guide Book), **trophies** (the five Cinder Colossus trophy designs, then the Rat King's five, then the three mounted skulls, then the Huntsman's five), **spawn eggs** (trader, Cinder Colossus, stone golem, lava golem, Rat King, Plague Rat, Plague Ravager, Rimehorn, Moose, Pond Skater, Huntsman, then the Frostfolk's three: warrior, worker, elder), **weapons and tools** (Admin Sword, Rage Wand, Moonsilver Spear), **enchanted books** (Necromancy I-V, then Deathforged I-X), **materials** (Raw Moonsilver, Moonsilver Ingot, Stormite Shard), **crops** (Frostroot, Snow Lichen, Rimeberries; October 20). Dross enchanted books appear **only** in the Dross tab: vanilla's automatic copies in Ingredients (and search) and Combat are removed (`enchant/DrossBooksTabFilter`), so searching shows each book once, tagged "Dross".

7. **The early-game quest** [built]. Progress is **per player, per world**, saved by Quest (a `SavedData` keyed by player UUID). The trader's side (dialogue, hand-ins, gifts) is Villager's, through Quest's API.
   1. **Normal survival.** Nothing from the mod is needed yet.
   2. **The Weathered Letter** (Quest):
      - A custom item, readable like a written book. It opens `BookViewScreen` from client code, with its pages in the item's tag.
      - Found in **every Nether Fortress and Bastion Remnant chest**, 100% for testing (`LETTER_CHANCE = 1.0`, a labeled constant so it can be lowered later).
      - Added with a Forge **global loot modifier**, so other mods' loot changes still work. Tables: `minecraft:chests/nether_bridge`, `bastion_treasure`, `bastion_other`, `bastion_bridge`, `bastion_hoglin_stable`.
      - **The story (4 pages):**
        1. Strange blue portals have appeared across the world, in shapes no one built: rifts from the Dross, spilling over into ours.
        2. "We" closed them, but the Dross is pressing to break through again, and we need your help. An old villager has made his home at the edge of a village (or, if he's at the emergency spot, in an old chapel in the wilds).
        3. He's the writer's friend, looking for someone strong enough to help fight back against the Dross.
        4. A **rough location** of where he really lives: the direction from world spawn plus coordinates rounded to 50 (for example "…north-east of where the world began, near X 250, Z -100"), then "Tell him an old friend sent you about the rift." and the sign-off "- A friend".
      - Lang keys: `book.dross.weathered_letter.page1`, `page2_intro` (`_wild`), `page3`, and the location page `location`, `location_near_spawn`, `location_wild`, `location_near_spawn_wild`, `location_unknown`. Every page fits a book page (checked with Minecraft's font widths: 13 of 14 lines at most).
      - The text is written when the letter is created. If the hut isn't built yet, it uses vaguer wording, and rewrites itself once with the real location (within a second) when it's in a player's inventory after he has spawned. Creative-tab copies get their text filled in once they're in a player's inventory.
      - Picking one up grants "A Weathered Letter".
   3. **Meeting the trader** (Villager):
      - The first time a player right-clicks him, he speaks in chat with his name as the speaker: `<Dross Trader> You've come about the rift? I don't believe you're strong enough. Show me. Bring me a skull from the Nether's fortresses, shards from the cities of the deep dark, and stone from the End.`
      - Each later right-click (when he isn't taking an item) repeats what's still needed.
      - Every line is a lang key in `en_us.json`, so the wording can be edited without code.
      - Only the main hand counts. His trading screen never opens before the quest is complete.
   4. **The three tributes** (Villager, any order): 1 **wither skeleton skull**, 3 **echo shards**, 15 **end stone**.
      - Vanilla trades allow only two cost items, so instead the player **right-clicks him while holding** a tribute.
      - **Full amount only**: if they're holding at least the required amount, he takes exactly that much and confirms in chat. If not, he says how many to bring.
      - When all three are in, he gives the **Dross Compass** (dropped at the player's feet if their inventory is full) and says he knows how to forge a key but needs materials. This grants "Proven Worthy".
   5. **The key materials** (Villager, only after all tributes, any order, same rules): 1 **trident** (any), 1 **Heart of the Sea**, 8 **amethyst shards**.
      - When all are in, he forges the **Rift Key** and gives it with a line of dialogue. This grants "Keymaster".
      - The quest is now complete, and his shop opens for that player.
   6. **Lost items:**
      - If a player earned the compass and has none in their inventory, he gives a new one for free.
      - A lost Rift Key means bringing the key materials again (no free duplicates). After the quest he only takes key materials when the player has **no** Rift Key in their inventory; otherwise a right-click opens the shop.
      - The Rift Key is fireproof and never despawns.
   7. **The Rift Key** (Portal): a custom item with an original texture, a crystal key with a blue heart. Throwing it into the castle frame lights the portal and uses it up (step 2).
   8. **The Dross Compass** (Villager): a vanilla compass with lodestone tags, re-aimed every second while it's in a player's inventory. Moving the site later keeps compasses correct. It targets `PortalSite.getOpeningCenter` and spins in other dimensions.
   9. **The Dross advancement tab** (Quest): all Dross advancements move out of the vanilla Minecraft tab into their **own "Dross" tab**.
      - The tab has a root advancement with a background (the new frame texture). The root shows no toast and no chat message, and is granted silently when the player picks up a Weathered Letter, so **the tab stays hidden until then**.
      - Chain: root → **A Weathered Letter** → **Proven Worthy** → **Keymaster** → **Enter the Dross** (challenge, the main achievement), whose children are **Rise!** (challenge), **Set in Stone** (`set_in_stone`, challenge, obsidian icon: "Defeat the Cinder Colossus.", granted by the boss area through the grant helper; `/dross quest reset` leaves it alone, like "Rise!") and **Down the Drain** (`down_the_drain`, challenge, Rat King spawn egg icon: "End the Rat King's reign beneath Blightwood.", granted by the boss area through the grant helper; `/dross quest reset` leaves it alone) and **The Hunter Hunted** (`the_hunter_hunted`, challenge, Huntsman spawn egg icon: "Bring down the Huntsman and his Alpha Moose.", granted by the boss area through the grant helper; `/dross quest reset` leaves it alone).
      - There is **no** "The Rift Reopened" advancement.
      - Quest owns every advancement JSON and a grant helper. Other areas call the helper when their event happens.
      - Quest's progress API grants "Proven Worthy" and "Keymaster" itself when those stages complete.
   10. **Test commands** (cheats on):
       - `/dross goto hub` teleports you to the hub (World site hooks it up).
       - `/dross quest status|reset|complete` shows, clears or finishes your quest progress (Quest logic, World site hooks it up). `reset` also clears the Guide Book flag and revokes every Dross advancement except "Rise!" (which is about Necromancy, not the quest).
   11. **Creative "Dross" tab** additions: the Weathered Letter, the Rift Key, the Dross Guide Book (in its "quest items" group, step 6).

8. **The Cinder Colossus** (Boss area, `com.reg21meme.dross.boss.golem`): a golem mini-boss that serves the **Fire Necromancer** (one of the three elemental necromancers, Parked). **The user picked 3G Saddleback** (round 3, with changes; see "The pick" below), and its **fight is built** (see "The fight" below; not yet tested in-game). It lives in its **lair**, a volcano in the Cinder Wastes (below), and also comes from its spawn egg or `/dross boss summon colossus`. "Cinder Colossus" is a working name.
   - **The look** [built]: a big, brawny, gorilla-like brute, hunched over and walking on its knuckles, with huge fist arms (bigger than its legs), dense and heavy. Cracks instead of a clean face, and a layered jaw: an outer bottom jaw and an inner jaw behind it. **A volcano on its back** (prominent: it's where its long-range attacks will come from) and **spikes**. About **5.5 blocks tall** to the volcano's rim, about **5.2 wide** (spikes included) and about 4.3 deep. The head sits out in front of the chest so the jaw doesn't clip into the body. No blue or electric accents: fire colors only.
   - **The user's rules for the look** (from round 2):
     - Volcanoes and spikes **grow out of the back at its own angle**: never forced upright, never hanging off the body. Their feet sit flush on the back and are sunk into it (`volcano(direction=...)`, `spike(direction=..., embed=...)`, `back_dir()`); `tools/golems/kit/clipping.py` checks it on every build (every part that grows out of the body needs most of its foot on it, and parts mustn't pass through each other in any animation).
     - **Dark**, like 3C: scorched near-black stone and plenty of obsidian.
     - The crater's **center always shows lava** (even dormant).
     - **Spikes pointing up off the shoulders** (3C's) and a **beard** of obsidian shards (3D's).
     - Liked: the lava forms, the core volcano erupting all the time (throwing big magma), the death, the walk, the dormant look, and the erupt animation, which should **lean forward before the blast**.
     - From round 3: the eruption's blast and the lava form's constant fountain **shoot out along each volcano's own angle**, never straight up (the particles too); it **leans in further** before the blast; the walk's arms must move **smoothly** (no snapping); **3H's horns** on its head; the back spikes **a little larger**. Then: the **shoulders mustn't slide through the body** when it walks, and their fronts were a little too much.
   - **Three stages** (`GolemPhase`):
     1. **Dormant** (start of stage 1): a body of dark scorched stone and obsidian, mostly rock with a few dim red cracks and its lava glowing only dimly through the gaps; the volcano only smokes, but lava always glows in its crater.
     2. **Erupted** (halfway through stage 1): the volcano erupts and lava pours down over its body (a visible change: lava streams over its back, shoulders and arms).
     3. **Lava core** (stage 2): the shell breaks off; a body made entirely of lava, and the volcano, now bigger and made of lava, **erupts all the time** (a fountain, lava bombs, particles).
   - **Round 1** (five designs, October 2026): the user liked No. 3, **Old Calderon** (an ancient golem: a volcano's crater and basalt columns on its back, an obsidian beard, three eyes) and asked for variations of it. Round 1 is archived: `tools/golems/designs/round1/`, `boss-previews/cinder_wastes/cinder_colossus/round1/` (not in the game).
   - **Round 2** (four variations of No. 3: 3A Old Calderon, 3B Mount Calderon, 3C Spined Calderon, 3D Twinpeak; archived in `tools/golems/designs/round2/`, `boss-previews/cinder_wastes/cinder_colossus/round2/`): the user liked 3D's twin peaks and beard and 3C's dark look and upward shoulder spikes, and wanted a bigger volcano tried, but the volcanoes (3A and 3B most) stood upright on the sloping back with parts hanging off, and 3C's spikes stood straight up instead of following the back (see the rules above).
   - **Round 3** (four variations made to those rules, sharing `tools/golems/designs/calderon.py`: 3E Twinpeak, 3F Great Calderon, 3G Saddleback, 3H Spined Calderon): the user ruled out 3F and 3H and **picked 3G**, with the changes listed in the rules above. 3E, 3F and 3H are archived in `tools/golems/designs/round3/`, `boss-previews/cinder_wastes/cinder_colossus/round3/` (not in the game).
   - **The pick: 3G Saddleback** [built] (`tools/golems/designs/d3g_saddleback.py`, the only design built, in the showcase and in the fight; previews in `boss-previews/cinder_wastes/cinder_colossus/`: `01_saddleback_<dormant|erupted|core>_<front|side|back>.png`, the sheet `01_saddleback_sheet.png` with night shots and filmstrips, the fight page `01_saddleback_fight.png` (the Cracking look and every move), `minions.png`, and `all_golems.png`):
     - Two great volcanoes grow out of its back, their feet merging into one saddle of rock across the spine, each leaning back with the body and splayed out over a shoulder blade; wide craters that always glow with lava.
     - Big obsidian spikes in the saddle and down its lower back (raked further back the lower they are), their roots glowing where they break out of the stone; 3H's two obsidian horns on its brow (glowing roots too); spikes pointing up off its shoulders; spikes along its forearms; obsidian knuckles and studs; a beard of obsidian shards; three eyes in a cracked stone face; scorched near-black stone and obsidian.
     - As lava, two bigger molten volcanoes erupt along their own angles all the time; lava horns, lava spikes on its back and shoulders.
     - Its forearms are 2 units longer than round 2's, so its elbows are a little bent when it stands (about 35 degrees) and its arms can reach in the animations without locking straight.
     - Its shoulder pads (and their spikes, and the lava form's shoulder caps) sit on mounts at the shoulder joints that follow only about a third of the arm's swing (`PAD_FOLLOW`), like armour strapped to the body, so they don't slide in and out of the chest as it walks; the pads are trimmed at the front; the lava upper arm stops just above the joint (`ARM_TOP`) and stays behind the pad; stone collars either side of the neck cover the front of the shoulders.
   - **Animations** [built] (every design; GeckoLib, `animation.cinder_colossus.*`):
     - **idle**: breathing, looking around, shifting a fist. **walk**: knuckle-walking, a diagonal gait with fists and feet planted, a little lower than it stands, each lifted fist easing out and back in as it passes the body (the loop covers `walkBlocks` in `walkTicks`, `GolemDesigns`; the loop is seamless).
     - **erupt** (dormant to erupted): it braces (each fist steps a little wider), then pressure builds as it leans in over its fists (hips up, chest and head down: its back and volcanoes tilt about 30 degrees forward), trembling; the volcanoes blow (at 1.3 s: blasts of lava and rocks shooting out along each volcano, `volcano_burst`), it rears up and roars with its fists off the ground, the lava pours down over it (the flow layer, `FLOW_START`-`FLOW_END`), and its fists step back in.
     - **break_shell** (erupted to lava core): it tenses, rears up and roars, every plate (the rock volcano too) flies off on its own arc, lands and crumbles, and the lava volcano grows out of its back.
     - **volcano_erupting** (lava core, its own controller, loops): each fountain pulses out along its volcano and lava bombs fly out of the craters and fall with gravity.
     - **death** (any stage, 9.7 s): it rears back (its fists coming up off the ground) and screams for about 3.4 s (`DEATH_SCREAM` in `tools/golems/kit/anims.py`; the cooling times follow from it): in two waves, its jaw gaping wider and its head thrown back on each, swaying side to side and trembling harder; then it sinks onto its knuckles with its head bowed, and slowly cools from lava through dark crust into an obsidian statue (the glow fades out at 4.0-7.2 s, the statue texture fades in at 6.0-9.6 s; steam rises while it cools). The user asked for the long scream (only in the death: the eruption's and the shell-breaking's roars stay as they are) and the slow cooling. **statue**: the death's last pose, held.
   - **Showcase** [built]: `/dross showcase boss_designs colossus` spawns the design (3G), frozen (dormant), with a sign giving its number and name. `/dross showcase boss_designs colossus dormant|erupt|erupted|break|core|die|walk|idle` (also clickable in chat) drive every showcase golem within 64 blocks. Right-clicking one moves it on a stage (dormant > erupts > breaks out > dormant again); sneak + right-click makes it die. Showcase golems only die to `/kill`, never despawn (not even on Peaceful) and can't be pushed.
   - **The fight** [built, tested in-game by the user, October 2026] (`CinderColossus`, `ColossusAttackGoal`, `ColossusStage`; every number is a labeled constant in `ColossusTuning` or `ColossusStage`; built from the user's request, recorded below):
     - **Getting one:** the "Cinder Colossus Spawn Egg" in the Dross creative tab, or `/dross boss summon colossus` (it rises 10 blocks in front of you, facing you; not on Peaceful). `/dross boss arena colossus` builds a placeholder arena round you: a flat round floor of red nether bricks and similar blocks, 31 blocks across (a chiseled middle, a ring and a border of nether bricks, a few cracked bricks, shroomlights set in the floor so nothing spawns on it), a knee-high red nether brick wall round it with a 3-block gap at north, east, south and west (its charge stops at the wall), the air cleared 20 blocks up (chests and other block entities are left alone) and gaps under it filled; you stay in the middle, and its chat message has a [Summon the Cinder Colossus] button. The logic is `boss/BossCommands` and `boss/BossArena`; world-builder wires them into `/dross boss`. Its real home is the lair (below). **For any boss**, `/dross boss arena bedrock` (`boss/BedrockArena`; the user, October 25, 2026: "a boss arena made of bedrock so I can test spawns ... walls lit with glowstone so no other mobs can spawn") builds a square test arena round you: a bedrock floor 61 blocks across with glowstone set in it every 7 blocks, bedrock walls 8 high round it with two bands of glowstone (every 4 blocks, 2 and 6 up), the air cleared 30 up (block entities left alone); nothing spawns in it by itself (Minecraft never spawns a mob on bedrock or on a block as bright as glowstone); its chat message has a summon button for each boss. Headless test: `boss/BedrockArenaGameTest` (bedrock and glowstone only, its walls whole, the air clear, no zombie, skeleton, cow, stone golem, Pond Skater or moose may spawn on its floor or walls).
     - **Stats:** 300 health; immune to fire, lava, falling, drowning and suffocation; can't be knocked back, pushed or leashed; never despawns (except on Peaceful, like any monster); notices players it can see within 40 blocks; 50 XP.
     - **Boss bar:** shown to players within 40 blocks, hidden when they leave. The server sends a normal boss bar named `boss.dross.cinder_colossus.<shell|cracking|core>`; `boss/client/DrossBossBars` cancels Minecraft's drawing of it (vanilla bars have no orange) and draws its own: Shell obsidian purple with glints, Cracking obsidian purple split by glowing orange cracks, Lava Core lava red with bright streaks flowing along it. The name shows the stage. Colors in `DrossColors` ("Boss").
     - **Stages** (`ColossusStage`: armour / speed attribute (its walk animation speeds up to match, up to 3.6 times its own speed: `MAX_WALK_SPEEDUP` in `CinderColossus`) / damage x / cooldowns x / pause between moves / turn speed / ranged damage taken). The speeds are 25% higher than the first version's (0.15 / 0.17 / 0.20) at the user's request; Minecraft moves a walking mob by its speed squared, so it covers about 56% more ground: about 1.5, 2.0 and 2.8 blocks a second (a player walks 4.3). The charge's run (12 blocks a second, `CHARGE_SPEED`), the cooldowns and the turn speeds were left as they were:
       1. **Shell** (100% to 70% of its health; 60% until October 17, the user shortened it so the three stages take about the same number of hits): 12 / 0.1875 / 1.0 / 1.0 / 24 ticks / 6 degrees a tick / 50%. Slow and heavy: swipe, throw, slam, charge.
       2. **Cracking** (70% to 35%): 8 / 0.2125 / 1.15 / 0.8 / 16 / 8 / 35%. At 70% it plays the eruption (it can't be hurt during it) and turns into the erupted look plus the **cracking**: shell plates knocked loose (the design's `LOOSE` list: pushed out and tilted, lava glowing in the gaps; applied in `CinderColossusModel`) and bright lava cracks (the `_cracks` texture, `GolemCrackLayer`), growing as its health falls towards 35%. Adds the volcano lob and summons stone golems.
       3. **Lava Core** (35% to 0%): 4 / 0.25 / 1.3 / 0.6 / 10 / 11 / 15%. At 35% it plays the shell breaking (it can't be hurt). **Every hit sets you on fire** (5 s), its charge leaves a **fire trail**, it summons lava golems, its thrown rocks are molten, its volcanoes erupt all the time (the look already did).
       **Damage per stage** (the user's numbers, October 17; `ColossusStage`, in hearts: Shell / Cracking / Lava Core): swipe 6.5 / 7.5 / 8, charge 6 / 6.5 / 7, slam 3.5 / 4 / 5, the roar 0 / 1 / 2 (the first hit's roar is harmless; the eruption's roar 1, the shell burst's 2), lob blobs 2 in Cracking, 4 in the Lava Core. The "damage x" column now only scales the thrown rock (4.5 / 5.2) and the lava blobs (3.5 / 3.9 on a direct hit). Before: swipe 6 / 6.9 / 7.8, charge 5.5 / 6.3 / 7.2, slam 1 / 1.15 / 1.3, no roar damage, 2 blobs in both.
       **Ranged damage** (the user's numbers, `ColossusStage.rangedDamageTaken`): anything tagged `minecraft:is_projectile` (arrows, risen skeletons' too, tridents, fireballs, snowballs...) deals only **50%** in the Shell, **35%** in Cracking and **15%** in the Lava Core (cut in `CinderColossus.actuallyHurt` before its armour counts, so the stage clamp and the kill credit work as before; `/kill` is never cut). Melee (swords, risen undead's blows, Strength, Sharpness) is never reduced, and the minions take full ranged damage. Feedback where the projectile hits: Shell and Cracking a pitched-up stone clink and a few sparks (`DrossColors.COLOSSUS_RANGED_SPARK`), Lava Core a short hiss and a puff of smoke with embers (`COLOSSUS_RANGED_EMBER`).
       **Pickaxes crack the shell** (the user's request, October 2026): in the Shell and Cracking stages a melee hit from anything holding an item tagged `minecraft:pickaxes` (modded pickaxes too) does **2x** (`PICKAXE_DAMAGE_TAKEN`, before armour, like the ranged cut; never for projectiles or `/kill`; the Lava Core takes normal damage).
       **Facing** (the user's request: it should face the player it targets about 90% of the time): it steers straight at its target whenever the way is clear (`boss/BossChase`: in sight, about level, no wall, drop or liquid in the next 8 blocks; otherwise the pathfinder) and keeps turning during its moves, the eruption and the shell breaking, except just before a blow lands (`SWIPE_COMMIT_TICKS`, `SLAM_COMMIT_TICKS`, the throw's and lob's release) and during the gallop. Checked headlessly: it faced its target 96.7% of a scripted fight (`ColossusFightGameTest.facesItsTargetMostOfTheTime`).
       **The Lava Core roar** starts as it begins raising its arms in the shell breaking (`BREAK_ROAR_LEAD`, 0.45 s before the burst), so you hear it while its jaw is open; the crack and the roar's push stay at the burst.
       A hit never skips a stage: the health stops at the threshold (`/kill` isn't stopped). The stage is the look (`GolemPhase`, saved), so a rejoin mid-fight restores the look, the stage's armour and speed, and the bar.
     - **Moves** (`ColossusAttackGoal`; it walks up to its target and, between pauses, picks one of the moves that fit at random; a move always plays to its end; hits and projectiles line up with the animations through `GolemDesigns.Moves`):
       - **Swipe** (melee, 25-tick cooldown): a backhand sweep of its right fist: everything within 3 blocks of its front (a 160-degree arc) takes 6.5 / 7.5 / 8 hearts by stage and is knocked away to its left.
       - **Throw** (6 to 28 blocks, 110 ticks): it punches a fist into the ground, tears out a rock (shown in its fist), lifts it high and hurls it: `dross:colossus_rock`, 9 damage and knockback, aimed where the player will be; it shatters into dust and **never places or breaks a block**. Shell and Cracking only: the Lava Core never throws rocks (round 3).
       - **Ground slam** (a player within 7 blocks, more likely with two or more; 200 ticks): both fists raised high for about a second with a roar (the warning), then smashed down: a shockwave 6.5 blocks round where they land, 3.5 / 4 / 5 hearts by stage, and it throws players far (`SLAM_KNOCKBACK` 2.4: about 20 blocks a few blocks from the middle, up to 23 right at it, about 12 at the edge; about 15 before October 17); rings of dust.
       - **Charge** (7 to 32 blocks with line of sight and a clear-ish run; cooldown 9 / 6 / 4 s by stage; round 3): head down, a fist raking the ground (1.3 s), then a gallop on all fours, head first, at 12 blocks a second **all the way to where the player stood** (at most 32 blocks; during the wind-up it follows the player with its eyes and the spot locks 0.5 s before the gallop, `CHARGE_AIM_LOCK_TICKS`; after the run it wheels round to face its target); everything in its way takes 6 / 6.5 / 7 hearts by stage and is thrown aside and up; running into a wall dazes it for a moment; a trail of fire behind it in Cracking (2 to 2.5 s) and the Lava Core (4 to 5 s).
       - **Volcano lob** (Cracking and Lava Core, 6 to 32 blocks, 240 ticks): a rumble as it hunches, then its volcanoes spit lava blobs (`dross:lava_blob`; 2 in Cracking, 4 in the Lava Core, `ColossusStage.lobBlobs`) on high arcs, landing 4 to 6 blocks from the player spread round them (never on them); each leaves a roughly round pool of temporary lava (at most 3 blocks out from its middle) for 20 to 40 seconds.
       - **Summon** (Cracking and Lava Core, when fewer than 2 of its minions are alive, never more than 6; 500 ticks): a roar, then three pounds: a minion climbs out of the ground at each (to its left, its right, in front of it, 4.5 blocks out).
       - **Against Necromancy** (round 3): with risen undead within 24 blocks, a summon raises one golem per two risen undead (never more than 6 alive), its golems go for the risen undead first, and it may summon in every stage while it has fewer golems than half the risen undead (a 35% summon chance where the table below has none).
       - **How it picks a move** (the user's table, round 3; `ColossusTuning` `SHELL_/CRACKING_/CORE_CHANCES`; moves that can't be used right now drop out and the rest are rescaled; bands: close up to 7 blocks, mid 7-18, far 18-28, very far 28-32): Shell close Swipe 60 / Slam 40, mid Charge 50 / Throw 50, far Charge 40 / Throw 60, very far Charge 100; Cracking close Swipe 40 / Slam 25 / Summon 35, mid Charge 35 / Lob 25 / Throw 20 / Summon 20, far Charge 45 / Lob 25 / Throw 15 / Summon 15, very far Charge 70 / Lob 30; Lava Core close Swipe 40 / Slam 30 / Summon 30, mid Charge 50 / Lob 35 / Summon 15, far Charge 60 / Lob 40, very far Charge 70 / Lob 30. When 3 or more risen undead attack it within 7 blocks, 20 points move from Swipe to Slam. Picking Swipe from 5-7 blocks away, it steps in first.
       - **The roar** (round 3): the first time it's hurt in a fight and at both stage changes, a dust ring rolls out and every player within 15 blocks is thrown back about 15 blocks (players only); the first hit's roar does no damage, the eruption's 1 heart and the shell burst's 2 (October 17).
       - **Stage-change attacks** (round 3): entering Cracking, 4 stone golems climb out at once; entering the Lava Core, 4 lava blobs land 4-6 blocks from the players (never on them).
     - **Minions** (`GolemMinion`; models and animations by tools/golems, `designs/minions.py`; about a block tall): **Stone Golem** (`dross:stone_golem`, the Cracking stage: 12 health, 3 damage, armour 4, speed 0.2645, about 3.1 blocks a second) and **Lava Golem** (`dross:lava_golem`, the Lava Core: 8 health, 2 damage and 4 s of fire, speed 0.322, about 4.6 blocks a second (a little faster than a walking player, slower than a sprinting one); 7 s after it climbs out it swells and hisses for 1.5 s and blows up if nobody killed it: up to 7 damage within 3 blocks and fire, **no blocks broken**). Both are 15% faster than the first version (0.23 and 0.28) at the user's request; their walk animation speeds up to match (up to 3 times its own speed, `MAX_WALK_SPEEDUP` in `GolemMinion`). Fire-immune; they crumble when their Colossus dies or is gone; 2 XP each, no drops. **Wild golems** (round 2-3): in the Cinder Wastes they're the only mobs (stone 80 / lava 20 by weight, groups of 1, a `spawn_costs` crowding limit, any light; a lava golem only within 6 blocks of lava), and a top-up keeps lava golems about 1 in 5 of the wild golems near each player there, always next to lava (`WildLavaGolemTopUp`). A wild golem (natural, spawn egg, `/summon`) never crumbles, despawns like a monster, and a wild lava golem lights its fuse only within 2.5 blocks of its target.
     - **Temporary lava and fire** (`dross:cinder_lava`, `dross:cinder_fire`, placed by `LavaPools`): the mod's own blocks, not Minecraft's lava or fire. They burn and hurt like lava (you wade through it slowly) and fire, but never spread, can't be picked up with a bucket, never make cobblestone or obsidian (water just washes the lava away) and drop nothing. They go only into empty air (or, with the mob griefing rule on, in place of soft plants: grass, flowers, thin snow), on solid ground; never into water, never replacing anything solid or any block with a block entity (chests...). They clean themselves up with scheduled block ticks, which are saved with the chunk (an unload and reload doesn't strand them; a random tick restarts a lost countdown). A pool cools in from its edge, its last step a dark crust.
     - **Death:** the cooling death into an obsidian statue (it stays standing, no red flash); the statue stands 2 minutes, then crumbles into obsidian dust. 50 XP, and a **trophy** for every player who helped (below); no other drops. Its minions crumble. **"Set in Stone"** (`dross:set_in_stone`, Quest's JSON, granted through `DrossAdvancements.grant`) goes to the player credited with the kill (their own hit, arrow, pet or Necromancy risen undead; wherever they are) and to every player within 40 blocks, but not when it's removed with `/kill`.
     - **Sounds** (`registry/ModSounds`, `assets/dross/sounds.json`; vanilla grindstone, iron golem, ravager, basalt, deepslate, explosion and lava sounds pitched down; no new audio files): grinding stone (idle), a lava gurgle in the Lava Core, heavy stomps, stone cracking when hurt, roars, a rumble before eruptions and lobs, the slam, the gallop, the hiss as it cools, the statue crumbling, and the minions' own; every one has a subtitle.
     - **Animations** for the moves (`tools/golems/kit/moves.py`): swipe, throw (the `prop_rock`/`prop_magma` rock in its fist), slam, charge_start and charge (the gallop), lob (and the volcano's `volcano_lob` spurt), summon; the minions' idle, walk, attack and spawn. All pass `clipping.py` (the shell form also with every loose plate pushed out). Previews: `boss-previews/cinder_wastes/cinder_colossus/01_saddleback_fight.png` (the Cracking look and every move), `minions.png`.
     - **Headless tests:** `boss/golem/ColossusGameTest` (`.\gradlew.bat runGameTestServer`): stage thresholds and clamping, invulnerable while changing stage, the statue staying, minions crumbling, a lava pool's size and cleanup (never replacing a chest or stone), the fire trail going out, a thrown rock placing no blocks, an arrow's hit taking 50 / 35 / 15% of its damage in each stage while a melee hit takes all of it (`rangedHitsAreWeakerEachStage`).
     - **Trophies** [built, not yet tested in-game] (`boss/trophy/`): `dross:colossus_trophy`, a 1x1x1 decorative block, a miniature of the boss: its real model and textures scaled down to fit the block (about 0.15 to 0.18 of its size), no base or pedestal. Five designs (a `design` block state, 1-5; names are lang keys `block.dross.colossus_trophy.design_<n>`, working names): **1 Volcano's Wrath** (the lob as the lava leaves the craters, erupted look, the lob's spurts showing), **2 Molten Charge** (mid-gallop on all fours, head first, lava core), **3 The Roar** (reared up roaring, both fists raised high: the slam's warning, erupted), **4 Earthshaker** (the slam as its fists hit, the Cracking look: plates loose, bright cracks), **5 Dormant Shell** (crouched in its shell, head bowed: the death's last pose, dormant and dim). **The user liked all five, so instead of picking one, every trophy can show any of them** (decided: no single final design is needed): **right-click a placed trophy to change its pose**, 1 > 2 > 3 > 4 > 5 > 1 (below). The showcase (`/dross showcase trophies colossus`, world-builder's `command/TrophyShowcase`, through `ColossusTrophies.DESIGNS`) still places all five.
       - How they're made: `tools/golems/kit/trophies.py` (run by `build.py`, or `build.py --trophies-only`) samples each move at its moment as a held GeckoLib animation (`animations/block/colossus_trophy.animation.json`, `animation.colossus_trophy.design_<n>`; hidden bones scaled to 0, the root moved so the figure stands centred on the block floor), writes two copies of the boss's model (`geo/block/colossus_trophy.geo.json` for the placed trophy, `geo/item/colossus_trophy.geo.json` for the item: GeckoLib shares a model's bones between everything drawn with it, so the boss, the placed trophies and the items each get their own), paints each design's lava streams over the figure in its own pose (`textures/block/colossus_trophy/design_<n>_flow.png` hot and `_flow_base.png` cool; alpha = how far down the stream), renders `boss-previews/cinder_wastes/cinder_colossus/trophies.png` and writes `boss/trophy/TrophyModels.java` (generated: scale, outline box, drip spots, craters, glow and crack strength). They're all outside the `cinder_colossus` folders, so `remove_stale_files` never deletes them.
       - The look (`boss/trophy/client/`: `TrophyBlockRenderer`, `TrophyItemRenderer`, `TrophyLayer`): the boss's texture, then its `_glow` texture full-bright at a subtle strength (dormant 0.4, erupted and cracking 0.75, lava core 0.85), the `_cracks` texture for design 4, and the **flowing lava**: the streams drawn with the dissolve shader (like the boss's poured lava): the cool streams, then two bright bands that slide down them from the craters and loop (`FLOW_PERIOD_SECONDS` 7, `FLOW_BAND`, `FLOW_BANDS` in `ColossusTrophies`). Now and then lava drips off it (`DRIPPING_LAVA` particles from the generated drip spots) and its craters smoke or pop (`Block.animateTick`, client only, just particles: nothing catches fire). It gives off light (`TrophyStyle`: dormant 3, erupted/cracking 6, core 8). The item (`ColossusTrophyItem`, a `GeoItem`) shows the same 3D figure in the hand, the inventory and on the ground (`models/item/colossus_trophy.json`, `builtin/entity`); its tooltip names the design ("No. 1: Volcano's Wrath").
       - **Changing the pose** (`ColossusTrophyBlock.use`): right-clicking a placed trophy (main hand, empty or holding anything) turns it to the next design (`ColossusTrophies.next`: 1 > 2 > 3 > 4 > 5 > 1), keeping its `FACING`; the block state carries everything, so the figure, look, lava streams, outline and light change at once. A quiet click (stone button pitched down) and a soft basalt grind, and a small puff of ember dust (`DrossColors.TROPHY_CYCLE_DUST`) and smoke (numbers: `CYCLE_*` in `ColossusTrophies`). The hand swings and nothing else happens (holding a block, it isn't placed); sneaking with an item in hand skips it like vanilla (so sneak to place a block against it), sneaking with empty hands turns it too. Not in adventure mode (`Player.mayBuild`), so map makers' displays stay put. The item's tooltip adds a gray hint, "Right-click to change its pose" (`block.dross.colossus_trophy.hint`). Checked headlessly (`ColossusGameTest.rightClickCyclesTrophyDesign`).
       - The block: placed on the floor (it needs a block under it; it pops off as an item if that goes), facing the player who placed it (`FACING`); its outline fits the figure. Any tool or a bare hand breaks it and it drops itself with the design it shows (loot table `blocks/colossus_trophy`, `copy_state`; pick-block gives the same design). Never burns; explosions can't break it (blast resistance 1200). The item is fireproof and never despawns (like the Rift Key).
       - **The drop:** in `CinderColossus.die`, with "Set in Stone" and for the same players (`victors`: the player credited with the kill, wherever they are, and every non-spectator within `BOSS_BAR_RANGE`, 40 blocks, each once), every one gets their own trophy of `ColossusTuning.DROP_DESIGN` (**1**; any is fine, since players can turn it to any pose), **straight into their inventory** with the item pickup sound (`ColossusTrophies.giveTo`). If it doesn't fit (inventory full), it's dropped **1 block in front of them** (the way they face, at their feet's height; at their feet if a block is there or in the way: `dropInFront`); a player who's dead (waiting to respawn) gets it dropped where they died (`dropFor`). A dropped one is kept for them: only they can pick it up (`setTarget`), invulnerable (fire, lava, explosions, cactus), never despawns, 1 s pickup delay. Never for `/kill` or a showcase golem (note: the Admin Sword's one-hit kill falls back to `/kill` when the Colossus survives the hit, which it always does in the Shell and Cracking stages, so it gives no trophy there). Checked headlessly (`ColossusGameTest.trophyForEveryPlayerInRange`: into the inventory, nothing dropped; `trophyDropsInFrontWhenInventoryFull`: full inventory, facing a block, dead; `noTrophyForKillCommand`).
       - API for the showcase (`boss/trophy/ColossusTrophies`): `DESIGNS` (a `List<Design>`, design 1 first), `count()`, `get(int number)`, `Design.number()`, `Design.displayName()` (a translatable `Component`), `Design.nameKey()`, `Design.style()`, `Design.blockState(Direction facing)` (the figure faces `facing`), `Design.stack()`, `stack(int)`, `designOf(ItemStack)`, `giveTo(ServerPlayer, int)` (the boss's reward), `dropInFront(ServerPlayer, int)`, `dropFor(ServerPlayer, int)`.
     - **Spawn eggs** for its minions [built]: "Stone Golem Spawn Egg" and "Lava Golem Spawn Egg" (Dross creative tab; colours in `DrossColors`, "Boss"). An egg minion is wild: it doesn't crumble, despawns when you walk away, and an egg lava golem waits for you (it lights its fuse only within 2.5 blocks).
     - **The lairs** [built] (`boss/lair/`; numbers in `LairTuning`): **one in every group of 3 Cinder Wastes patches, out forever** (the boss-site planner, step 12; the user's rule, October 16: "of the nearest 3, one has a Colossus, then the next nearest 3, one of those, etc."). The volcano's own fit test (`LairFinder`, plugged in by `LairSetup` with `BossSites.setFinder(COLOSSUS_LAIR, ...)`: Cinder Wastes high ground where the rim can rise its full 20 blocks with 4 to spare, a walkable way in) picks each group's patch. A lair is built **the first time a player comes within 384 blocks** (`BUILD_RANGE`): every 40 ticks the Dross players' positions go to a background thread that asks the planner for lair sites near them; an unbuilt one is queued (one at a time), its chunks load with tickets while the heights are worked out in the background, then it's planned from the real ground and built a piece at a time (a row of the cone, a rivulet...), at most about 40 ms of work each tick (`LairTuning.STEP_MILLIS`, like the hub's building); the lava's work (placing it, walling it in, clearing what burns near it) always gets one tick of its own, so lava is never left half walled in. A typical build is about 130-500 ms of work over 4-12 ticks; the log gives each kind of work's time and the longest tick (about 40-55 ms; before October 18 a build came in 8 big steps and its longest tick took about 70-515 ms).
