Building a piece at a time gives exactly the same blocks as all at once (`SteppedLairGameTest`). A half-built lair is finished when the server stops, and nobody is sent to one before it's done. Saved per lair by its patch (`ColossusLairData`, `data/dross_colossus_lairs.dat`), so a patch never gets two. (Until October 16 there was one lair per world: the furthest of the nearest Cinder Wastes high ground in three directions from the hub.) Each lair is a **volcano** (about 40 blocks above the arena floor): a jagged crater rim with smoke and embers, 4 contained lava rivulets and pools at its foot (where a rivulet cuts under or beside a natural lava pool on the flank, the space it opened next to that pool is filled with rock again, so the pool can't pour out either: a leak the headless check found on some seeds, fixed October 18), a 9 x 8 cave mouth with obsidian teeth narrowing to a 5 x 6 passage; the way in from the land never steps more than 1 block (planned as a staircase in `LairShape.approach`; on steep land a rock ramp runs out to meet the land, and only as a last resort the mouth faces another side); inside a 41-wide obsidian floor with magma, lava under the floor behind sealed orange-glass fissures, about 30 obsidian stalactites, no reachable lava. The Colossus **rises when a player steps onto the floor** (not on Peaceful); **once beaten (any death, `/kill` too) it never comes back** and that lair stays empty (the user: "do not include respawn feature"; a boss removed without dying, on Peaceful, isn't beaten). No natural spawns inside. `/dross goto lair colossus` goes to the nearest lair's entrance (it says it's looking, builds the lair first if needed and teleports once it's ready, never onto a half-built volcano; gives up after 5 minutes). `/dross goto biome` never lands where a lair stands or is planned. Previews: `boss-previews/cinder_wastes/cinder_colossus/lair_outside.png`, `lair_cutaway.png`.
     - Not built yet (later): the golem floor of the Fire Necromancer's tower (Parked), other loot (a Cinder Heart for the Magma Wand, Parked?), the user's "boss revive" feature (lairs have no respawn now), and boss progression with Quest.
   - **The fight: the user's notes** (a record of what was asked for):
     - First notes: deep, rumbling, grinding stone sounds. Stage 1 (dormant shell): it throws cobblestone (thrown cobblestone **never places blocks**) and slams the ground; the volcano on its back is for **long-range attacks**. Halfway through stage 1 the volcano **erupts** and lava pours over its body. Stage 2 (lava core), when the shell breaks: faster, angrier and more dangerous; **every hit sets you on fire**, a **temporary fire trail**, the bigger volcano **erupts all the time**. **On death it cools into an obsidian statue.**
     - The request it was built from (kept as a record): a spawn egg in the Dross creative tab and `/dross boss summon colossus`; `/dross boss arena colossus` builds a placeholder arena around you (a flat circular floor about 30 blocks wide, of red nether bricks and similar blocks). A custom-drawn boss bar (vanilla bars have no orange), shown only to players within about 40 blocks and hidden when they leave: Shell obsidian purple, Cracking obsidian purple with glowing orange lines, Lava Core lava red. Three phases (thresholds as labeled constants): **Shell** (100% to 60%, slow and heavy: thrown cobblestone chunks that never place blocks; a ground slam with little or no damage that knocks players far away; a charge of about 10 blocks in a straight line, gorilla-style on all fours, head first); **Cracking** (60% to 35%: the shell visibly breaking with lava showing through, a bit faster and more aggressive, the Shell attacks plus a volcano attack of 2 lava blobs landing near the player, not on them, each making a roughly circular temporary lava pool at most 3 blocks out that never spreads, disappears after about 30-50 seconds and never destroys chests or other important blocks, and 3 small stone golems (about 1 block tall) summoned in front, left and right; at 35% the shell-breaking animation plays and it can't be hurt during it); **Lava Core** (35% to 0%: faster, angrier, more dangerous; every hit sets you on fire; a fire trail behind its charge lasting about 4-5 seconds; lava golems with 5-10 health that set you on fire and explode after a few seconds if not killed). On death the cooling animation into an obsidian statue; no drops for now; about 300 health; deep rumbling, grinding stone sounds. A purple challenge advancement for defeating it, to the player who kills it and every player within the boss bar's range when it dies. All tuning numbers as labeled constants.

9. **The Rat King** (Boss area, `com.reg21meme.dross.boss.ratking`) [design picked and built: **Gnawrick, build 1C**; his fight built and tested in-game by the user (October 2026)]: one of the **Plague Necromancer's** three servants. A giant rat standing upright, lean and sly, about 1.5 blocks wide (with his cape and staff), about 1 block deep (plus his tail) and 3 to 4 blocks tall, holding a staff in one hand, wearing a crown and a red cape. The user asked for **only the looks** first (October 14, 2026), then **original sounds** (below). Made by the Python generator in `tools/ratking/` (its README; the general process is `BOSS_GUIDE.md`), previews in `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/` (the rats in `rats/`, the sounds in `sounds/`, round 1 in `round1/` with its in-game screenshots).
   - **Round 1** (six designs, archived in `tools/ratking/ratdesigns/round1/` and `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/round1/`; the user's notes: fur leaning grey and grey-green; the Plague Necromancer's colours, poison green, decaying red and bone beige, never the Dross blue): 1 **Gnawrick** (sewer grey-green, a small three-point crown cocked to one side, a ragged blood-red cape, a twisted staff with a bony claw clutching a glowing green orb; the sly hunch, drumming fingers, tiptoe creep), 2 Lord Squeakley (pompous royal, monocle), 3 Pied Usurper (piebald, the Pied Piper's pipe), 4 Pale Prophet (albino plague priest, candle crown, censer), 5 Old King Knot (ancient elder, a cane topped with a knot of tails), 6 Tin-Can Tyrant (scrappy brute, eyepatch, a giant fork). **The user picked No. 1, Gnawrick, as the main design** and asked for round 2: his variations with different widths and depths (same height); the curled-up sleep (on the ground, then the staff pick-up as he gets up: "I like the staff moves when he gets up"); his ears never through the staff, and in the sleep the staff never through him or the floor; the burrow's dirt mound where his head goes in, not where he stands (he goes in at an angle).
   - **Round 2: Gnawrick in seven builds** (archived in `tools/ratking/ratdesigns/round2/` and `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/round2/`; his look and posture, so the same height; signs said "No. 1A" ... "No. 1G", "Gnawrick", the build): **1A** the original, **1B** skinny (narrow and thin all over), **1C** broad shoulders (a wide chest and shoulders, thicker arms), **1D** barrel chest (a deep, round chest and belly), **1E** potbelly (narrow shoulders over a big round belly), **1F** hulking (wide and deep all over, thick arms and legs), **1G** hunchback (a narrow body with a hunched upper back under his cape). Every build: sleeps curled up on his side, the cape on the ground, the staff lying beside him where it clears him and the floor (searched for each build, `staff_bed`); a staff guard (`tools/ratking/ratkit/guard.py`) tilts or slides the staff in his fist, frame by frame, just enough to keep it out of his ears, head, crown, body and cape and above the floor; a clipping check (`ratkit/clip.py`) runs on every animation of every build.
   - **The pick: 1C, broad shoulders** [built] (`tools/ratking/ratdesigns/gnawrick.py`, the only design built; id `gnawrick`, sign "No. 1C"; previews `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/ratking_1c_gnawrick_*`). The user's notes on round 2, built in: (1) the rage's free (left) arm used to swing through his head: now his fist goes up out to his left (fingers curled on their own bone, `fingers_l`) and pumps in the air with every stomp, then shakes as he screeches; (2) the staff smashes the ground: he lifts it beside him and slams its foot down just in front of him (dust and green sparks there, `DrossColors.RAT_KING_SLAM_SPARK`, at the generated `slamForward`/`slamRight`); (3) his fist holds the staff the way a hand grips a stick: `rig.grip_across` turns the hand so the shaft runs across the knuckles; (4) the death's cartoon X's only on the front of his face; (5) the staff's head all green (the claw round the orb was bone white). The user also picked **train whistle 1** for the rage.
   - **Animations** (every design, `animation.rat_king.*`): **sleep** (curled up; eyelids shut, slow breathing, the mod floats Zzz up from his head: particle `dross:zzz`), **wake** (startles, looks round, stretches and yawns, squats to pick his staff up off the ground, stands), **idle** (breathing, shifty glances, a sniff, a blink, drumming fingers), **walk** (on tiptoe, on the spot in the showcase), **burrow** (glances round, crouches low over a spot about a block in front of him and claws at it with his free hand, his staff planted beside it, dirt flying; hops up and over head first and slides down the hole at a steep angle, his whole body through it; the dirt mound stays: `burrowed`), **popup** (a hole opens under him, his nose and ears poke up out of it, he springs straight up out of it, his staff following him and his tail trailing out of the hole, and lands in place, his crown bouncing; the user saw the first version, which came up 14 units ahead and hopped back, as "going backwards"), **rage** (trembling fists, a stomping tantrum, eyes turn glowing red, steam blows out of his ears in puffs while a train whistle blows, a screech, a staff slam; he stays red-eyed until he sleeps), **death** (clutches his chest, wobbles, stiffens and falls flat on his back like a plank, staff clattering down, crown landing on his belly, tongue out, cartoon X eyes popping, legs stuck up in the air, a foot twitching; `dead` holds it). The eyelids, X eyes, tongue and dirt hole are built hidden inside the head or under the ground and slid out by the animations; the dirt hole is sunk deeper than its tallest clod and is only drawn while he burrows, is under the ground or pops up (`RatKingRenderer`), so a knockback never shows it (the user saw it under him); the burrow's hole (`parts.HOLE_FORWARD`, 14 units in front of his feet, where his head goes in) is sized to his belly, the pop-up's sits under him (`popupHoleForward`); the timings the particles and sounds need are in the generated `RatKingDesigns`.
   - **The rats** (`dross:rat`, about 0.65 blocks long; `dross:enraged_rat`, a **separate, scarier breed** (the user's answer), about 1.4 times bigger with glowing red eyes, bristles and fangs), idle and run each (`animation.rat.*`): regular R1 Sewer Rat, R2 Black Rat, **R3 Plague Rat**, R4 Hooded Rat; enraged E1 Bristleback, E2 War Rat, **E3 Plague Ravager**, E4 Ghoul Rat. **The user picked the Plague Rat and the Plague Ravager** (round 1) and asked for their green spots on both sides: the Plague Rat's pustules are now three mirrored pairs on its flanks and two down its back; the Ravager's glowing boils are mirrored pairs on its cheeks, shoulders and haunches and a pair beside its spine, its drool in the middle of its jaw. The other six were dropped (archived in `tools/ratking/ratdesigns/round1/rats_round1.py`, previews `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/round1/rats/`): each kind has one design now. **Spawn eggs** [built]: "Plague Rat Spawn Egg" and "Plague Ravager Spawn Egg" (Dross creative tab, spawn eggs group; colours in `DrossColors`, Boss): a rat that isn't a showcase rat wears the pick of its kind (`Rat.DEFAULT_DESIGN`), wanders about (running when it moves) and squeaks; a wild Plague Rat never attacks (it flees when hurt), a wild Plague Ravager is hostile (see the fight). **The Rat King's spawn egg** [built] ("Rat King Spawn Egg", before the rats' in the spawn eggs group; colours in `DrossColors`): an egg king is a fighting king, asleep, like the summoned one.
   - **Sounds** [built] (the user's request: original, not from Minecraft; a train whistle when he gets mad; several versions of the attack sounds to pick from later): all 77 made from scratch by `tools/ratking/sounds.py` (`ratkit/synth.py`: voices with vowels, squeaks, a chime train whistle, knocks, thuds, whooshes, reverb), in `assets/dross/sounds/rat_king/`, `rat/`, `enraged_rat/`. The king: sly snickers, sniffs, muttering and a cackle (ambient, 4), hurt (3), the death's long squeal, a startled "eep"/"huh?!", a yawn, snores with a little whistle (every sleep loop), the rage's stomps, **three train whistles** (1 "choo-choo-chooo" timed with his steam puffs, 2 one long wailing blast, 3 a shrill toy train; `RAGE_WHISTLE` in `sounds.py` picks one: **the user picked 1**), screech and staff slam, footsteps, digging, the dive, the ground rumbling, the pop, picking up his staff, the thud (with a silly boing), the staff's clatter and the crown's clink. The mod plays them at the animations' moments (`RatKing.playActionSounds`, even for the silent showcase kings). **Attack versions for the fight** (not used yet): claw swipe (3), bite (3), staff strike (3), plague orb (3), summoning his rats (3), sonic screech (2), tail whip (2), plague breath (2). The rats: the Plague Rat's squeaks, hurt, death, tiny footsteps and bites; the Plague Ravager's growls, hissing snarls and gurgles, hurt screeches, death, hisses, footsteps and snarling bites. Listen in a browser: `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/sounds/index.html`; in the game: `/dross showcase boss_sounds ratking` (`command/RatSoundShowcase`) lists them all with [play] buttons.
   - **Showcases** (`command/RatKingShowcase`, `command/RatShowcase`, `command/RatSoundShowcase`): `/dross showcase boss_designs ratking` spawns him frozen with a sign (No. 1C, Gnawrick, broad shoulders); `/dross showcase boss_designs ratking sleep|wake|idle|walk|burrow|popup|rage|charge|die` (also clickable in chat; `charge` plays the spear charge on the spot) plays an animation on every showcase Rat King within 64 blocks; right-click plays one king's next animation, sneak + right-click: he dies. `/dross showcase rats` spawns the two rats on pedestals with signs (R3 Plague Rat, E3 Plague Ravager); `idle|run` switches them; right-click switches one. Showcase entities can't be hurt except by `/kill`, can't be pushed, never despawn.
   - **Code:** `boss/ratking/` (`RatKing`, `RatKingAction`, `Rat`, `RatKingEvents`, the generated `RatKingDesigns`, `RatDesigns` and `RatKingSounds`; `client/`: models, renderers with the glow and rage (red eyes) layers and the marker-bone tracking for the particles, `ZzzParticle`), entity types `dross:rat_king`, `dross:rat`, `dross:enraged_rat`, the sound events (`registry/ModSounds`), the three spawn eggs (`registry/ModItems`), GameTests `RatKingShowcaseGameTest` and `RatKingFightGameTest`.
   - **The fight** [built, tested in-game] (`RatKing`, `RatKingAttackGoal`, `RatKingBurrow`, `PlagueBall`, `PoisonCloud`; every number is a labeled constant in `RatKingTuning`; built from the user's request, October 2026):
     - **Getting one:** the "Rat King Spawn Egg", or `/dross boss summon ratking` (he appears asleep 14 blocks in front of you, facing you, just outside his wake range; closer if there's no room; not on Peaceful). `/dross boss arena ratking` builds a placeholder arena round you (`boss/RatKingArena`): a flat round floor of dirt with about a third coarse dirt in patches, 31 blocks across, the air cleared 15 blocks up (block entities left alone), gaps filled up to 6 blocks down; its chat message has a [Summon the Rat King] button. His home is the rat caves at the end of Blightwood's sewers (step 11): there he's asleep on his nest when you arrive, and once beaten he never comes back.
     - **Stats:** 200 health, armour 6, shrugs off 60% of knockback, speed 0.33 (about 4.7 blocks a second) and 0.38 in the Rage (about 6.2; a walking player is 4.3, a sprinting one 5.6) (the user's request, October 16: "increase his speed and his staff attacks"; the first version was 0.30 / 0.36), steps up 1 block, notices players within 40 blocks, 40 XP, never despawns (except on Peaceful).
     - **Asleep and waking:** he starts curled up asleep (Zzz, snores). A player (not a spectator) within 12 blocks, or 8 while sneaking, wakes him, and so does anything that hurts him (that hit does no damage). He can't be hurt while he wakes up; then his boss bar appears and the fight begins.
     - **Facing** (the user's request, like the Colossus): he steers straight at his target when the way is clear (`boss/BossChase`), turns to it between, during and after his moves and all through the rage, turns to face it before he digs, faces the way he tunnels underground, and comes up facing it. Checked headlessly: 99.8% of a scripted fight (`RatKingFightGameTest.facesHisTargetMostOfTheTime`).
     - **Moves** (`RatKingAttackGoal`): between moves (a pause of 12 ticks, 8 in the Rage) he runs at his target (the `run` scurry, played as fast as he moves).
       - **How he picks a move** (the user's table, October 16: "the poison throw is used too often"; `FIRST_CHANCES`, `RAGE_CHANCES` in `RatKingTuning`; rolled each time he's free to pick; a move on cooldown or that can't be made drops out and the others share its chance; "run in" means he runs at his target and doesn't roll again for 2 s, `RUN_IN_TICKS`): within sweep reach (about 4 blocks) Sweep 100%; 5-16 blocks Charge 60 / Poison 25 / Run in 15 (Rage: 60 / 35 / 5); 16-24 blocks Charge 40 / Poison 30 / Run in 30 (Rage: 35 / 35 / 30); over 24 blocks or out of sight Run in 100%. Between about 4 and 5 blocks he runs in. (Before, he threw poison whenever it was ready at 5-24 blocks.)
       - **Spear charge** (5 to 24 blocks, line of sight and a clear-ish straight path; cooldown 5 s, 3.5 s in the Rage; the user's request): a 0.4 s wind-up (`charge_start`: he crouches with his staff levelled like a spear, turning to face his target); at its end he **locks his target's spot** and dashes in a straight line **to that exact spot** (no overshoot; `charge`: a fast low scurry, the staff held out like a lance) at **9 blocks a second** (11 in the Rage); the first thing in his way (never his rats, never creative players) takes **6** (8 in the Rage) with a small knockback (0.2) and he goes **straight into a staff sweep** if his target is in reach (6 + 9 = 15, Rage 8 + 11 = 19); if he misses he skids to a stop there (`charge_skid`) and can't act for 0.5 s, turning back to his target; a wall stops him (then the same recovery). His body faces the dash (excluded from the facing test, like the Colossus's gallop). Its own original sound (`entity.rat_king.charge`, two versions: a war-cry squeak and a rising squeal over scurrying claws). Previews: `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/ratking_1c_gnawrick_charge.png`.
       - **Staff sweep** (close): the staff swings flat in front of him (`sweep`): everything within 4.5 blocks in a 140-degree arc takes 9 (11 in the Rage) and is knocked back hard (strength 1.0, a sword with Knockback I is 0.9); cooldown 22 ticks (16 in the Rage; 30 / 22 before October 16). After the user's play test (October 15) it reaches 1 block further, hits 1 heart harder and knocks back further than the first version (3.5 blocks, 7 / 9, 0.6).
       - **Poison shot** (5 to 24 blocks, when he can see his target): he holds the staff out and flicks it (`cast`), and a clumpy brownish-green plague ball (`dross:plague_ball`) arcs from the orb, aimed a little ahead of a moving target; in the Rage a **fan of three**, 18 degrees apart; cooldown 70 ticks (50). It bursts into a **poison cloud** (`dross:poison_cloud`, 2.5 blocks round, 3 s, shrinking as it fades) that gives Poison I, topped up only when the poison is nearly out (`CLOUD_TOPUP_BELOW`), so standing in it is a steady Poison I (about 1 damage every 1.25 s; the first version re-hurt you every half second); never the king or his rats.
       - **Burrow and summon:** every 25 health he loses (at 175, 150 and 125, then the Rage at 100; in the Rage counted from the rage: at 75, 50 and 25; a big hit never skips one; every 30 and 20 until October 17) he digs and dives into the ground. Under the ground (80 ticks, 50 in the Rage) he can't be hurt or targeted, and a trail of dirt moves over the ground as he tunnels towards his target (0.35 blocks a tick). Meanwhile his rats burst out of the ground in a ring 3 to 6 blocks round his target, one after another: **10, 12 and 14 Plague Rats** in the first phase's three burrows, **8, 9 and 10 Plague Ravagers** in the Rage's (`RATS_PER_BURROW`, `RAGE_RATS_PER_BURROW`, the user's numbers of October 17; 63 in a whole fight; before: 10 each and 7 each; one every 5 ticks, or a little faster when that many wouldn't all be out before he pops up: the Rage's last 10 come every 4), with **no limit** on how many of his are alive (the user's request after the play test; the first version called 7 and stopped at 14 alive). He pops up 4 blocks from his target, straight up out of a hole, facing it; no knockback or damage.
     - **The Rage** (at 50% of his health, 100 of 200; 40% until October 17; the health stops there and he can't be hurt while it plays): stomps, his fist pumping, red eyes, steam from his ears, train whistle 1, a screech and the staff smash. Then he's faster, hits harder, his moves come back sooner, he throws three balls at once, burrows more often for less time and calls enraged rats; his bar turns red.
     - **His rats:** summoned ones carry a `Master` tag, attack players, never despawn mid-fight and crumble when he dies or is gone; no drops, 1 XP. Plague Rat: 6 health, bites for 2. Plague Ravager: 9 health, bites for 3 and 2.5 s of Poison I (2 s until October 17; wild ones too). Wild ones (spawn eggs, `/summon`): a Plague Rat is harmless (it wanders and flees when hurt), a Plague Ravager is hostile.
     - **Boss bar** (shown to players within 40 blocks once he's awake): drawn by `DrossBossBars` from its generated texture (`textures/gui/rat_king_bar.png`, positions in `boss/client/RatKingBarArt`). Nine designs were made (`tools/ratking/bossbars.py`, each normal and Rage, full/half/low): 1 Crown, 2 Nose & whiskers, 3 Curling tail, 4 Fur edge, 5 Gnawed, 6 Sewer pipe, 7 Claw & orb, 8 Beady eyes, 9 Pipe & eyes. **The user picked 9**: option 6's sewer pipe with option 8's rat peeking over it, browner, no pink paws. Only 9 is kept (preview `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/bossbar-previews/option_9.png`): the other eight, their textures and previews and their showcase (`/dross showcase bossbars ratking`) were removed on October 18, 2026.
     - **Death:** his silly death; his rats crumble; he lies there 10 s, then vanishes in a puff of smoke. 40 XP and a trophy for every player who helped (below); no other drops. **"Down the Drain"** (`dross:down_the_drain`, "End the Rat King's reign beneath Blightwood.", the user's name; Quest's JSON, granted through `DrossAdvancements.grant`) goes to the player credited with the kill and every player within 40 blocks, never for `/kill`.
     - **Sounds** (placeholders until the user picks): the sweep `attack.staff_1`, the cast `attack.orb_1`, the cloud's burst `attack.plague_breath_2`, his call to his rats `attack.summon_1`, the rats' bites; the other attack versions stay in `sounds.json` to pick from.
     - **Headless tests** (`RatKingFightGameTest`): waking by distance (sneaking closer) and by a hit without damage, the rage stopping at 50%, burrows every 25 in both phases with 10 / 12 / 14 and 8 / 9 / 10 rats of the right kind (and none after the Rage's third), the speeds, the move table over 4,000 rolls (every share within 1.5 points), the charge (locks the spot at the end of the wind-up, reaches it at the right speed, 6 + 9 and 8 + 11, the miss's recovery, a wall stopping him, the cooldowns), one ball then three, the cloud's poison (brief, steady, never the rats), facing, the Ravager's poison bite, wild rats, rats crumbling with him, "Down the Drain" (not for `/kill`), the boss bar's range, the arena.
   - **Trophies** [built, tested in-game] (`boss/ratking/trophy/`, copying the Colossus trophies, step 8): `dross:rat_king_trophy`, a 1x1x1 decorative block, a miniature of him (his real model and textures on the trophy's own 256x256 texture, `textures/block/rat_king_trophy/`), no base except a dirt mound under No. 4, his orb and crown gem glowing full-bright, light level 4. Ten candidates were made (1 Fast Asleep, 2 Rude Awakening, 3 Sly Schemer, 4 Staff Sweep, 5 Plague Flick, 6 Tantrum, 7 Ground Pound, 8 Down the Hole, 9 Pop Goes the King, 10 Belly Up; previews kept in `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/trophies/candidates/`); **the user picked candidates 1, 6, 7, 9 and 10**, which are the game's five designs (a `design` block state, in the order of his fight): **1 Fast Asleep** (curled up asleep, staff beside him; little Zzz), **2 Tantrum** (screeching, fist in the air, red eyes; steam puffs), **3 Ground Pound** (the staff smash, red eyes), **4 Pop Goes the King** (bursting out of a dirt mound, arms flung up), **5 Belly Up** (flat on his back, legs up, X eyes, crown askew). Made by `tools/ratking/ratkit/trophies.py` (`build.py --trophies-only`; `--candidates` redraws the ten's record): held GeckoLib animations `animation.rat_king_trophy.design_<n>`, two model copies (`geo/block/`, `geo/item/rat_king_trophy.geo.json`), the generated `RatKingTrophyModels` (scale, outline box, glow, red eyes, light, particle spots), previews `boss-previews/blightwood/rat_king/trophies/trophies.png`.
     - Exactly the Colossus trophy's rules: placed on the floor facing the placer (needs a block under it), any tool or a bare hand breaks it and it drops itself with the design it shows (pick-block too), never burns, blast resistance 1200; the item shows the 3D figure, is fireproof and never despawns, its tooltip "No. N: name" and the gray hint. **Right-click** turns it to the next pose (1 > 2 > 3 > 4 > 5 > 1; main hand, sneak with an item to skip, not in adventure mode) with a wooden click, a rat squeak and a puff of green dust. Now and then: a green glint at the orb, Zzz (1), steam (2), green dust at the staff's strike (3), a dirt crumb at the hole (4).
     - **The drop:** when he dies, with "Down the Drain" and for the same players (the player credited with the kill and every non-spectator within 40 blocks), each gets their own **design 5, Belly Up** (`RatKingTuning.TROPHY_DROP_DESIGN`; they can turn it to any pose) straight into their inventory; a full inventory drops it 1 block in front of them, a dead player gets it where they died; a dropped one is kept for them, can't be destroyed and never despawns. Never for `/kill` or a showcase king. The give/drop logic is shared with the Colossus (`boss/trophy/TrophyRewards`).
     - Dross tab: the five in the trophies group after the Colossus's. `/dross showcase trophies ratking` (`command/TrophyShowcase`) places the five in a row on pedestals with signs; running it again replaces the row. Headless tests: `RatKingTrophyGameTest`.
   - Not built yet: the final attack sounds (the user picks). (Where he lives: the rat caves, step 11.)

10. **The Rage Wand** [built, tested in-game; its teams (October 16) not yet] (Boss area, `boss/rage/`; every number in `RageTuning`): a creative-only test tool (the user's request, October 2026: "a basic item… the rage wand… a creative mode only staff so don't worry about adding a cure").
    - **The item** (`dross:rage_wand`, `RageWandItem`): in the Dross tab after the Admin Sword (weapons and tools), no recipe, stacks to 1, an original texture (a charred shaft, a corded grip, a glowing crystal in its team's colour: five textures `rage_wand_<team>.png` from `tools/ragewand/draw.py`, picked by the item model override `dross:rage_team`), a gray tooltip with its team and a hint. Like a sword it doesn't break blocks in creative.
    - **Teams** (the user's request, October 16; `RageTeam`): **right-click** (any use) changes the wand's team: **Blue → Red → Green → Yellow → Purple → Blue** (saved on the item; a new wand is Blue), with a small click and "Rage Wand: <Team> team" above the hotbar in the team's colour (`DrossColors.RAGE_TEAM_*`).
    - **Hitting a mob** with it never damages or knocks it back; it gives the mob **Rage** (`dross:rage`, `RageEffect` in `registry/ModEffects`: infinite, no cure, milk doesn't remove it, its swirl hidden) **and the wand's team**; hitting it again with another colour switches its team (red, then purple, makes it purple). A short growl and a burst of team-coloured dust, and a puff of it over the mob every second while it lasts (no glowing outline: "not for now"). Players can't be raged. It refuses (a click and a puff of smoke) mobs with no AI (showcase entities), the Ender Dragon, the Dross trader and the hub's survivors (they can never die, so their fight would never end).
    - **Team fights:** a raged mob **never targets, chases or hits its own team** (friendly blows do no damage either). **The team beats natural enmity** (the user, October 17): an iron golem and a zombie, or a pillager and an iron golem or villager, on one team leave each other alone. When a mob joins or switches team (the wand's hit, a boss's minions joining or moving with it, a reload), it and every raged teammate within 48 blocks (`SETTLE_RANGE`) drop each other at once (`Rage.settleTeam`: the target and the target goal that picked it, a neutral mob's anger, a warden's anger, hurt-by and last-hurt, the brain's attack target, anger, hurt-by and walk target, and the chase), so a fight two mobs started before they were teammates (one coloured a few seconds after the other) ends the moment they are; as a safety net every raged mob checks every 10 ticks (`TEAMMATE_CHECK_TICKS`) that it holds nothing against a teammate and that no target goal of its own is stuck on a refused target. (Until October 17 a target held from before was never dropped: a blue golem and a blue zombie kept chasing and swinging at each other, harmlessly, until another team's mob came.) It goes for the nearest raged mob of **another team** within **40 blocks** (`RAGE_RANGE`), the next nearest when that one dies; while it has an enemy-team mob near it ignores players, even one who hits it; with none near it behaves normally towards everything except its own team (players, mobs that aren't raged). **Teams are permanent** (the user: "they stay their team forever: this is a creative-only test item"): the Rage and the team never wear off, and they survive a reload. A Rage given by `/effect` has no team and fights nobody. (Until October 16 it was a free-for-all and the last one standing calmed down after 10 s.)
    - **The user's rule: no new animations**, "all old Minecraft principles apply": every mob fights with its own vanilla attack and walk (zombies swing, skeletons shoot, creepers explode, golems smash; neutral mobs get angry; slimes and magma cubes hurt by touch). Mobs with no attack of their own (cows, pigs, sheep, chickens, villagers, horses, llamas, snow golems, wandering traders; also rabbits, goats and frogs) **headbutt**: 2 damage (1 heart) every second within reach. Brain-driven mobs (piglins, hoglins, zoglins, axolotls; villagers, camels, sniffers, allays) are best effort; **the warden** gets more care (`RageAi.wardenAttack`; the user's bug of October 18: a raged warden ignored a raged Rat King of another team, even when he hit it after his burrow): its brain roars before it fights, its roar only starts while it has no attack target, and it stays "about to roar" while it has a roar target, so a roar target picked just before the Rage set its attack target (the king coming out of the ground) left it standing still for ever; now a roar already going is turned on its enemy and finishes, any other roar target is dropped, and its anger at its enemy is raised again whenever it falls under "angry" (Minecraft drops a target the warden isn't angry at); a roar target on a teammate is dropped like its other grudges; bats and squid can't chase; ghasts and phantoms are untested.
    - **Bosses** (`RageLeader`): raging the Cinder Colossus makes an idle one fight; raging a sleeping Rat King wakes him. They fight raged mobs with their own moves (their blows, rocks, lava, plague balls and clouds hit raged enemies). Their minions already alive, and every golem or rat they summon while raged, **join their team** (re-raging a boss moves its minions to the new team; a minion hit separately with another colour joins that team instead): they fight the other teams and never their master or their own team.
    - **Headless tests** (`RageGameTest`): cycling the team by use; a mob taking the wand's team and switching on a re-hit; two teams fighting, never their own team (teammate blows do no damage); a blue iron golem and a blue zombie calm side by side for 10 s (no target, anger or hurt-by against each other, no damage); the golem turning blue and fighting the zombie, then the zombie turning blue: both let go within 10 ticks and stay calm, and a hurt-by slipped in behind the Rage's back is dropped within 10 ticks (the safety net); a red iron golem coming: both blues go for it, and once it's gone they stay calm with each other for 5 s; a blue pillager and a blue iron golem calm for 10 s; the last team standing keeping its Rage and team; raged zombies ignore a player; the wand does no damage and its Rage is infinite; milk doesn't cure it; a raged pig headbutts a raged cow for 2; a raged villager headbutts; a raged Colossus's golems and a raged Rat King's rats join their teams; the Rage and the team survive a save and load; a raged warden whose brain has just picked its enemy to roar at still fights and hits it (`ragedWardenFightsAfterItsRoar`), and a raged warden hits a raged Rat King of another team again after his burrow (`ragedWardenFightsTheRatKingAfterHisBurrow`).
11. **The sewers under Blightwood and the rat cave** [built, not yet tested in-game] (Dimension area, `dimension/sewer/`; the Rat King's part is the Boss area's, `boss/ratking/lair/`; the user's requests, October 2026): the Dross's first underground areas, kept to the sewers. New worlds only (the mod isn't published, so no old-world support).
    - **Where they go** (the boss-site planner, step 12): Blightwood's patches are taken nearest the hub first, in groups of 3; in every group **one patch gets the Rat King's sewer** (the network with his rat cave), and **each of the other two gets a regular sewer with a 1 in 2 chance** (`REGULAR_SEWER_CHANCE` 0.5, a seeded roll), if one fits; never more than one network in a patch. Both are structures placed by the planner (`dross:boss_site` placement, `structure_set/sewers.json` and `rat_cave.json`), so `/locate` can't find them: use `/dross goto sewer`, `/dross goto lair ratking` or the API. A maze shrinks to fit its patch (`MAZE_FILL` 0.8 of the room, at least `NODES_SMALLEST` 16 junctions; a group's roomiest patch gets the smallest form if nothing else fits). `SewerPlanner` seeds its randomness from the world seed and the chunk only, so the planner's fit test and the generated network are identical.
    - **The maze** (`SewerPlanner`): junctions on a 10-block grid, grown from the middle hall (or the rat cave's doors) by carrying on from the newest junction or branching off an older one, then extra loops (at least 2): long winding corridors, side branches and dead ends; junctions are plain crossings, pillared brick rooms with drains, or rough natural caves (tuff, deepslate, mud, stalactites). Main tunnels (5 wide, arched, a sewer-water channel down the middle, a thin copper pipe on the wall) run from each entrance to the hall or the cave; side tunnels are 3 x 4, dry, with cobwebs; iron grates; stone and deepslate brick floors with Sewer Sludge patches. At least 16 blocks of rock over every tunnel, all below y 58.
    - **A regular sewer**: rats only, no king, **3 pipe entrances** (the user's count; `PIPES_MIN`/`PIPES_MAX`, `SewerPipePiece`): Mario-style pipes of waxed oxidised copper (green, streaked and banded) standing 4 blocks out of the ground, a 7-wide rim round a 5-wide body and a 3 x 3 opening, a stone collar and stone-brick steps up to the rim; you drop down the copper shaft (34-58 blocks measured) into a 5 x 5 pool 4 deep that breaks the fall, and a ladder runs up the inside wall (about 15-25 s to climb). **Glow lichen** here and there on its walls (`LICHEN_TUNNELS` 0.035).
    - **The Rat King's sewer** (the user picked all four of these looks): **one grand entrance** (`SewerGrandPipePiece`): a huge copper pipe, its 11-wide rim shaped like his crown (bright copper points, tall and short in turn, each tipped with a glowing Blight Vent like his crown's green gem), bands of bright copper (**no gold blocks**: the user's request after playing it, October 16), 2 tattered red banners (his cape's colour), 5-wide steps; a 5 x 5 drop into a 7 x 7 pool 5 deep, a ladder out. **Poisoned ground** within about 16 blocks (wobbly edge): coarse dirt, sludge puddles (never fewer than 8 sludge blocks, `POISON_SLUDGE_MIN`), dead bushes, 5 rat nests (bone heaps with carpets), cobwebs, gnawed fences; nothing grows over it (`dross:not_near_sewer_entrance` keeps Blightwood's ponds, logs, giant mushrooms, Gloomcaps and Sporepods 26 blocks from it and 14 from a regular pipe). **A green glow and spores**: the new **Blight Vent** block (`dross:blight_vent`, light 12, breathes poison-green spores; original texture; end of the Dross tab's blocks group): on the crown's tips, 4 in the shaft wall under the rim, 4 in the pool room, 3 in the poisoned ground. **The royal road**: the main tunnel from the entrance to his cave (about 6 junctions): a deepslate floor, red banners and lit green candles on both walls, bone piles, raw gold and gold coins (light weighted pressure plates). No rough caves or water channel on it. **No carpet** (the user's request after playing it, October 16: a red runner from the entrance to his nest showed the way through the maze).
    - **Green sewer water**: ordinary water in its own underground biome, **`dross:sewers`** ("The Sewers": water `DrossColors.SEWERS_WATER` 0x56602A, murky green, never vanilla blue; thick green fog, drips and plinks; Blightwood's features and ores), from 10 blocks under Blightwood's ground and only below y 64, through the multi-noise `depth` slot driven by `dross:climate/sewer_depth`. The surface biome map is unchanged (any lookup at y 64 or above returns the surface biome).
    - **Sewer Sludge** (`dross:sewer_sludge`): a murky brownish-green mud (original texture, randomly rotated; mud and slime sounds pitched down), a full block you sink 2 pixels into; it slows walkers to about 0.65 of their speed (soul sand 0.4); a shovel mines it; in the Dross tab's blocks group (then the Blight Vent); preview `block-previews/sewer_sludge.png`.
    - **The rat cave** (`RatCavePiece`, only in the Rat King's sewer): the final chamber all its main tunnels lead to, about 40 across and 14 tall under a rough dome; copper pipes along its wall and ceiling pour water into floor drains (the floor never floods), one oozes sludge; Sewer Sludge patches over about a quarter of its muddy floor; 24 stalactites, 5 Gloomcaps; brick paths from each door, the arrival pad at a side door (off the royal road). In the middle **his nest** (carpets in sewer colours on a heap of wool, a 3 x 3 top) and **his hoard** (about 80 blocks: about 70% `dross:duskstone_gold_ore`, 30% gold blocks). **Lit** (the user's pick): lit green candles round the nest (10 spots) and on 5 gold heaps, and glow lichen on the walls (`LICHEN_RAT_CAVE` 0.05).
    - **Light levels**: glow lichen 7, a lit candle 3 per candle (3-12), Gloomcap 6, Blight Vent 12 (only at the grand entrance). Rats may spawn in this dim light inside the sewers (the Boss area's spawn rule, below).
    - **Spawning** (the user's shares, labeled in `worldgen.py`, SEWERS section; checked by `DrossSpawnsGameTest`): Blightwood's surface: **no zombies, skeletons or endermen** (the user, October 17, like the Cinder Wastes; more Blightwood mobs come later), only rats and **witches**, the witches **7%** of the mobs by count (in place of the endermen), the rats the rest, **1 in 5** of them enraged (Plague Rat 124 in groups of 2-4, Ravager 62 in groups of 1-2, witch 35 alone: 74 / 19 / 7%; its animals and bats stay the old Dross lists; before: the old list plus rats, a third of the mobs). So Blightwood no longer drops Deathforged books or gives Necromancy souls (only zombies and skeletons do); the sewers biome: **only rats**, **3 in 5 enraged**; the rat cave (`RatCaveSpawns`): only rats, 3 in 5 enraged.
    - **The API**: `Sewers.findNearest(dross, from[, radius])` → `Network(id, startChunk, ratKing, bounds, floorY, entrances)`, each `Entrance(opening, approach (ground at the foot of its steps), approachYaw, groundY, grand)`; `RatCaves.findNearest(dross, from[, radius in blocks, SEARCH_RADIUS 12,000])` (through the planner), `caveAt`, `isInRatCave`, `of`; `RatCave(id, startChunk, bounds, centre, nest, arrival, arrivalYaw, floorY, piece, entrance())`; `RatCaves.entrancesNear`, `SewerSites.entrancesNear` / `nearEntrance`, `Sewers.isInSewers`, `DrossBiomes.SEWERS` (not in `/dross goto biome`).
    - **The Rat King's part** (Boss area, `boss/ratking/lair/`: `RatKingLair` (`isBeaten`), `RatKingLairKeeper`, `RatKingLairData`, `RatKingSnores`; numbers in `RatKingTuning`, rat cave section):
      - **His home:** when a player (not a spectator) comes within 48 blocks of a cave's nest (`RAT_CAVE_KING_RANGE`) and the cave's king hasn't been beaten (not on Peaceful), a fighting Rat King appears asleep on the nest (feet on the top carpet), facing the arrival tunnel, once the cave's chunks have their entities loaded; he wakes as in the fight (12 blocks, 8 sneaking, or a hit), with the same boss bar, "Down the Drain" and trophy. One king per cave; he never pops into view (not while a player who was already inside at the last check is still there). **Once beaten (any death, `/kill` too) he never comes back** (the user: "do not include respawn feature"); removed without dying (Peaceful) isn't a defeat.
      - **His snores at the grand entrance:** while his cave's king is asleep (or hasn't appeared yet and isn't beaten), players within 16 blocks of the crowned pipe hear his snore rising out of it every 5-11 s, quieter (0.4) and a little deeper (pitch about 0.85); awake or beaten, the pipe is quiet.
      - **Rats in dim sewer light** (`RatKingEvents.checkRatSpawnRules`): inside the sewers (the `dross:sewers` biome, or a sewer piece the sky can't see) both rats may spawn at block light up to 7 (`RAT_SEWER_SPAWN_MAX_BLOCK_LIGHT`), so the glow lichen and candles don't stop them; elsewhere (and on the poisoned ground and pipe tops) darkness only, as before; never on Peaceful.
      - **`/dross goto sewer`** (`command/GotoSewer`): the nearest regular sewer's first pipe, at the foot of its steps facing it, on safe ground.
      - **Stronger cave rats:** a Plague Ravager that spawns naturally inside a rat cave gets 1.5x health (13.5) and 1.5x bite damage (4.5) (saved modifiers). His summoned rats, egg rats, Plague Rats and rats elsewhere stay normal.
      - **`/dross goto lair ratking`**: the nearest rat cave (from you in the Dross, from Dross 0,0 elsewhere), onto its arrival spot facing the nest, with a chat note: asleep, awake or beaten.
      - **Headless tests:** `RatKingLairGameTest` (`kingAppearsOnceAndNeverReturns`, `caveRavagersAreStronger`, `ratSpawnRules`: light 7 in the cave yes, 8 no, 7 in the open no; `snoresAtTheGrandEntrance`), `GotoLairGameTest` (`gotoLair`, `gotoRatKingLair`, `gotoSewer`), and for the Colossus `ColossusLairGameTest` (`fitTestForFixedSeeds`, `lairsAreBuiltWhenAPlayerComesNear`, `colossusRisesOnceAndNeverAgain`).
    - **Previews**: `terrain-previews/sewers/` (`network_map.png` (a regular sewer's 3 pipes), `rat_cave_network_map.png` (grand entrance, royal road, cave), `pipe.png`, `grand_entrance.png`, `grand_entrance_night.png`, `grand_entrance_cut.png`, `royal_road.png`, `rat_cave_top.png` (lit by its candles and lichen), `rat_cave_section.png`); regenerate with `$env:DROSS_SEWER_PREVIEWS="1"` on a GameTest run, then `python tools/terrain/preview_sewers.py`.
    - **Headless tests** (`dimension/SewersGameTest`): a regular sewer found through the planner with its 3 pipes (open to the sky, a ladder, a deep pool of green water), lichen, tunnels connecting everything, dead ends and loops; the Rat King's sewer with exactly 1 grand entrance (green crown tips, a copper band, no gold blocks, banners, vents, light in the shaft, pool, ladder), its poisoned ground (no trees or mushrooms within 16) and its royal road with no carpet (banners, candles, bones, gold); the cave's size, nest, hoard (70% ore, within 12 points or 4 standard deviations of its blocks' chance spread, whichever is wider: each hoard block is its own 70% roll), candles, lichen, sludge, pipes and waterfalls, a floor that never floods, a safe arrival; sludge slowing a walker to about 67% (soul sand 54%); every entrance of every network in Blightwood's first 3 groups whole (`everySewerEntranceIsWhole`: each ladder rung from the water to the rim present, facing the shaft and held by its wall); the tidy-up leaving a half-built pipe alone (`tidyUpLeavesAHalfBuiltPipeAlone`: the world's tidy-up step, `TidyUpFeature`, never removes ladders, banners or candles, which only structures place; it once took the top rungs off a pipe built across a chunk edge before the next chunk existed).
    - **The user's requests** (kept as a record): sewer entrances are big vertical pipes sticking up out of the ground, like Mario pipes, made of copper, stone and similar blocks; you jump down and land in a pool of murky green (not blue) sewer water that breaks your fall; the pipes lead into tangled, maze-like tunnels and caves, the first underground areas in the Dross, kept to the sewers; Sewer Sludge covers sewer floors in patches and slows you less than soul sand. A rat cave is the final chamber where all the pipes lead, bigger than the tunnels, with pipes along its walls and ceiling emptying into it; it's the Rat King's battlefield, with sludge patches spread round the fight area; in the middle he sleeps on a nest of piled carpets surrounded by heaps of gold (about 70% Dross gold ore, 30% gold blocks), asleep when you arrive and waking as you approach. Spawning: rats about a third of Blightwood's surface mobs, 1 in 5 of them enraged; only rats in the sewers, 3 in 5 enraged; only rats in the rat cave, its enraged rats stronger. All rates as labeled constants. `/dross goto lair ratking` (the user picked this over `goto ratcave`, following rule 9). Then (October 16): the rat cave and its sewer are placed like bosses (one per group of 3 Blightwood patches, step 12); "the regular sewer: 3 normal copper pipes, a 1/2 calc for each of the other two patches"; the Rat King's sewer must look different: one grand entrance, the crowned pipe, poisoned ground, a green glow and spores, a royal road; the cave lit with green candles round the nest and glow lichen on the walls; regular sewers get glow lichen on their walls; no respawn ("do not include respawn feature"). After playing it (October 16): no gold blocks on the entrance ("pick something else") and no carpet leading straight to his base.

12. **Boss sites: one boss per patch, in groups of three, out forever** [built, not yet tested in-game] (Dimension area, `dimension/sites/`: `BossSites`, `BossSitesTuning`, `Patch`, `Group`, `Site`, `SiteUse`, `SiteFinder`, `SiteTerrain`, `BossSitePlacement`, `BiomeCells`, `PlannerThreads`; the user's rule, October 16): each boss biome will have 3 bosses (the three servants of its necromancer), never two in one patch of that biome. The biomes themselves generate exactly as before.
    - **The rule:** a boss biome's surface patches (chunks whose middle at y 80 is that biome, joined along their sides; specks under 10,000 blocks² skipped) are numbered by their nearest point's distance from `DrossHub.CENTER`; patches 1-3 are group 1, 4-6 group 2, and so on forever (to 32,000 blocks). In each group the world seed picks, among the patches where it fits, the one that holds the biome's current boss (slot 0); the other two are kept for its two future bosses (slots 1-2). If nothing fits normally, the roomiest patch gets the boss's smallest form; if even that fails, the group has none (a WARN). Today: **Cinder Wastes** (slot 0: the Cinder Colossus's lair) and **Blightwood** (slot 0: the Rat King's sewer; the kept patches roll for regular sewers, step 11); **Rimefall** (slot 0: the Huntsman's village since October 23, 2026; the kept patches roll for regular hunter villages, step 14), whose every patch gets a lake and a few herds through a **per-patch use**, `RIMEFALL_AREA` (`SiteUse.perPatch`: placed in every patch of its biome whatever its slot, asked straight from the patch without planning its group; step 14).
    - **The numbers** (measured over 60 seeds, October 16; edge distances, average): the nearest patches of Cinder Wastes are 418 / 623 / 848 blocks out, Blightwood 419 / 552 / 724, Rimefall 387 / 556 / 821, Gravecrags 783 / 814 / 885; group 1 ends about 700-900 blocks out, group 2 about 1,100-1,500, group 3 about 1,400-1,900, group 10 about 3,100-3,700; patch k's middle is about c·√k from the hub (c about 680 for Cinder Wastes, 630 for Blightwood); about 60-85 groups per biome within 10,000 blocks; a group's 3 patches lie on different sides of the hub, often 1,300-3,500 blocks apart. Patches are typically 325-370 across. The measuring scripts are kept outside the project.
    - **How it works:** from the noise alone (no chunks), deterministic and thread-safe (the same answers whichever thread asks first, and when), read outwards ring by ring and cached per seed.
    - **It doesn't stall world generation** (the optimisation pass, October 17; before, a thread asking about a far place read the map out to there under a lock while chunk generation waited, 2-13 s in play): a background thread reads the map out to 6,000 blocks when the world starts (about 2 s of one thread), then keeps it 3,000 blocks past the furthest player in the Dross and plans the groups of the patches within 1,024 blocks of each player; what's been read is published after each step, so questions about places already read never wait (`BiomeCells` remembers each 16-block cell's biome and patch; a column that isn't a boss biome is answered at once, however far out); a question further out reads the next rings itself, a band at a time, with helper threads sampling (`PlannerThreads`: half the processors, 1 to 8), and a group's fit tests run side by side; biome samples use a copy of the climate functions that works the shared warp noises out once per point (`SiteTerrain`; exactly Minecraft's answers, checked when it starts, with a fallback). Every number is in `BossSitesTuning` ("Reading the biome map"). Measured (22 processors, seed 1752286405569615815): reading the map from nothing out to 12,000 blocks took 1.7-2.0 s (9.7-11.1 s before); threads asking about places already read while another thread read out to 12,000 waited 0-7 ms (8.5-10.4 s before); 8 threads going through the 625 chunks round a spot 12,000 blocks out (as if a player had just teleported there) waited 1.6-1.7 s at worst (5.2-6.9 s before). The patches, numbers and plans are bit for bit the same as before (a fingerprint of every patch out to 8,000 blocks and the first 6 groups' plans for 5 seeds matched). Waits over 250 ms are logged (`[boss sites] waited ... ms`, split into sampling and joining). The timing probe `BossSitesSpeedGameTest` and `BossSitesFingerprintGameTest` have their own GameTest namespace, so the normal run leaves them out: `.\gradlew.bat runGameTestServer -PgameTestNamespaces=dross_speed -PgameTestWorld=<a new folder> --console=plain`.
    - **Its API:** `BossSites.forLevel(dross)`; `patches`, `patch`, `patchAt`; `group(biome, i)` (`patches`, `slots`, `bossSite`, `slotPatch`, `slotOf`, `filler`); `site(use, patch)`, `fitIn`, `siteAtChunk`, `isSiteChunk`, `nearest(use, from, max)`, `sitesNear`. Uses: `COLOSSUS_LAIR`, `RAT_KING_SEWER`, `REGULAR_SEWER`. A boss plugs in its own fit test with `BossSites.setFinder(use, finder)` in common setup (it may only read through `SiteTerrain` and must stay deterministic). The structures it places use the `dross:boss_site` placement (`BossSitePlacement`), which asks the planner.
    - **Preview**: `terrain-previews/boss_sites.png` (±3,200 blocks round the hub; every patch labelled like "B7 g3"; gold rings for boss sites, teal for regular sewers). **Headless tests** (`BossSitesGameTest`): groups of 3 nearest-first, exactly one boss patch per group, the kept two free, regular sewers on about half the kept Blightwood patches, every site inside its patch, the same answers in any order, the same answers whether the map was read by the background reader with 6 threads asking at once or by one thread furthest first (`sameAnswersWithOrWithoutTheBackgroundReader`, which also checks the quick biome sampler against Minecraft's on 20,000 columns), and every planned sewer starting in its planned chunk.

13. **The Rimefall candidates** [built: the looks only; the user's picks, with round 3's notes and fixes; the latest fixes not yet looked at in-game] (Boss area, `boss/rimefall/`; the user's requests, October 17-18, 2026): Rimefall's first bosses, the **Ice Necromancer's** three servants (step 12 keeps three Rimefall slots), and some of Rimefall's regular mobs.
    - **Round 1** (archived: `tools/rimefall/rimedesigns/round1/`, `boss-previews/rimefall/round1/`): about 20 original candidates for an ice-biome boss ("a yeti is hella overused"), from the size of a player to about 15 blocks tall, crazy and different, **only the models** ("no animations nothing"); then living things "like the ice statue" that look **strong** (the objects, vehicles and weather dropped; the Lost Balloon above all), Sea Angel and Aurora Eel taken back: 22 (1 The Glutton, 2 Pond Skater, 3 Starnose, 4 The Thawed, 5 Rimehorn, 6 Water Bear, 7 Rime Basilisk, 8 Bellowmaw, 9 Frost Mantis, 10 Sea Angel, 11 The Sculptor, 12 The Hush, 13 The Unfinished, 14 Tuskbreaker, 15 Shellback, 16 The Huntsman, 17 Floe King, 18 Fisher Below, 19 The Penitent, 20 Aurora Eel, 21 Sleeper Shark, 22 Reaching Hand). The user looked at them in-game (October 18), liked the see-through ice ("try not to force it"), kept nine (the others are out) and asked for round 2 with these notes, answering: an ice **trumpet**, the hand **about 25 blocks**, the Thawed **both sizes**, Rimehorn **the same size as now**; and "remove the lights from the showcase".
    - **Round 2: 23 models** (`tools/rimefall/rimedesigns/d01_reachinghand.py` ... `d09_thawed.py`, each with its variants; the user's notes in brackets):
      - Bosses: **1 Reaching Hand** (much bigger, the fingers must really look like trees): a giant's frozen hand out of a mound of Hoarfrost blocks, its fingers trunks two blocks thick in the Frostbitten Log's own bark texture, knotted at the knuckles, branches out and up like Rimefall's dead trees, icicles under them, forked twig tips, bark roots creeping down the grey frozen palm, a frozen ring with a pale stone; **1A the grove** (only the fingers out of the ground, a ring of dead trees, 16 tall), **1B the reach** (hand and wrist out, fingers spread, 27 tall), **1C the grab** (fingers curling forward, 20 tall). **2 The Snow Angel** (was The Penitent: "angelic, with the see-through ice: wings, a halo and an ice [trumpet] in the hand that doesn't hold the bell"): the snow-spike robe, hood, eyes and bell kept, wings of see-through ice feathers, a glowing ice halo, an ice trumpet; **2A wings folded** (trumpet at its side, 15 tall), **2B wings spread** (about 25 blocks across, the trumpet raised to its hood, 17 tall). **3 Sleeper Shark** (it lives under the ice; walk over an ice lake and it pops out): bursting up through a frozen lake, slabs of see-through lake ice tipped up round the hole, dark water, frozen spray, jaws wide with a U of teeth on each jaw, a pale belly, dorsal fins, glowing parasites; **3A straight up** (14 tall), **3B lunging at you** (9 tall). **4 Aurora Eel** (the prettiest, see-through ice in aurora colours, longer, about 50% bulkier): its body clear ice in northern-lights colours with a glowing spine of light inside, curtains of light with gaps, glowing whiskers, half as long and thick again as round 1's; **4A green and teal** (a crest of crystals), **4B green to pink** (green head through teal and violet to a pink tail, ribbon fins). **5 Fisher Below** (the fish much bigger; the house in real Minecraft house textures, then the fish shows up; different colour ices; see-through ice teeth): a huge dark anglerfish head through a frozen lake, fangs of clear ice, a crust of ice crystals in four colours (deep blue, teal, white, violet), glowing eyes and spots, a lit lantern on its lure, and its bait a 5 x 5 spruce cabin of **real blocks** (spruce logs and planks, cobblestone, glass panes, a spruce door, a stair roof, a slab ridge, a brick chimney with frozen smoke, a lantern by the door; warm light inside); **5A house on a line** (the cabin dangling from its lure in front of its open mouth, 21 tall), **5B in its jaws** (the cabin on an ice floe, the fish come up under it, jaws gaping round it, 22 tall). **6 The Huntsman** (a much better, more complex model; the mount more complex): the rider's deer-skull mask with tall antlers in a deep hood, glowing sockets, a wolf-fur mantle with the wolf's head, bone ribs over a leather jerkin, gauntlets, a cloak torn into streaming strips, a belt of trophies, a hunting horn, a quiver of ice-fletched arrows, an ice lance with a pennant; the mount's shaggy mane, pale knobby legs, cloven hooves, a saddle, blanket, girth, breast strap, bridle and reins, a bone face plate, skulls hanging from the saddle; **6A on a moose** (a high hump, a drooping muzzle, the bell under its throat, broad flat antlers, 12 tall), **6B on a giant elk** (a high neck, enormous antlers, 13 tall).
      - Regular mobs: **7 Rimehorn** (no ice on its back; its 2 front horns see-through ice; better fur layers; non-ice eyes; the same size): a woolly rhino in tiers of shaggy fur like shingles, longest along its belly and chin, horns of clear ice, plain dark eyes, about 5 blocks; **7A frosted coat**, **7B dark brown coat**. **8 Pond Skater** (the same height, legs 10-25% shorter, options): **8A-8D** legs 10, 15, 20 and 25% shorter, all 3 tall. **9 Thawed Soldier** ("the thawed soldier"; both sizes): hide armour with whale-bone ribs, a fur mantle, a bone helmet with tusks and horns, an icicle beard, glowing eyes, glacier slabs still on him; **9A axe, 2.5 tall**, **9B spear and shield, 2.5 tall**, **9C axe, 4 tall**, **9D spear and shield, 4 tall** (the axe a crescent of clear ice in a whale's jawbone, held upright; the spear's head a leaf of ice, a round hide shield with a bone cross).

Its spots, spines and crystals rooted in its head; the roof of its mouth now strips that stay inside the lip (one wide slab poked out at its corners: "part of the gums seems to be leaking out").

Then "shrink the hunter by anywhere from 2-5 blocks ... just shrink down the moose and hunter together": four sizes, rider and moose scaled down as one (`Scaled`), and the user picked **"7.7ish"** (all the Huntsmen are now about 7.7 blocks tall; round 2's 12.4 is archived in round3/). With a bow raised, his left fist (and its rein) moves up; everything else holds the reins as before. **The user's pick, put together from three: "6H's body, just replace the head with 6C's [antlers], and the face should be like 6D"**: now the only Huntsman, **6A**: 6H's pale mantle under bone pauldrons and bearskin, a wolf's head worn as a hood (6D's snout and fangs over his face, his eyes glowing under it) and 6C's tall deer antlers rising out of it, about 7.7 blocks tall; then ("a bow on his back, on his moose a bunch of arrows with the quiver, and he holds a spear; make a spot for him to put his spear") **6A (spear and bow)**: the axe swapped for a boar spear of ice held upright in his right fist, its butt resting in a leather cup on the saddle's front corner (a lance bucket), a bow on his back (on the left, clear of the bearskin, now three narrower strips), and a quiver of nine ice-fletched arrows strapped to the saddle behind his right leg (the ten riders and their parts are archived in `rimedesigns/round3/d06_huntsman_ten_riders.py` and `boss-previews/rimefall/round3/huntsman_riders/`).

Then (the user: "got much better"): the box of a beard under its chin gone ("this weird part below his mouth"), and the icicles, which hung from the skirt's ragged edge and looked as if they floated, now hang down the outside of the skirt, touching it, and on below its edge. Then ("it's still there", "the big horn placement is off ... connect the nose/snout better to the face ... a smaller horn on the top of its head then a bigger one"): the box under its throat and the cheek tufts gone too, and the head rebuilt as one tapering line at one tilt (neck, furry skull, bare face, muzzle with nostrils and mouth; round 2's turned fur neck had a box of a snout stuck on its end), the short horn on top of its head and the great one growing from the top of its nose behind the nostrils (the first layered coat is archived in round3/); then the face and muzzle 6 units shorter ("the snout is way too much out").
