The bow is slung across his back over it (left shoulder to right hip) on a strap over his left shoulder. (A dark brown cloak vanished against the moose.) Then ("make his bow a little bigger and his spear a little longer ... half a block or even a block"): the spear 119 units long (was 98; about 0.8 of a block longer in the game, its head now above his antlers in the saddle and above his hood on foot) and the bow 77 tip to tip (was 57.5; about 0.75 of a block bigger), reaching up over his left shoulder (its lower tip stays where the cloak turns back over the saddle).
      - **His body** ("make sure the anatomy of the rider makes sense ... in the second stage of his boss fight he'll leave his moose ... his legs don't seem to connect with the torso"): built on a real skeleton of a man's proportions (`d06_huntsman.py`: hips under the belt, thighs 28 units, shins 27, the boot's sole 7 under the ankle, arms 19 + 17 bent by two-bone IK) that sits the moose (thighs over the saddle, shins down its sides into the stirrups) or stands; standing he's about 4.5 blocks to the top of his hood and 5.6 to his antler tips. His belt's trophies (they hung into the saddle) are now one small skull at his left hip, and his hunting horn hangs across his belly on a cord over his left shoulder (on his hip it went through his thigh and the cloak).
      - **Rider and moose apart**, so they can split in his fight: the gallery shows **6A "the Wild Hunt"** (riding, as his fight starts), **6A1 "on foot"** (standing, his spear upright beside him) and **6A2 "alpha moose"** (the Alpha Moose with nobody on it: its stirrups hanging, its reins looped back to the saddle). The saddle's spear cup and quiver belong to the moose; in the saddle his reins run to his left fist.
      - **Rimehorn 7A/7B** ("make them like four and a half blocks tall and like eight blocks long"): the body one slice shorter and the whole animal at 0.92 of its size: 4.5 blocks tall (4.7 with 7B's snow), 8.2 long, 3.6 wide (was 4.9 x 9.4).
    - **The look**: the Ice Necromancer's pale blues and white, never the Dross blue (the Aurora Eel's northern-lights colours, the Fisher's coloured ice and warm windows are the user's requests); see-through ice where it belongs (wings, halo, trumpet, horns, fangs, lake ice, the eel), not forced.
    - **Made by** `tools/rimefall/` (its README): `build.py` (`--only`, `--quick`, `--previews-only`), `closeup.py`, `zcheck.py`, `rimekit/` (shapes with `Scaled` and `block()`; the materials, among them `tiled`/`tiled_ground` (the mod's own block textures at one texel a unit), `shag` (layered hanging fur), `aurora_ice`; the four textures; `uvpack.py`: **per-face UVs**, every face its own piece of texture and hidden faces none at all, so the giants' textures stay small; `zfight.py`; `connect.py`: floating pieces (and `floatcheck.py`); `blocks.py`: real blocks in the previews, from Minecraft's own textures in the Gradle cache, preview only). Previews in `boss-previews/rimefall/`: `<label>_<id>.png` (four sides, at night, from above), `all_designs.png`, `lineup.png` (all of them at one scale beside a player).
    - **In the game**: one entity type, `dross:rimefall_candidate` (`RimefallCandidate`), wears any design (a synced number, saved as `Design`); `RimefallDesigns.Design` (generated): a unique number, its label ("1A"), id, name, variant (`mood`), boss or regular mob, measured sizes, which textures it has, and its real blocks (`Placed`: a block state and its corner in model units). Its hitbox is the design's measured size and it's drawn as far as the model (and its blocks) reach. No AI, silent, can't be hurt except by `/kill`, can't be pushed or leashed, never despawns, doesn't freeze; GeckoLib with no animation controllers. Its renderer (`client/RimefallCandidateRenderer`) draws the solid parts, their glow, the see-through ice and the ice's glow, and `client/RimefallBlocksLayer` draws the real blocks with the game's own block models (`RimefallBlocks` reads their states). Right-click: its label, name, variant and height on the action bar.
    - **The showcase** (`command/RimefallShowcase`): `/dross showcase boss_designs rimefall` builds a snowy gallery ahead of you, about 106 blocks long and 55 wide (the picks, 10 models: the Huntsman three times; the Rimehorns and the Pond Skater left it when they became real animals, step 14) (clears 3 blocks over the tallest): a 5-wide calcite walkway (no lights: the user's request), the bosses first and then, after a gap, the regular mobs, each section opening with signs either side ("Rimefall", "Bosses" / "Regular mobs", "round 2"); the models take turns left and right in order, so a design's variants face each other across the walkway, each facing it (the long ones stand sideways, facing back towards you: the Aurora Eels and Rimehorns), a packed ice post and a sign by each ("No. 1A", name, variant). The chat lists them all with [go] buttons. Running it again replaces the gallery (old candidates within 300 blocks removed).
    - **Headless tests** (`RimefallShowcaseGameTest`): every design spawns frozen with its measured hitbox, shrugs off a big hit and a push and stays put; the gallery holds all 10, each once, on its floor, facing along the compass, and a second run replaces them; every real block (316, the two cabins) is a block state the game reads (`candidateBlocksAreRealBlocks`). 179 GameTests pass.
    - **Next**: step 14 (Rimefall's animals and the Huntsman's fight); later the other picks' animations and fights (`BOSS_GUIDE.md`) and their Rimefall homes on the boss-site planner (step 12).

14. **Rimefall's animals and the Huntsman's fight** [parts 1-3 built (the animals and Rimefall's lakes not yet tested in-game); part 4, the Huntsman's fight, built October 20 (a spawn egg and a basic arena; not yet tested in-game); his village home, patrol and sounds to build] (Boss area for the mobs and the fight; Dimension for Rimefall's spawn lists; World site for the commands; Quest for the advancement; the user's plan and answers, October 18, 2026). In this order, the user checking in-game after each: (1) the Huntsman's cloak and body, rider and moose apart, the Rimehorn's size [built: step 13's round 4]; (2) skeletons (bones) and animations for the Rimehorn, the moose and the Pond Skater, with preview filmstrips and a clipping check [built, previews only: see "The animals' skeletons and animations" below]; (3) their behaviour, spawning, spawn eggs, `/dross showcase rimefall_mobs` and original sound options [built: see "The animals in the game" and "Rimefall's big lakes and herds" below]; (4) the Huntsman's and the Alpha Moose's animations, the fight, the arena, boss bar options and sound options. Every number a labeled constant.
    - **Rimefall's spawns** (the user: "the skaters, the rimehorns and the moose should be the only mobs spawning there"): no zombies, skeletons, endermen, witches, bats or vanilla animals; only Rimehorn packs, moose and Pond Skaters (and its bosses later). So Rimefall gives no Necromancy souls or Deathforged books.
    - **Rimehorn** (`dross:rimehorn`, 7A/7B): neutral; hitting one (a baby too) turns its pack (within about 16 blocks) on you. Packs of 6-8: 2 frosted males (7A), the rest dark brown females (7B). The three animations the user asked for: **eating** (scraping the snow and grazing), a **horn flick** (a headbutt flicking up that launches you mostly up, a little back: 6 damage, about 8 with the landing; the user: 8 "including the knock, or maybe not, you can pick"), a **rush** (a 7-8 block charge when you hurt it from range, an arrow for example: 5 damage); plus idle, walk and run. **Babies**: bred from a male and a female with Frostbloom; a baby takes its sex's coat and grows up in about 35 minutes (the user: 30-40, since "their meat will give out more saturation" later); **a Rimehorn you bred never attacks you**, grown up and even when its pack is angry. No drops for now (meat later), a little XP.
    - **Moose** (`dross:moose`, about 3.5-4 blocks tall with its antlers; the Huntsman's Alpha Moose stays bigger): neutral and solo; hit one and it lowers its antlers and charges, knocking you mostly back (the Rimehorn's flick knocks you mostly up). **Mothers**: sometimes one spawns with a calf (like polar bears); a mother with her calf near attacks you on sight, a moose without a calf never does. Calves grow up in 20 minutes; moose aren't bred. No drops for now. Animations: idle, graze, walk, run, antler charge.
    - **Pond Skater** (`dross:pond_skater`, 8C): hostile, spawning on Rimefall's ice (its packed-ice patches and frozen ponds; bigger frozen ponds if that's too little); spider speed off the ice, almost an ocelot's on it ("they catch up to you instantly"); it rears up and stabs with its beak (16 health, 3 damage, like a spider). Its hitbox: the body, and a small one on each of its 6 legs that moves with it (Forge's multipart parts, like the Ender Dragon's), so a swing at a leg hits it; the legs don't bump into walls. Animations: idle, walk, skate (rowing strokes, then a glide), attack.
    - **The Huntsman's fight** (part 4; planned with the user October 19, 2026, building in stages: boss bars, then the villages, then the animations and the fight; `dross:huntsman` riding `dross:alpha_moose`): his spawn egg (riding), `/dross boss summon huntsman`, `/dross boss arena huntsman` (a snowy arena). He blows his horn when he spots you.
      - **Stage 1, "The Wild Hunt"** (the user's pick of the pair's name; one boss bar of **200** health for both; the moose runs, he fights from the saddle). The user's picks: **spear thrust**; the moose's **antler charge**; **arrow rain** (he shoots **10-20 arrows** quickly into the sky; they land at random spots in a circle round you a moment later, frost marks showing where; a hit frosts your screen and slows you, like powder snow); **back kick** (the moose kicks you if you're behind it); **circling hunt** (the moose gallops a ring round you while he shoots from the saddle); **Horn of the Hunt** (a horn blast: a cone of icy wind that slows you and pushes you away); **bola throw** (a weighted rope of ice: it holds you still for 2 s, then 5 s of Slowness). Dropped: trample, harpoon, rearing stomp.
      - **The split**: when the 200 bar runs out the moose rears, he leaps off and blows his horn; neither can be hurt for 2 seconds. **Stage 2**: two fresh bars of **150** each, "The Huntsman" and "Alpha Moose" (the user: "200 for the total together, and then 150, 150 for each").
      - **Stage 2, the Huntsman on foot**: **spear combo** (thrust, thrust, sweep), **thrust and sweep**, **leaping spear strike**, **arrow rain**, **ice snares** (he throws 3 traps near you that snap shut if you step in them), **thrown spear** (he hurls it, then pulls it back on its rope), **aimed shot** (a charged arrow with a frost glint as its warning; a hit freezes you briefly), **backstep and shoot** (he leaps out of reach and fires twice). Dropped: war cry, mark the prey.
      - **Stage 2, the Alpha Moose**: **charges** more often, **antler toss** (scoops you up and flings you high), **stomp**, **back kick**, **bellow** (a roar in a cone: knockback and slowness), **cracking stamp** (cracks run across the ice towards you, then spikes burst up under you, with a warning). Dropped: rearing slam, snow kick, the pincer and herding pair moves.
      - **When one dies** the other gets angrier (faster, harder hits, shorter waits), and that's all (the user: "when one dies, that's it"); its bar turns to its angry look.
      - **The attack table** (approved by the user October 20, 2026, with their edits; damage in hearts): **Stage 1** (riding, 200): Spear Thrust 4 (cd 2 s); Antler Charge **6.5** (locks your spot; cd 6 s); Back Kick **4.5** (automatic when you're within 3 blocks behind the moose; cd 4 s); Arrow Rain 15 arrows of 1.5 and a frost slow, **no marks warning where they land** (the user); Circling Hunt (the moose rings you about 10 out while he fires 4 arrows of 2.5); Horn of the Hunt (a cone that pushes you about 8 and gives Slowness II, 1 heart); Bola (held still 2 s, then Slowness 5 s); Ride Off. By distance: 0-5 Thrust 50 / Horn 20 / Ride Off 20 / Bola 10; 5-14 Circling 30 / Charge 30 / Bola 20 / Rain 20; 14-28 Charge 40 / Rain 40 / Circling 20; 28-40 Rain 60 / Ride In 40. **He blows his horn when you first attack the pair** (the fight starts then) and at the split. **Stage 2, on foot (150)**: Spear Combo 3/3/4, Thrust & Sweep 3.5 + 4, Leaping Strike 5 and a small shockwave, Thrown Spear 4.5, Aimed Shot 5 and frozen 1.5 s (a frost glint warns you), Backstep & Shoot 3, Arrow Rain 10 arrows, the Bola (in place of the ice snares), and the Horn of the Hunt (the user: also in stage 2); 0-4 Combo 40 / T&S 35 / Backstep 25; 4-12 Leap 35 / Thrown 25 / Bola 20 / Run In 20; 12-28 Aimed 35 / Rain 30 / Thrown 20 / Bola 15; 28-40 Aimed 50 / Rain 50. **The Alpha Moose (150)**: Ram 4.5, Antler Toss 4 (flung about 6 up), Stomp 5, Back Kick 4.5 (automatic), Bellow (a cone: pushed back, Slowness II, 1 heart), Cracking Stamp 5 (cracks, then ice spikes; about 1.2 s warning), Antler Charge 6.5 (more often); 0-5 Ram 35 / Toss 25 / Stomp 25 / Bellow 15; 5-14 Charge 45 / Stamp 30 / Bellow 25; 14-30 Charge 60 / Stamp 40; over 30 Charge 70 / Run In 30. **Angry** (the other dead): 20% faster, 25% more damage, moves back 30% sooner, shorter pauses, the bar blood red. With two or more players the moose takes the closest, the Huntsman the furthest. No damage cuts. **For now he only comes from a spawn egg and a basic arena** (`/dross boss arena huntsman`), not the villages (the user: "don't make him spawn in yet with the village, let him just be a spawn egg with a basic arena"). As built, the Horn of the Hunt is fitted into stage 2's table on foot: 0-4 Combo 35 / T&S 30 / Backstep 20 / Horn 15, 4-12 Leap 30 / Thrown 25 / Bola 20 / Horn 10 / Run In 15 (the other rows as above).
      - **The fight as built** [built October 20, not yet tested in-game] (Boss area, `boss/huntsman/`; every number in `HuntsmanTuning`): **getting him**: the "Huntsman Spawn Egg" (spawn eggs group, after the Pond Skater's) brings him in the Alpha Moose's saddle (`HuntsmanPair`), calm; `/dross boss summon huntsman` (16 blocks in front of you, not on Peaceful); `/dross boss arena huntsman` (`boss/HuntsmanArena`): a round floor of snow with patches of packed ice, 41 blocks across, the air cleared 22 up, gaps filled 6 down, 12 lantern posts round its edge, and a [Summon the Huntsman] button. The two are partners (each saves the other's id) and never hurt each other (blows, arrows, the bola, the spear, the spikes). **Stages** (`HuntStage`: calm, the Wild Hunt, the split, the duel, won): the first hit by anything living starts the Wild Hunt and his horn (`ride_horn`); in stage 1 their own health stays full and every hit comes off the moose's shared pool of 200 (250 since October 23; `HuntMob.actuallyHurt`, `HuntFight.damageWildHunt`); at 0 the split: the moose rears, he leaps off, neither can be hurt for 2 s, he blows his horn when he lands; then the duel, each on a full 150 with his own bar. One dead, the other is angry (`HuntFight.makeAngry`: 20% faster, 25% more damage, moves back 30% sooner, shorter pauses, the blood-red bar). Both dead: "The Hunter Hunted" for the player credited with the last kill and every player within 40 blocks (never for `/kill`), 25 XP each, and the bodies vanish in a puff of snow 10 s after their death animations end. **Whom they go for** (`HuntFight.chooseTarget`): the moose the closest player, him the furthest (not creative or spectators), else whatever hurt them. **The moves** (`WildHuntGoal` in the saddle, `FootGoal` and `MooseGoal` on their own; picked from the tables above by distance, a move on cooldown or that can't be made drops out, the Back Kick made at once whenever someone is close behind the moose): his arrows are real arrows loosed from the model's bow (`HuntCues`), the arrow rain's fall from 18 blocks up round you with no warning marks and frost you (Slowness and frozen ticks), the aimed shot's freeze you (`dross:snared`: held still, and you can't jump), the bola (`dross:bola`, `Bola`) holds you 2 s then slows you 5 s, the thrown spear (`dross:hunt_spear`, `HuntSpear`) comes back to his hand after it hits or after 4 s (drawn as Minecraft's trident until the user's own spear model exists), the cracking stamp's cracks run over the snow to you and ice spikes burst up (block displays, gone after 1.5 s and on a restart). **Broken off** (a village's pair lets a player who got away go and heads home: "The chase as built" below): with no player within 64 blocks for 3 minutes (`BREAK_OFF_RANGE`, `BREAK_OFF_TICKS`; 30 s until October 24, 2026), the fight ends: both back at full health (a dead one comes back), him back in the saddle, calm. Its sounds are the user's picks ("Sounds" below). Headless tests (`boss/huntsman/HuntsmanFightGameTest`): an egg brings him calm in the saddle; the first hit starts the Wild Hunt and the horn, hits come off the shared pool, their own health stays full; the split (no hurting, him off the moose, both at 150, the two bars); one down makes the other angry and faster, both down gives "The Hunter Hunted" to the killer and a player near, not one 60 blocks off; every table row's shares over 20,000 rolls (within 4 standard deviations of chance); no friendly fire; the bola snares; the moose kicks what's behind it; broken off, they come back full and calm, him in the saddle.
      - **The user's rule for the fight** (October 20, 2026): "the hunter wants to keep the battle ranged, but once
        you approach, he or his moose will attack you: the struggle is trying to survive a far-away battle, or come
        close to out-damage him at near range". Every later tuning or new move follows it (his arrows, rain, bola and
        horn from afar; his spear and the moose's kicks, ram, toss and stomp up close). Since October 22 his footwork
        follows it (below).
      - **Fixes from the user's play test of October 22** (stage 1 of the four-stage plan made from it: 1 the fight, 2
        the cloak never through the moose's back, 3 the Moonsilver Spear, a standing spear block, mounted skulls and
        the egg's colours, 4 lights inside the villages and their stairs) [built, not yet tested in-game] (numbers in
        `HuntsmanTuning`, "spotting, turning, moving"):
        - **He spots you** (the user: the horn "when there is a hostile nearby, not when you first attack it"): a foe
          within 30 blocks that he or the moose can see starts the Wild Hunt and his horn (checked every half second,
          `SPOT_RANGE`, `SPOT_INTERVAL`; a hit still starts it too). A foe (`HuntFight.isFoe`): a player not in
          creative or spectator, a hostile mob (a Pond Skater: he hunts those too) and, raged, a mob of another team.
          Whom they go for: a Rage enemy first, then players (the moose the closest, him the furthest), then the other
          foes the same way, then whatever hurt them.
        - **The Rage Wand**: raging either one rages both, on that team (each is the other's follower, `RageLeader`),
          and raged they fight other teams' raged mobs, a raged Rat King too, with their own moves (until October 24,
          2026 they only headbutted: neither has an attack-damage stat, their blows being `HuntsmanTuning`'s numbers, so
          the Rage took them for animals with no attack and gave them its headbutt, which outranks their moves; now a
          boss, any `RageLeader`, never gets it: `RageAi.headbutts`; the user: "the Huntsman doesn't seem to attack when I
          use the Rage Wand on him against the other bosses". Measured by a probe, `HuntProbeGameTest.ragedHuntVersus...`:
          the raged pair took a raged Rat King from 200 to 150 in 45 s and a raged Colossus from 300 to 268 in 23 s).
          **They deal with what attacks them first** (the user, October 25, 2026: fighting the Cinder Colossus "the moose
          and Huntsman ignore the little golems that the Cinder Colossus spawns in": they kept on at the nearest raged
          enemy by its middle, the Colossus, while its golems went for them): a mob of their foes within 10 blocks going
          for either of them is their target first (`HuntFight.attacker`, `DEFEND_RANGE`), raged or not (players still
          come first when not raged). Measured (the same probe, the Colossus at 215 in Cracking): they turned on its stone
          golems as they climbed out and killed four in 45 s, the moose's charges and ram doing most of it. **Then
          (October 25, the user, after playing it: "the hunter still doesn't target the rock minions like they are
          attacking him ... during the split part")**: after the split each deals with what's going for it first, and
          only then with what's after the other (together in the saddle, both alike); and what he aims at one (an
          arrow, the rain, his thrown spear, the bola) flies on through any other but a player (`HuntFight.AIM_TAG`, read
          by `HuntsmanFightEvents.onImpact`): his shots at a golem had been hitting the Colossus behind it. Measured
          (`HuntProbeGameTest.footVersusGolem`): on foot against a golem going for him 3 blocks off, 12 blows of his
          spear combo, 8 of the thrust and sweep, 3 throws of his spear and 2 arrows landed in 30 s; the post-split fight
          against a raged Colossus (`ragedDuelVersusColossus`, also alone in `HuntShotProbeGameTest`) has him turning
          on its golems as they come and back to it when they're dead.
        - **Hitting him in the Wild Hunt**: in the saddle his hitbox sits over the moose's back (3.2 wide and 4 tall,
          from 3.8 over the moose's feet: `RIDER_SEAT`, `RIDER_WIDTH`, `RIDER_HEIGHT`; it was the moose's own feet), so
          blows and arrows at him hit him, and they come off the shared bar of 200 like the moose's. Up there branches
          can't smother him.
        - **Deaths**: only their own death animations (`HuntRenderer`): no red flash on a dying one and no vanilla tip
          onto its side (which put the moose's legs through the ground); showcase ones keep vanilla's.
        - **Moving** (the user: "awkward and jittery", "wavy" circling, the moose facing north while moving
          diagonally): the body always faces where they go (`HuntMob.tickHeadTurn`; the moose's went stale while it
          stood with him on it); they turn at most 9 degrees a tick (the moose) and 20 (him) and slow down when turning
          sharply (to 30% at 90 degrees off, `HuntMoveControl`), so a circle is a smooth ring; walk and run switch with
          a margin and a 4-tick hold, so they don't flicker between them; the walk, run and gallop play as fast as they
          really move (`MOOSE_*_PACE`, `HUNTSMAN_*_PACE`: blocks a second over the blocks a loop covers, kept to
          0.5-1.6x); and the moose and his saddle share one clock (`HuntController`: each animation's time comes from a
          synced start and pace, `HuntMob.anchor()`/`pace()`, so his gallop keeps the moose's beat and a change of
          speed carries on from the same moment of the stride). The renderer turns him with the moose's body (GeckoLib
          turned a rider by his own head).
        - **The split** (the user: "he leaps off from one spot ... doesn't shake around"): neither moves while the moose
          rears, and he lands on the same spot on the server and on screens (`Huntsman.landing`).
        - **Roles** (the user: the moose close up, the Huntsman at range; in stage 2 "they shouldn't clip into each
          other"): on foot, when no move is ready, he falls back to 18 blocks within 12 (away from the moose too,
          `fall_back`), circles at 14-24 blocks from 12 to 28 (`keep_range`) and closes in to 22 from further
          (`close_in`); his table's "run in" (4-12 blocks) is now "fall back". In the duel he's pushed out of an oval
          round the moose (3.6 blocks ahead and behind, 2.2 to its sides: `APART_*`), and his backstep and leap never
          land inside it.
        - **Walls** (the user, October 25, 2026: "he often moves into walls"): falling back, keeping his range, the
          moose riding off and circling now go only where there's room (`HuntRoom`: how far one can go along the ground
          before a wall, a drop of over 3 or water stops it): falling back he takes the nearest turn of his way out with
          room that still takes him away from his foe (along the wall), circling he goes round the other way at a wall
          (the moose too, and if it's walled in both ways it ends its circle), and the backstep shot only leaps where
          there's room behind him. Until then their spots were picked by distance alone, so beside a wall they ran into
          it until the move's time ran out. Headless test: `HuntsmanFightGameTest.heFallsBackAlongTheWall`.
        - Headless tests (`HuntsmanFightGameTest`): `aFoeInSightStartsTheHunt` (a creative watcher doesn't, a survival
          player does, with the horn, and both go for him), `ragedTheyFightAsAPair`, `theRiderCanBeHit`,
          `theClockKeepsTheBeat`.
      - **The cloak never through the moose's back** (stage 2 of the plan of October 22; the user's rule: "the cape can
        never go through the moose's back") [built, not yet looked at in-game]: the riding cloak (`d06_huntsman`:
        `CLOAK_RIDE`, `CLOAK_RIDE_FOLD`, and new, `CLOAK_RIDE_MIDDLE`, each stretch's flat middle as a share of its
        width) was fitted to the moose as it's built (its z-fighting fix grows some boxes by up to 0.3 units): from
        the saddle back it's lifted 1.5 to 8.5 design units (at most about a quarter of a block in the game) and lies
        flat over the saddle, its cantle and rolled fur; over the rump its middle is wider (0.7 of its width) and its
        sides still fold down round it (55, 55 and 40 degrees). `tools/rimefall/mobs.py` checks the rule on every
        build of the riding Huntsman or the Alpha Moose (`check_cape`): each of his riding animations over each of the
        moose's (but its rear and death: he leaps off first; he gallops in the saddle while it runs or charges), the
        saddle at the moose's own moment, and no box of the cloak may come nearer its back, tail or hind legs than 0.05
        units (no allowance for what touched at rest, as there was before); as built it keeps at least 0.09 units
        off (in the thrust over the moose's gallop). The gallery's 6A was rebuilt with it.
      - **The Moonsilver Spear, the planted spear, the mounted skulls and the egg** (stage 3 of the plan of October 22)
        [built, not yet tested in-game] (Boss area: `boss/huntsman/spear/`, `boss/huntsman/skull/`; the user's
        answers: it stabs and is thrown like a trident and comes back, both freeze; bare skulls; players can get
        them):
        - **The Moonsilver Spear** (`dross:moonsilver_spear`, `MoonsilverSpearItem`; numbers in `SpearTuning`): the
          user's recipe, two sticks on a diagonal and a Moonsilver Ingot at the top right (`recipes/moonsilver_spear`);
          a stab does 8 (a trident's attack speed); held back half a second and let go it's thrown
          (`ThrownMoonsilverSpear`, entity `dross:moonsilver_spear`: 8, like a trident) and flies back to its thrower
          like a trident with Loyalty II, only its thrower can catch it; what it hits either way is frozen for 4 s
          (vanilla's frozen state: the frost, shaking, slowed) and gets Slowness II as long (`SpearFreeze`; mobs that
          can't freeze are only slowed); 500 uses, repaired with Moonsilver Ingots. **Its enchantments** (the user,
          October 25, 2026, of a list of those that fit a thrown hunting spear: "those are good, add those"; until then
          only Unbreaking, Mending and Curse of Vanishing, as it's neither a sword nor a trident to the game): Sharpness
          or Smite (Moonsilver being the anti-undead metal), Impaling (a thrown spear's hit on anything, not only on sea
          creatures as a trident's: `ThrownMoonsilverSpear`), Looting, Knockback and Channeling (lightning on what it
          hits in a thunderstorm), at the enchanting table and from books (`MoonsilverSpearItem.canApplyAtEnchantingTable`,
          `SpearTuning.EXTRA_ENCHANTMENTS`); not Fire Aspect (it fights the freeze), Riptide (it would throw you), Loyalty
          (it comes back anyway) or Sweeping Edge. In the Dross
          tab's weapons and tools after the Rage Wand. Its look (`tools/huntsman/spear.py`): the Huntsman's own spear,
          a plain dark pole, a leather collar and a spike of moonsilver with an icy edge, 2.5 blocks long, 3D in the
          hand, on the ground and thrown (`spear/client/SpearRenderer` draws its item model along its flight), a flat
          icon in the inventory and item frames like the trident's (Forge's `separate_transforms`); preview
          `boss-previews/rimefall/huntsman/moonsilver_spear.png`. **The Huntsman's thrown spear** (`HuntSpear`) is
          drawn with the same model at 1.8 times the size (a trident stood in for it).
        - **The Planted Moonsilver Spear** (`dross:planted_spear`, `PlantedSpearBlock`): the spear's model at 0.7,
          standing 1.75 blocks tall on its block, a decoration that drops itself (end of the blocks group). It
          replaced every lightning rod that stood in for a spear in the igloos, the lodge, the barn and the
          decorations. **Nothing over a planted spear** (the user, October 26, 2026: in the igloos the spears stuck up
          through the snow, the lightning rods having been 1 block tall): every template's check
          (`villagekit/checks.py`, igloos, decorations and whole villages) fails on a spear with any block over it;
          the Twin Hunters' two and two of the Workshop's four stood against the dome's wall and moved a step in.
        - **The mounted skulls** (`dross:alpha_moose_skull`, `moose_skull`, `rimehorn_skull`; `HuntSkullBlock`, its
          `facing` and `wall`): placed on top of a block a skull stands there (on a post) facing whoever placed it,
          against a block's side it hangs there facing out. Bare bone skulls without the lower jaw, made by
          `tools/huntsman/skulls.py` with the Rimefall kit from the animals' own shapes (the moose's antlers,
          `d06_huntsman.antlers`; the Rimehorn's horns), exported as baked block models (Forge's OBJ loader:
          `models/block/hunt_skull/`, no block entity, so a village of them costs nothing to draw; the Rimehorn's
          drawn translucent for its clear ice): the Alpha Moose's (old frosted, cracked bone, its great palm antlers
          hung with icicles), a bull moose's (clean bone, no icicles), a Rimehorn's (a long low rhino skull, its two
          horns of clear ice); since October 23 **life size**, the size of the animals' own heads (the user: "the size
          of their actual skull size, not a downgrade"; `skulls.py` builds each at its animal's scale, `alpha_moose.K`,
          `moose.K`, `rimehorn.K`): the Alpha Moose's 5.2 blocks across its antlers and 2 tall, a bull's 3.7 across and
          1.4 tall, a Rimehorn's 1 wide, 2.8 tall with its horn and 2.4 long (until then 3.8, 2.8 and 2.1). Preview `boss-previews/rimefall/huntsman/
          skulls.png`. In the trophies group after the Rat King's. **Drops** (`SkullDrops`): the Alpha Moose always,
          every time it dies (a fight broken off and won again gives another); a grown bull moose or a grown Rimehorn
          1 in 10, plus 1 in 50 a level of Looting, only when a player killed it.
        - **In the villages** (`tools/huntsman`): moose skulls crown the antler posts, the antler gate's beam, the
          antler lamps, the trophy board and the palm antlers' post (their antlers of birch fences gone; the palm
          antlers' an Alpha Moose's until October 25), the Alpha Moose's skull only the lodge's way in and the barn's door
          (the user, October 25, 2026: "only the Huntsman's home's front and the barn's front should have Alpha Moose
          skulls, the rest should be regular moose"; the barn's was a bull's until then), a bull's the corral's posts;
          Rimehorn skulls the
          skull posts, the skull lamps and the Rimehorn corral's corners; his yard's path between moose and Rimehorn
          skulls by turns. Skeleton skulls stay indoors and in the bone and skull heaps (a moose's antlers are too wide
          for a room). A decoration keeps room round its skulls' antlers and horns (`villagekit/dross_blocks.REACH`,
          used by `villagekit/decor.py`). The barn's gables now start on its end walls (a slit ran along their tops,
          and only the old block-built moose head held its roof to the walls in the build's check).
        - **The Huntsman Spawn Egg**: drawn whole (a spawn egg has only two tints), tinted white. First in cream,
          glacier blue and brown (the user's colours), then redrawn on October 23 (the user: "the spawn egg sucks"),
          and that day the user picked H2 from the drawn options (`tools/eggs/eggs.py`, below; `tools/huntsman/egg.py`
          is gone).
        - Headless tests (`spear/HuntGearGameTest`): the recipe's shape, a stab's 8 and its freeze, a throw that hits
          for about 8, freezes and comes back, every skull on the floor and on a wall facing each way, the planted
          spear, the Alpha Moose's skull at its death, a moose's 1 in 10 only for a player's kill.
      - **Lights inside the villages and their stairs** (stage 4 of the plan of October 22; the user: "we need some

        meet the ground") [built, not yet looked at in-game] (`tools/huntsman/village_designs/warren.py`, the props
        `court_lamp` and `lane_lamp` in `villagekit/props.py`): every courtyard has tall poles of stripped spruce round
        its floor (one every 7 blocks, 1.8 in from its rim: a fence crossbar on top with a lantern hanging from each
        end), every lane poles on its banks by turns (one every 9 blocks, an arm reaching out over the lane with a
        lantern); always there (not decorations). Every staircase (up out of a courtyard and the lodge's lane) ends on
        a **landing**: 2 rows of snow past its top step, 5 wide, solid snow 3 deep under it and nothing over it or its
        top steps 2 up (a snow layer or a plant of the world's), a lantern post beside it. The bone arches over the
        lanes stand at least 5 blocks from any stair or landing, their feet sunk 3 into the ground. A courtyard's own
        steps that ran into the lodge's lane are gone (the lane and its stairs are the way up there). Checked on every
        build (`villages.py`): every landing solid at the ground and clear over it, with and without the decorations;
        no courtyard, lane or step floor under light 1 (a last dark nook beside an igloo's wall is filled with snow).
      - **Fixes from the user's play test of October 23** [built, not yet tested in-game]:
        - **One model for the Wild Hunt** (the user: "the movement is still glitchy when he aims and moves ... make
          them one model then separate the models after the split"): while he rides it, the Alpha Moose wears one
          model of the two together, `wild_hunt` (`tools/rimefall/wildhunt_model.py`, run by `mobs.py` whenever it
          builds the Alpha Moose or the riding Huntsman: the moose's and the rider's bones in one file, the rider's
          renamed `r_...`, their textures side by side, 384 x 512; every animation of both, `animation.wild_hunt.*`),
          drawn with three animation controllers on the moose (`AlphaMoose.registerControllers`): its own animation,
          the rider's (the Huntsman's synced animation and its clock; his gallop on the moose's beat) and
          `carry_<the moose's animation>` (the saddle's motion, on the moose's own clock). So the rider can't lag,
          jitter or turn apart from the moose: they are one thing on screen. The Huntsman is still there (his hitbox,
          his shots, his fight) but isn't drawn while he's in the saddle (`HuntRenderer`); at the split he gets off,
          his own on-foot model appears, and the moose goes back to `alpha_moose` (`HuntAnims.WILD`, `HuntAnims.raw`,
          `AlphaMoose.isRidden`; `HuntsmanClientEvents` picks the moose's model).
        - **The split's leap timed with the rear** (the user: "the jump is too early, when the moose would neigh or
          whatever"): `ride_leap` is 1.4 s now (`huntsman_rider.ride_leap`): he rides the moose's rear up in the saddle
          (its `carry_rear`, fading out as he springs), springs off at 0.85 s, when it's well up on its hind legs
          (`LEAP_SPRING`), and lands at 1.26 s, 46 units out as before; the moose neighs at 0.7 s of its rear
          (`alpha_moose.REAR_NEIGH`, `HuntCues`; a placeholder, the wild moose's charge call pitched down, until the
          user picks sounds). The showcase plays the moose's rear with his leap, then its death after the leap.
        - **Life-size skulls** (above) and **his trophy corridor** (the user: "the Alpha Moose on the front of his
          door, then like a wall of Rimehorn and moose skulls inside his home"; their pick of three, a corridor): the
          lodge's way in (`igloo_designs/lodge_house.py`, `_trophy_corridor`) is a straight corridor 12 long, 7 wide
          and 6 tall inside (the hall's tunnel made long, wide and tall), behind a snow front with his doorway 3 wide
          and 4 tall, the **Alpha Moose's skull hung over it** and his banners either side; down its two walls six
          life-size skulls hang facing in, in three rows (4, 7 and 10 blocks in from the hall), a bull moose's (3 up,
          over your head) across from a Rimehorn's (2 up) and swapping each row; three lanterns hang down its middle.
          Outside, its roof steps up to a ridge, with ribs of packed ice every 3 blocks and snow lying between them.
          In the Huntsman's villages his lodge's box is 9 deeper for it (`LODGE_SIZE` 64 x 46) and the barn moved
          down with his door (`BARN_AT`), so his yard is as it was. (A trophy wall in the hall itself had room for only
          two life-size skulls between its curving walls, his seat and the furniture.)
        - **The spawn egg** redrawn (above).
        - Headless tests: `HuntsmanShowcaseGameTest` checks the `wild_hunt` files hold every animation of the moose and
          the rider and a `carry_` for each of the moose's.
      - **The user's second look of October 23** [built, not yet tested in-game]:
        - **The Wild Hunt's walking stutter** (the user: "the walking stutter with the hunt also looks weird"): the game
          sent the pair's positions every 3 ticks (vanilla's default), and a move of over a block was shown at once,
          not slid (`HuntMob.SNAP`, meant for his leap and backstep), so a galloping moose (about 1.3 blocks between
          updates) hopped. Now their positions are sent every tick (`updateInterval(1)` in `registry/ModEntities`) and
          only a move of over 2 blocks snaps (the leap and the backstep, about 3).
        - **The Alpha Moose's hitboxes** (the user: "increase his hitbox near his legs"): it's 6.7 blocks long and its
          hitbox a square 2.8 across round its middle, so its legs, chest, rump, head and neck stuck out of it. Three
          more hitboxes (`AlphaMoose.PART_SPOTS`, the wild moose's `RimefallBodyPart`, measured from its model): its
          chest and front legs (1.9 ahead) and its rump and hind legs (1.9 behind), each 2 wide from the ground to its
          back, and its head and neck (3.4 ahead, 1.6 wide, 2.7 to 5 up). A blow on one hurts it like any other (in the
          Wild Hunt off the shared bar). They count as the Huntsman's partner (`HuntFight.isPartner`), and what either
          of them shoots or throws flies through the other and its hitboxes (`HuntsmanFightEvents.onImpact`, Forge's
          `SKIP_ENTITY`: his arrows from the saddle would have hit the moose's head). The Huntsman's own legs were
          already inside his hitbox. Headless test: `HuntsmanFightGameTest.theMoosesLegsAreHitboxes`.
        - **No teeth on the skulls** (the user: "the teeth also look very bad"): the moose skulls' cheek teeth hung
          under their faces as a row of loose stubs and the Rimehorn's sat half out of its jaw; both are gone
          (`tools/huntsman/skulls.py`; real moose and rhinos have no front teeth, and the jaw now ends cleanly).
        - **Drawn spawn eggs for the three bosses** (the user: "a wolf-like hat on top with black under, then his blue
          eyes; do that for the Cinder Colossus and the Rat King; give me options I can pick from"): `tools/eggs/eggs.py`
          draws each option as pixel art on vanilla's own egg shape, shaded like vanilla's, and writes an option sheet
          per boss (`boss-previews/<biome>/<boss>/spawn_eggs/options.png`, each shown big and in an inventory slot).
          **The Cinder Colossus: C1, "Dormant shell"** (the user: "C1 is good for dormant shell": scorched stone and
          obsidian, lava glowing up one great crack from its crater; the others: C2 twin volcanoes, C3 three eyes,
          C4 lava core, C5 cracking). **The Rat King: R1, "Crowned rat"** with its crown centred (the user: "make the
          crown more centred": his rat face filling the egg, round pink ears, beady eyes, pink nose, whiskers and buck
          teeth, his gold crown with its green gem on top; the others: R2 crown and cape, R3 red eyes, R4 plague orb,
          R5 plague spots). Their item models are now `item/generated` on the drawn texture and their tints white
          (`DrossColors.COLOSSUS_EGG_*`, `RAT_KING_EGG_*`), like the Huntsman's. **The Huntsman**, five options
          with the wolf in his own hood's colours (the user: "make the wolf similar to the colour of the Huntsman's":
          dark slate fur with frost on its tips, `d06_huntsman`'s pelt): H1 the hood (ears, the wolf's eyes, muzzle and
          nose, black under it, his blue eyes), H2 with his white fangs, H3 with fangs and his bone antlers, H4 with
          fangs and his pale mantle round the bottom, H5 all of them; **the user picked H2** ("h2": the wolf hood in
          his colours, its two white fangs over the black, his blue eyes).
      - **The user's third look of October 23** (planned with the user first: "let's plan first"; their answers in
        brackets) [built, not yet tested in-game]:
        - **The arrow rain** ("it needs to spawn more than the visual arrows"; he shoots 12 up, four volleys of three:
          "48: 4 per arrow"): 48 come down in both stages (`RAIN_ARROWS`, `RAIN_ARROWS_STAGE2`; 15 and 10 before, which a
          player standing still was hit by about once in six rains), over 1.5 s (`RAIN_SPREAD` 30 ticks), crowded towards
          where you are (a spot's distance from the middle is the radius times a random 0-1 to the power 0.75,
          `RAIN_SPREAD_POWER`; standing still in it you take about two), still with no warning. **Then (October 24,
          2026) the arrows he looses are the ones that come down** (the user: "the hunter's arrows that he launches go
          one way and the arrows that actually hit you just spawn in ... make it so you see arrows go up from the
          hunter's bow ... no, don't make arrows spawn in, just make them aimed at you automatically"; and against Pond
          Skaters the rain didn't hit them): each of the 12 he looses up leaves his bow as a fan of 4 real frost arrows
          (`RAIN_PER_ARROW`; 48 in both stages), each on a high arc worked out with an arrow's own drag and gravity
          (`HuntFight.launch`) to a spot within 5 blocks of where his target will be (`RAIN_RADIUS`, `RAIN_SPREAD_POWER`
          0.8, led by its movement up to 6 blocks: `RAIN_LEAD_MAX`), each its own flight of 34-46 ticks (`RAIN_FLIGHT`),
          and as it comes down it steers towards its spot round where the target is then (`RAIN_STEER`, `HuntFight.steer`;
          its client told every tick). Measured (`HuntProbeGameTest.rainProbe`, from 14 blocks): a villager standing took
          3-4 of the 48, one walking across 6-8, a Pond Skater standing 10-16; every arrow starts within 1.5 blocks
          of his bow, none in the sky (a first try never steered: a new arrow first moves the tick after it's loosed, and
          seen unmoved it was taken for stuck in the ground). **Then (October 25, 2026) the user's own way** ("arrow
          leaves his bow, then they disappear at a certain height while still going up, then you spawn in the arrows
          above the target x blocks above, so it seems like those are his arrows"; the low arcs were "this sad little
          arrow thingy"): each of the 12 he looses shoots up fast (`RAIN_UP_SPEED`, a full draw) and vanishes 16 blocks
          over his bow, still climbing (`RAIN_VANISH_HEIGHT`, `HuntFight.vanishHigh`; on show too); for each, 4 frost
          arrows appear 22 blocks over spots round his target (`RAIN_HEIGHT`), 0.5-1 s after he looses it (`RAIN_DELAY`,
          `RAIN_DELAY_SPREAD`), a little back towards him (`RAIN_SLANT`), and streak down onto them in about 0.6 s
          (`RAIN_FALL_TICKS`), steering as before (`HuntFight.rainFor`, `dropArrow`); spread within 6 blocks
          (`RAIN_RADIUS`, `RAIN_SPREAD_POWER` 0.7), 3 a hit (`RAIN_BASE_DAMAGE` 1.3). Measured (`rainProbe`, from 14
          blocks): his arrows vanished 16-17 blocks up, the falling ones appeared about 23 over the target; a villager
          standing took 0-4 of the 48, one walking across 1-7, a Pond Skater 8-13. **Then (October 25, after playing
          it: "don't make the rain of arrows so far ... the arrows don't seem to hit me that often, make them do less
          damage but more of them hit me")**: they come down within 2.5 blocks of the target (`RAIN_RADIUS`, was 6;
          `RAIN_SPREAD_POWER` 0.65), each hit half a heart (`RAIN_HIT_DAMAGE` 1, was 3), and every one counts: vanilla's
          half second of not being hurt again after a hit no longer stops the next arrow of the rain (most of the rain
          had hit a player in that half second and done nothing), and they don't push their target about
          (`HuntsmanFightEvents`, `RAIN_TAG`). Measured: standing 5-11 of the 48, walking across 4-7, a Pond Skater
          19-22.
        - **The Wild Hunt's health**: 250 (`WILD_HUNT_HEALTH`; "increase the Wild Hunt's health by 50").
        - **Two attackers at once in the Wild Hunt** ("the hunter needs to be shooting or hitting me with a spear while
          the moose attacks"): one table ran both of them, and he sat still while it charged. Now each has its own timer.
          The moose's goal (`MooseGoal`, both stages) uses all its attacks in the Wild Hunt too ("yes, all its
          attacks"; `WILD_MOOSE_CHANCES`: close ram 30, toss 20, stomp 20, bellow 10, ride off 20; mid charge 30, circle
          35, stamp 15, bellow 10, ride off 10; far charge 40, circle 40, stamp 20; very far charge 40, ride in 60; the
          back kick at once when a foe is behind it; he rides along with each, his `carry_` animations). His own saddle
          goal (`SaddleGoal`, `SADDLE_CHANCES`, pause 12 ticks) uses what fits where his target is ("use what fits where
          you are": his bow aims only to his left, the moose's antlers being in front of him): close thrust 50 (in reach
          ahead or on his right), horn 25 (ahead), shoot 15, rain 10; mid shoot 40, bola 25 (ahead), rain 20, horn 15;
          far shoot 50, rain 30, bola 20; very far rain 70, shoot 30; "shoot" is two arrows from the saddle
          (`ride_shoot`, 2.5 hearts each) at a target 4-28 blocks off within 60 degrees of straight out to his left,
          1.5 s between (`SADDLE_SHOT_*`). The circling hunt is now the moose's alone (it gallops round you for 6 s,
          keeping you on his left, `CIRCLE_TICKS`); he shoots from it on his own timer. (The user's approved table of
          October 20 for stage 1 is kept above as a record.) Headless test:
          `HuntsmanFightGameTest.theyAttackAtOnce` (both in a move at the same moment).
        - **After the split** (the user: "the moose always tries to attack me while the hunter shoots me down, or if I
          get close to the hunter it backs off or spears me based on its percentages"): as it was, plus **a quick
          shot** on foot ("yes, he keeps shooting"): whenever all his bigger moves are cooling down and his target is
          6-32 blocks off in sight, one plain arrow (`quick_shot`, 1.9 s, loosed at 1.15; 2 hearts,
          `QUICK_SHOT_*`, 1 s between), so at range he keeps shooting rather than only circling.
        - **The angry survivor**: kept as it is ("keep it").
        - **The backstep shot without the teleport** (the user: "when he does the back shot the hunter teleports forward
          ... he should be just there, no teleporting, and ready to attack right away"): the model's root carried him 3
          blocks back and the game moved him there when it was over, so as it went back to standing the model jumped
          back again and slid forward to him. Now the model only rises and falls and the game moves him back along
          with the leap, eased between its `jump` and `lands` moments (`HuntMob.glide`; never into a wall), the
          planted spear kept where it stood in the snow; and it's 0.35 s shorter (2.4 s: the bow away while he's still
          in the air, the spear back as he lands).
        - **The Huntsman a tenth smaller** ("10% smaller"; the moose as it is): `tools/rimefall/rimemobs/hunter_rig.py`
          `SHRINK` 0.9 (his models' scale `K`, his joints, his IK; in the saddle shrunk round his seat, lifted
          `SEAT_LIFT`, so his hips stay on the saddle; on foot round his feet; the saddle's quiver his hands reach for at
          the moose's own scale, `MOOSE_K`; in the saddle his cloak's part on the saddle and the rump keeps the moose's

          landing crouch (`land`) at his size too.
        - **The skulls: bone copies of the real heads** ("the skull should be the exact size of the head with the
          horns"; their pick: "bone copy of the real head"): `tools/huntsman/skulls.py` builds each animal exactly as

          lifts the boxes of its head off unchanged, painted as bare bone (fur, face and muzzle bone; eyes, nostrils and
          mouth dark sockets; antlers antler bone, the Alpha's icicles, the Rimehorn's horns clear ice); its tack (the
          bridle and face plate) and its ears left off. Sizes (wide x tall x long, blocks): the Alpha Moose's 5.21 x
          2.39 x 2.21, a bull's 3.73 x 1.73 x 1.57, a Rimehorn's 1.23 x 2.94 x 2.18 (each its live head's, ears aside).
      - **The villages in Rimefall and the Huntsman at home** (the user, October 23, 2026: "add the villages to the
        Rimefall biomes ... make sure the hunter spawns in his village"; "the same logic: 3 Rimefalls with a guarantee
        of 1, and then the other 2 have a 50% chance of a regular non-hunter village"; "the village should not be on the

        [built, not yet tested in-game]:
        - **Where** (the boss-site planner, step 12; Dimension's `dimension/rimefall/RimefallVillages`): Rimefall now
          has a boss, so its groups of 3 patches (nearest the hub first, out forever) work like Blightwood's: in every
          group the seed picks, among the patches where one fits, the one with **the Huntsman's village**
          (`BossSites.HUNTSMAN_VILLAGE`, slot 0; one of his two designs), and each of the other two gets **a regular
          hunter village** half the time (`HUNTER_VILLAGE`, `BossSitesTuning.HUNTER_VILLAGE_CHANCE` 0.5; one of the
          nine regular designs). Each patch keeps its lake and herds (`RIMEFALL_AREA`) and has at most one village.
        - **The fit** (`RimefallVillages.plan`, from the noise alone, the same every time for a seed; numbers in
          `RimefallTuning`, "Hunter villages"): of the patch's deepest spots (`VILLAGE_TRIES` 16, 24 apart), the one
          whose ground under the footprint is flattest (cut and filled to its median height); the footprint and its
          16-block blend ring at least 6 blocks inside the patch (every 16-block cell it covers checked: sampling its edges alone once let a village reach out of its patch, October 23) and the shore's width plus 12 off the lake's edge
          (never on or by the big lake: another boss will live there); the ground under it may step at most 10 blocks
          from the median; its design and which way its way in faces are picked by the seed among those that fit (3
          designs tried). **Relaxed** (the planner's last try for a group's roomiest patch; a regular village's
          fallback too): 48 spots 12 apart, a step of up to 24, and only the village itself must be in the patch (its
          blend ring may run over the border). Herd spots keep 24 blocks off a village (`HERD_VILLAGE_CLEARANCE`).
        - **The Huntsman's spot comes before the lake** (his villages are about 200 x 240 and a Rimefall patch about
          325-370 across, so with the lake planned first half of the groups had no room for him): each patch keeps a
          place for his village first, from the land alone, and its lake goes round it (`RimefallAreas.plan`): the
          big lake round any of his places (the best first, at most 40, `VILLAGE_RESERVE_TRIES`), else the small one,
          else (no lake fits round any of them) the lake first and his village wherever it then fits. Measured over 8
          seeds and Rimefall's first 6 groups (`dimension/rimefall/VillageFitProbeGameTest`, in the `dross_speed`
          namespace: `-PgameTestNamespaces=dross_speed`): his village in 46 of 48 groups (17 relaxed; the other two
          groups' patches are all too small: the roomiest 114 and 94 blocks deep), a regular village in 26 of the 42
          kept patches whose roll came up, and lakes 100 big, 33 small, 11 none of 144 patches (106, 27 and 11 with
          no villages).
        - **Built with the land** (structures `dross:huntsman_village` and `dross:hunter_village`, Boss's
          `boss/huntsman/village/HunterVillageStructure` and `HunterVillagePiece`, placed by the planner's
          `dross:boss_site` placement, in the surface structures step): a chunk at a time, each part of it on its own
          ground since October 23, 2026 (the fourth look's part 3, above: its zones; until then the ground under the
          whole village was cut and filled to one level and blended back to the land over 16 blocks), each column
          keeping its own top blocks; then the village's template (its pens' animals with it) and its decorations, each
          kept with the village's own 1 in 2 roll (the same in every chunk). Rimefall's trees, ice spikes, boulders,
          ponds, Frostblooms and snow drifts keep 6 blocks off its zones and decorations (`NotOnRimefallLakeFilter`), and
          the world's tidy-up leaves it alone (`TidyUpFeature`).
        - **The Huntsman at home** (Boss's `boss/huntsman/village/HuntsmanVillages`, `HuntsmanVillageData`): when a
          player (not a spectator) comes within 160 blocks of his yard (`HunterVillageDesigns.Design.homeX`/`homeZ`:
          in front of his lodge) and its land is loaded with its entities, he and his Alpha Moose come there, him in
