          the fight starts when he spots a foe, as anywhere; a fight broken off far from home puts them back in the
          yard (`HOME_RETURN` 16). One pair per village (its UUID and the pair's number saved for the village, the
          village on both of them as `DrossHome`; an older pair that loads after a newer one came is removed). **Beaten
          (both dead, `/kill` too), never again** there; removed without dying (Peaceful) isn't beaten: the next player
          near finds a new pair. (The patrol 80 blocks out, the chase and the 7-minute comeback below aren't built:
          "home, simple".)
        - **Commands** (World site's `command/GotoVillage`): **`/dross goto lair huntsman`** the way into the nearest
          Huntsman's village (from you in the Dross, from the hub elsewhere), facing in, with a note (he's at home,
          beaten, or never on Peaceful); **`/dross goto village`** the nearest village of either kind
          (`goto village <x> <z>`: the one nearest that spot); **`/dross locate villages`**, a test tool for now (the
          user's "temporary command"): the 5 nearest villages in chat, their kind (the Huntsman's in antler amber,
          `DrossColors.HUNTSMAN_VILLAGE_LABEL`), where, how far and how many igloos, each with a [go] button.
        - **The hunters to pick from** (the user: "give me in a showcase like 10-12 villagers that can spawn, 2 blocks
          tall, that are similar to the Huntsman"): twelve designs, Nos. 10A-10L (`tools/rimefall/rimedesigns/
          d10_hunters.py`; previews `boss-previews/rimefall/hunters/`): 10A young tracker, 10B village elder, 10C
          trapper, 10D archer, 10E javelin thrower, 10F butcher, 10G tanner, 10H ice fisher, 10I snow scout, 10J
          shield-bearer, 10K horn-blower, 10L herder, each about 2-2.5 blocks tall, worn by the gallery's frozen
          statues (`RimefallDesigns.HUNTERS`, numbers 11-22; not in the gallery). **`/dross showcase hunters`**
          (`command/HunterShowcase`; replaced the next day by the Frostfolk's showcase, below): the twelve in two rows of six ahead of you on snow, the back row a step up, a
          packed ice post and a sign in front of each ("No. 10A", "Hunter", what he is); running it again replaces
          them. Looks only for now: the user picks, then they come to the villages.
        - **His trophy candidates** (the user: "give me 10-15 options ... like the trophy you get when you kill the
          bosses"): fifteen poses for a 1x1x1 trophy block like the other bosses', previews only for now
          (`tools/rimefall/hunt_trophies.py`; `boss-previews/rimefall/huntsman/trophies/candidates/`, `index.html`),
          each drawn in the outline of its block: 1 The Wild Hunt (at a gallop), 2 Riding Down (the spear thrust at a
          gallop), 3 Horn of the Hunt (riding), 4 Rain of Arrows (riding), 5 The Circling Shot, 6 The Bola, 7 The
          Rearing (the moose on its hind legs, him in the saddle), 8 Full Draw (on foot), 9 Leaping Strike, 10 The
          Javelin, 11 Spear Sweep, 12 The Hunter's Call (on foot), 13 Alpha's Bellow (the moose alone), 14 Antler Toss,
          15 The Hunter Hunted (both fallen). The user picked 2, 4 and 7, and asked for two more (below).
        - Headless tests: `boss/huntsman/village/HunterVillageWorldGameTest` (`hunterVillagesArePlanned`: Rimefall's
          first 4 groups, a Huntsman's design in the boss's patch and regular ones only on the others, inside the patch,
          off the lake and its shore, no herd spot in a village, the same planned again; `huntsmanVillageIsBuilt`: the
          nearest Huntsman's village generated, its template's packed ice and snow blocks in place, ground to stand on
          at its way in and in his yard; he comes home once in the saddle with his home, a new pair after he's removed,
          none once beaten) and `command/GotoVillageGameTest` (the list, `goto village`, its [go] button's command and
          `goto lair huntsman`).
      - **The user's fourth look of October 23, part 1: the fight and the skulls** (the to-do list made with the user, then
        "go") [built, not yet tested in-game]:
        - **His arrows hit now** (the user: his bow "doesn't hit when he rides and tries to shoot me"): measured with a
          probe (`boss/huntsman/HuntProbeGameTest`, namespace `dross_probe`: him in the saddle on a floor in the sky, a
          villager that can't die as the target), a still target was hit at 6, 12 and 20 blocks, one running across his
          line at a sprint (0.28 blocks a tick) 0 of 6 at 12 and 20: the server never knows a player's speed, so he aimed
          where it had been. Now every Huntsman remembers his target's last 5 spots and leads his shots from them
          (`HuntMob.motionOf`, `HuntFight.aimAt` with the shooter, worked out twice for the time to the spot); the bola
          and his thrown spear too. 36 of 36 hit after. **Every arrow of his frosts you** now (his saddle shots, the
          quick shot and the backstep's had none): Slowness and frozen ticks, the aimed shot's freeze as before.
        - **A shot for each direction from the saddle** (the user: "make a shot animation for each major cardinal direction,
          that way the moose doesn't have to move in the direction perpendicular to you"): `ride_shoot` (his left) and
          new `ride_shoot_front` (past the moose's head on its left, the bow held high while he nocks), `ride_shoot_right`
          (twisted about 80 degrees round to his right) and `ride_shoot_back` (over his left shoulder, a Parthian shot),
          two arrows each (`tools/rimefall/rimemobs/huntsman_rider.py`, `SHOT_DIRECTIONS`); the saddle goal picks the one
          for where his target is (ahead within 45 degrees of the moose's front, behind past 135, else left or right:
          `HuntsmanTuning.SADDLE_SHOT_FRONT`/`_BACK`). Their check: nothing of his bow into the moose; the hand's sweep to
          the quiver behind his right leg crosses his chest for one frame in the back shot (2.2 units), like the horn's.
        - **Close up, his spear; running away, his bola** (the user: "when I am right next to his moose he should use his
          spear more often ... if I am running away he should use his bola to slow me down"): the saddle's close row is
          thrust 70, horn 15, shoot 15 (was thrust 50, horn 25, shoot 15, rain 10); a target running away from him at
          0.12 blocks a tick or more within the bola's reach gets it at once when it's ready (`SaddleGoal.react`,
          `BOLA_FLEEING_SPEED`), and the bola now reaches 120 degrees round from the moose's front. Stage 2 stays ranged.
        - **The cracking stamp's spikes of clear ice** (the user: "use that clear ice we created for some of the models ...
          smaller spikes on the ground clumped up, then the main [one] a giant [spike] around 1.5 blocks tall that slows
          you down and knocks you into the air"): `tools/huntsman/ice_spike.py` makes one spike of the models' see-through
          ice (the Rimehorn's horns' material: a column tapering to its point, three shards leaning off its foot; block
          `dross:hunt_ice_spike`, only shown as block displays, no item); where the cracks end 7 small ones burst up
          (0.35-0.75 of a block, leaning out, growing over 3 ticks), and 7 ticks later the big one, 1.5 blocks tall,
          grows up under you over 4 ticks: 10 damage, thrown up hard (`STAMP_LAUNCH` 0.95, was 0.6), Slowness II for 3 s
          and frosted (`HuntFight.spikes`, `bigSpike`; numbers `STAMP_*`). Its picked sound (version 1) has both.
        - **He hunts** Pond Skaters, grown Rimehorns and grown moose (the user: "not the children"; never a hunter
          village's penned ones: `HuntFight.isFoe`); players still come first.
        - **He patrols his village** (the user: "the Huntsman should be patrolling the village ... when he comes into view
          he should already be moving around"; their pick: a loop just outside the village's lights): a rounded ring 20
          blocks outside the village's footprint (`HuntsmanVillages.patrolLoop`: 24 points on a superellipse of power 6),
          the calm moose riding it point after point at 0.7 of its speed, skipping a point it can't reach
          (`PatrolGoal`; the loop saved on the moose as `Patrol`); he comes at its loaded point furthest from the nearest
          player, already riding on; after a fight broken off far from home they go home and take the loop up again from
          its nearest point. (The wider 80-block loop and the chase below aren't built.)
        - **The skulls lie level** (the user: "they sit weird on poles"): the bone copies kept the animals' heads' tilt
          (23-24 degrees nose-down); standing, each skull is turned so its head line (its biggest box's long axis) lies
          flat, resting on its jaw, and centred on the skull itself (not its antlers or horns) so it sits balanced on a
          post; on a wall it hangs as before. Its **hitbox** fits its skull (the box round its bone, as much as lies in its
          block, made with the models: the generated `boss/huntsman/skull/HuntSkullShapes`, turned to its facing).
        - Headless tests (`HuntsmanFightGameTest`): `thePatrolGoesRound`, `heHuntsGrownAnimals`; the keeper's test accepts
          him on his patrol.
      - **The user's fourth look of October 23, part 2: his trophies** [built, not yet tested in-game] (the user's picks of
        the fifteen candidates: "I like 4, 2, 7; make 7 but make a 7 that is the same but him shooting his bow; also
        make one where the moose is doing its ice slam attack and the Huntsman is ... jumping off its back shooting his
        arrow"; Boss area, `boss/huntsman/trophy/`, copying the Rat King's, step 9): `dross:huntsman_trophy`, a 1x1x1
        decorative block, a miniature of the Wild Hunt (their real models and textures, no base), about a ninth of life
        size (`MAX_SCALE` 0.115 in `tools/huntsman/trophies.py`, so the moose is about one size in all five). Five designs
        (a `design` block state, 1-5), each turned on its block so a player standing in front of the placed trophy sees
        its best side: **1 Riding Down** (candidate 2: his spear driven down past the antlers at a gallop; seen three
        quarters from the spear's side), **2 Rain of Arrows** (candidate 4: his bow drawn high, three arrows on the
        string; from his bow's side), **3 The Rearing** (candidate 7: the Alpha Moose up on its hind legs, him in the
        saddle; side-on, his right), **4 The Rearing Shot** (new: the Alpha Moose rearing, its front hooves up and its neck stretched
        out, him turned in the saddle with his bow drawn level back over its rump, side-on from its right; since October
        24, 2026, the user, with three pictures of riders on rearing horses: "the moose should neigh, but he should aim
        his bow parallel to the ground"; it was him loosing into the sky; a level shot ahead runs into the moose's neck
        and palm antlers however it's turned; its own draw, `shot` in `tools/huntsman/trophies.py`, aimed up in the saddle
        as far as the rearing tips him back), **5 The Parting Shot** (new: the moose's cracking stamp, a clump
        of the clear-ice spikes bursting up in front of it (the big one and six small, as the fight's), while he leaps
        off its back: him on foot, mid-air beside and above its rump, leaning back, his bow drawn forward, from his
        backstep shot). Made by `tools/huntsman/trophies.py` (held GeckoLib animations
        `animation.huntsman_trophy.design_<n>`; one model of the moose's, the rider's and his on foot's bones side by
        side under one bone that turns and centres the figure, and the spikes, `geo/block/` and `geo/item/
        huntsman_trophy.geo.json`; their textures side by side, 512 x 512, drawn like the boss: cut out, the glow
        full-bright, the clear ice blended, its glow; the generated `HuntsmanTrophyModels`; previews
        `boss-previews/rimefall/huntsman/trophies/`, `index.html`). Each design's spear, bow, arrows and body are checked
        against the moose (in the saddle only what sits there at rest too: his spear's butt in its cup 1.1 units, his
        chest against the cantle 0.4-1.5) and the spikes against both. Exactly the other trophies' rules: placed on the
        floor facing the placer, any tool or a bare hand breaks it and it drops itself with its design (pick-block too),
        never burns, blast resistance 1200; the item shows the 3D figure, is fireproof, never despawns, its tooltip
        "No. N: name" and the gray hint; **right-click** turns it to the next pose (1 > 2 > 3 > 4 > 5 > 1; main hand,
        sneak with an item to skip, not in adventure mode) with a click, a crunch of snow and a puff of frost. Now and
        then the moose's frosty breath at its muzzle, and in No. 5 snowflakes off the ice. **The drop**: with "The Hunter
        Hunted" and for the same players (the player credited with the last kill and every non-spectator within 40
        blocks), each gets their own **No. 1, Riding Down** (`HuntsmanTuning.TROPHY_DROP_DESIGN`), straight into their
        inventory (a full inventory or a dead player: the shared `TrophyRewards`); never for `/kill`. Dross tab: the
        five at the end of the trophies group (after the mounted skulls). `/dross showcase trophies huntsman`
        (`command/TrophyShowcase`) places the five in a row on pedestals with signs. Headless tests
        (`boss/huntsman/HuntsmanTrophyGameTest`): the cycle, the break drop and pick-block, a trophy for the killer and
        a player near but not one 60 blocks off, none for `/kill`, the showcase row.
      - **The user's fourth look of October 23, part 3: the villages on the land** [built, not yet tested in-game] (the
        user: "I don't like the way you just destroy the biome around it to make it flattened out ... it should be like
        an actual village, the same way the houses spawn in the village; if there are hills or trees that don't
        interfere with the spawning of the village then that's fine"; their pick: each courtyard on its own ground):
        - **Each part on its own ground** (`boss/huntsman/village/HunterVillageZones`, `HunterTerrace`,
          `HunterVillagePiece`; the zones made with the templates by `tools/huntsman/village_designs/warren.py`
          `_zones`, written by `villages.py` as `data/dross/hunter_village_zones/<id>.json`): every column of a village
          belongs to a zone or to none (the world's own land, left as it is: its trees and hills between the parts
          stay). A zone is flat at its own level, the median of the land under it (from the noise, when the village is
          planned: `RimefallVillages`, so every chunk agrees): a courtyard with its ring of igloos, the warren igloos
          behind them and their passages; the lodge with its yard and barn; each pen; the shrine; each Rimeberry Patch;
          each light of the ring; each decoration on the open ground (its box). A lane slopes between the two zones it
          joins, a block at a time, with a spruce stair at each step (its two courtyards are pulled together until it
          can climb a block every 3 blocks, `HunterVillageZones.LANE_RUN`); a passage between two courtyards' igloos
          slopes the same way, a block a block. A lane igloo, a lamp on a lane's bank and a decoration in a lane take
          the lane's level where they stand; a path over the snow and the shrine's lantern posts follow the land column
          by column. Two maps, the blocks at the ground and under it and those over it, so a lamp's arm over a lane
          rides with its lamp. The ground under a zone is cut and filled to its level (a spot whose ground is more than
          6 blocks off a zone's level doesn't fit: `VILLAGE_ZONE_MAX_STEP`, 12 relaxed); round it the land is eased back
          a block a step, out to 8 blocks and only where it's that far off (`VILLAGE_SOFT_EDGE`; it was a 16-block ring
          blended round the whole flattened village). Rimefall's trees and boulders keep 6 blocks off the zones and the
          decorations' boxes, not off the whole village (`VillagePlan.keepsClear`). No drifts of snow over the open
          ground any more (it's the world's own). The Huntsman's home and the way in stand at their zones' levels.
        - **No packed ice in the floors** (the user: "weird ice squares on the bottom of the villages ... I don't like
          the ice squares"): the courtyards', lanes' and walls' linings (`sunken_warren.py` `LINING`, `LANE_FLOOR`,
          `COURT_FLOOR`) and the paths and plazas (`villagekit/props.py` `PATH`, `PLAZA`) are snow, Hoarfrost and gravel
          only (the troughs' rims and the doorways' frames, built of it on purpose, keep it).
        - **The barn's corral** (the user: "replace them with regular skulls, no need to put moose skulls on the bone
          fence"): vanilla skeleton skulls on its bone posts, facing out (`igloo_designs/lodge_house.py`).
        - **None of Rimefall's animals spawn by themselves in a village** (the user: "within the light perimeter no
          Rimehorns or skaters or mooses should spawn besides the ones within the fences"): moose and Pond Skaters
          never spawn naturally on a village's footprint or its soft edge (`RimefallAnimals.inHunterVillage`; spawn
          eggs and commands still work); the herds' spots keep off villages as before.
        - **Only the penned are tame** (the user: "a herd of Rimehorns that spawned in the village were passive; that is
          only for the Rimehorns that spawn within the villages' fences or the ones you bred"): each pen animal carries
          its pen's fence line in its own frame (`PenFrame`, written by `warren._pen_frame`); turned with the village,
          its facing turns with it, so on its first tick it finds the fence line again (`PenBox`; `boss/rimefall/Pen`),
          and once it's outside it, it's a wild animal for good; a calf born in the pen has its mother's.
        - Headless tests: `RimefallAnimalsGameTest.penAnimalsTameOnlyInTheirPen`; the planning test checks every
          village has its zones' levels and that no animal spawns by itself in it; the built village's test checks its
          packed ice and snow blocks at their zones' levels.
      - **The user's fourth look of October 23, part 4: the Frostfolk** [built, on show, from their spawn eggs and in
        the villages (October 24); their look remade on October 24; not yet tested in-game] (the user: "as soon as you
        kill one of them the entire village turns on you ... they remain hostile for, let's say, 10 minutes; they have
        25-30 [health]; a spear and circular shield one, an axe one, a long spear one, a bow and arrow one, then a horn
        dude with the flag ... make them interactable, a unique voice that is nice ... around 10-12 different classes,
        each its own spawn egg; these guys are all hostile to [Pond Skaters] and [they] will attack these guys ... the
        basic dudes, not the warriors, do 5; also add an elder who has scarred eyes and a beard and is the one with a
        sword; this guy has 50 health; if you attack him you can leave and it resets his anger after 10 minutes; he will
        be the one you can talk to"; their pick: five workers). **Their name** (October 24): the **Frostfolk**, never
        hunters or anything of the Huntsman's (the user: "DON'T CALL THEM HUNTERS").
        - **The model** (`tools/rimefall/rimemobs/frostfolk.py`, built by `mobs.py frostfolk`; October 24, 2026, the
          user: the first, the Huntsman's model at half size, "suck": "more like regular Minecraft villagers ... same
          build as a pillager ... blue hoods, different styles of hoods, maybe a rhino horn hood for the farmer ... capes
          and stuff, the wolf stuff ... as detailed as the Huntsman, no armour plates, just basic clothing"; then "they
          should not have human faces because of their hoods: their face should be black with blue eyes, their skin
          should not be seen; the elder should have a white scar and white beard, kind of like the Huntsman's face"): a
          pillager's build (about 2.1 blocks to the top of a hood), no face under the hood but darkness and two glowing
          blue eyes, gloved hands, a tunic belted over trousers, fur-cuffed boots and sleeves, a capelet; its texture at
          twice vanilla's detail (`DENSITY` 2), one texture per calling (its hood's blue, its clothes) and its glow (the
          eyes, `<calling>_glow.png`). Each calling's hood style, cape and tools on bones of their own, the others
          hidden (`FolkRole.shows`, `client/FolkRenderer`): **spearman** a peaked hood, a wolf-pelt cape, a spear and a
          round hide shield; **axeman** a thick fur-trimmed hood, a wolf pelt, an axe of ice; **pikeman** a tall pointed
          hood, a long spear; **archer** a deep cowl with a drape, his bow gripped through the middle of his left fist
          and carried level at his side (its front limb a little up ahead, its string under it), and on his back a quiver:
          one straight brown box leaning its mouth over his right shoulder, four small ice fletchings standing out of its
          top in two staggered rows, a strap across his chest (the user, October 25, 2026, after seeing it in the game:
          "just make a rectangle ... make it browner ... at the top have 3-4 small icy rectangles", and "the tips where
          the arrows' features are aren't aligned with it": its pieces were laid out along a line leaning the other way
          from the box, a Bedrock turn flips x, and the z-fighting fix had swollen the arrows' little shaft stubs into a
          lump; now every size is a whole unit and no two faces share a plane; before that the same day a black box with a
          rim of ice, "he should hold the handle, not the string": the bow hung off the outer side of his fist; the user,
          October 24: "the archer's quiver looks weird", then a brown tube with bands; for a while the bow was slung on
          his back); **horn-blower** a hood with a wolf's
          ears, a spear, a horn at his belt, the village's flag; **farmer** a Rimehorn's hide and horns over his hood, a
          hoe, a basket of Frostroots; **butcher** a close hood, a cleaver, a leather apron; **herder** fur ear flaps and
          a bobble, a crook; **fisher** an oilskin hood with a wide brim, a rod, a fish; **tanner** a fringed capelet, a
          rolled hide, nothing in his hands (his scraper gone: the user, October 24, 2026, "don't make the tanner hold
          any items"); **the elder** a long hood with its tail down his back, a long blue cape, a white scar
          down over his right eye (that eye gone under it: one blue eye), a long white beard, a sword of ice in its
          sheath across his back, its hilt over his right shoulder (one straight line since October 25, 2026: its pieces
          were turned the other way from the line they were laid along, like the archer's quiver, and its tip floated). **How they hold their tools** (the user, October 24,
          2026, twice: the tools "are not properly held", then "the items go through the arms when they should be in
          their hands"): each calling carries its tool one of three ways (`STYLE` in the generator, `FolkAnims.Carry` in
          the game), each with its own idle, walk, run, talk and chop: **pole** (the spears, the pike, the crook, the hoe,
          the rod) stood upright out of the front of its fist, its arm forward 40 degrees and its fist turned back as
          far; **hang** (the axe, the cleaver) held out ahead of the fist at right angles to the arm, the way Minecraft
          holds a tool, swinging with the arm; **free** (the archer, the tanner; the elder, whose chop reaches over his
          shoulder and draws his sword, which goes back on his back after). The fist grips a tool 1.5 pixels towards
          the side that part of it leaves from (`GRIP`), so a shaft never runs up the sleeve; the spear thrusts are
          underhand, the spear levelled under the raised forearm. The archer's bow is level in his fist (upright it rose
          up through his sleeve, `BOW_LEVEL`); to shoot he raises it upright, slid first to the outer side of his fist so
          neither it nor its string comes through his sleeve as it turns (`BOW_SHOT_OUT`; on the inner side it went into
          his chest). Checked on every build: no tool into a sleeve at all in any of its calling's animations (the
          sleeves on bones of their own, `CLIP_STRICT`; before, only the body was checked, and a tool sat inside the arm
          at rest). The fisher's fish hangs on his hook at the end of his line; the butcher's cleaver bigger, with a
          bright steel blade. 144 boxes, about 40 drawn per calling, cut out plus its head drawn again for the glowing eyes
          (`boss/client/PartLayer`) (the first model was 470, drawn four times: the user saw the Huntsman's
          village lag with about 85 of them). Animations keyed by hand, blocky like Minecraft's villagers: idle, walk,
          run, talk and chop for each way of carrying (`idle`, `idle_pole`, `idle_hang`...; `chop_sword` the elder's),
          spear_combo, thrust_sweep, quick_shot, aimed_shot, horn_blow; they die the vanilla way (tip
          over, flash red; the user: "just the regular death animation"). Checked on every build: each calling's tools
          never into its body or its sleeves in any of its moves, nothing z-fighting. Previews: `boss-previews/rimefall/frostfolk/`
          (`frostfolk.png`, one card per calling, `index.html`; `tools/huntsman/folk_preview.py`).
        - **Eleven callings** (`boss/frostfolk/FolkRole`), each its own entity type `dross:frostfolk_<calling>` (0.6 x
          2.0); **three spawn eggs** (the user, October 24, 2026: "just one for the elder, one for warriors and one for
          workers"): "Frostfolk Warrior / Worker / Elder Spawn Egg" (`FrostfolkSpawnEggItem`: each use a random calling of
          its kind; their hoods' blue, the kind's spots: `DrossColors.FROSTFOLK_WARRIOR|WORKER|ELDER_EGG_SPOTS`), in health
          / damage / armour: warriors **spearman** 30 / 4 / 4 (thrust, thrust, sweep, the Huntsman's combo), **axeman**
          28 / 6 / 3 (a chop), **pikeman** 28 / 5 / 3 (the thrust and sweep, reaching 3.6), **archer** 25 / 3 / 2 (real
          arrows of 2.5 from 7-22 blocks, keeping off; a punch up close), **horn-blower** 26 / 4 / 3 (the thrust and
          sweep; when his village turns on a player, his horn: Speed I and Strength I for 30 s to every villager within
          24); workers **farmer**, **butcher**, **herder**, **fisher**, **tanner** 20 health, no armour, a
          chop with their tool for 2-4; **the elder** 50 / 7 / 4 (his sword). Speeds 0.28 / 0.25 / 0.22; they walk and
          run at their real pace. Every number in `FolkTuning`.
        - **How they act** (`Frostfolk`): calm, they wander, look at you and hum now and then; right-click one and it
          talks (a line in chat, `<Frostfolk Elder> ...`, its talk animation and voice; `dross.frostfolk.<calling>.line.<n>`,
          how many each has in `FolkTuning`: the spearman and pikeman 2, the axeman and fisher 4, the elder 7, the
          rest 3 (the archer's last two the user's picks of eight); the user's edits of October 24, 2026: the elder's cabin that moves round the lake every winter, the
          fisher's lit house on the lake), but not while it's angry at you. **Hit one** and it fights you back until
          you're 16 blocks from it (`HIT_LEAVE_RANGE`); **kill one and every villager within 96 blocks rushes you**, three
          of them shouting and **every horn-blower sounding his horn**, until you've left their village (80 blocks from
          where each one lives: `VILLAGE_LEAVE_RANGE`; saved) (the user, October 24, 2026: "if you hurt a villager, that
          villager will hit you back, but if you leave the area, a 10-20 block radius, then he'll stop; if you kill a
          villager, all the horns will blow and all the villagers will start rushing you; you'll have to leave the
          village"; until then 30 s for a hit and 10 minutes for a killing). **The elder** stays angry at whoever hit
          him until they've been 48 blocks off for 10 minutes (`ELDER_NEAR`, `ELDER_FORGIVE_TICKS`; saved). They never hurt each
          other (no friendly fire), **hunt Pond Skaters, and Pond Skaters hunt them** (`PondSkater`'s targets); hurt by
          one, it fights back wherever the skater stands (Minecraft's own rule had a village's Frostfolk ignore anything
          outside its 24-block home) and calls the others within 16 blocks who aren't fighting (`HELP_RANGE`). They're
          drawn only within 40 blocks (`RENDER_DISTANCE`, times the video settings' entity distance). They never despawn;
          they die the vanilla way.

      - **Where he is** (the user's answers): he spawns outside his village riding the Alpha Moose and **patrols a loop about 80 blocks out** round it; the fight starts wherever he finds you. **Running away**: he chases about 60 blocks past his loop, then stops and rains arrows on you while you're in range (about 40 blocks); once you're out of range or out of Rimefall, the fight is broken off: he (and the moose, whichever are alive) ride off and vanish, and **7 minutes later they come back together at full health**, the dead one too (the user: "you can't kill one of them, come back all healed up and kill the other"; 5-10 minutes, the pick left to me). Beaten (both dead), he never comes back, like the other bosses. **Then (October 24, 2026), after playing it: no vanishing** (the user: "don't add the thing where he disappears ... make it so he is still there, just that if you are gone for 3+ minutes then he goes back to full health; I don't want him to disappear for 7 minutes if I happen to leave, then can't fight him again"): see "The chase as built".

          stands and uses the rest (his shots, the arrow rain, the bola, the horn; the moose's blows up close), falling
          back still allowed. **Got away** (`HuntFight.gotAway`): a player outside the loop and further than `HOLD_RANGE`
          (40) off, or out of Rimefall, is let go: they never pick them (`chooseTarget`) and, with nobody else to fight,
          head home (`headHome`: back to his yard if over `HOME_RETURN` blocks off), still hurt, and take up their

          first the same day, then removed at the user's word: they rode off, vanished and came back 7 minutes later.)

Igloos by kind: sleeping (beds and fur bedrolls along the walls, at least one bed for every two igloos), cook (a fire under a smoke hole, a stew pot, a smoker), workshop (a fletching table, a grindstone, spears), trophy (skulls), storage (barrels, hay); every chest and table against a wall with the room in front of it open, never in a doorway or a passage's mouth. **Decorations** (100-190 a village, each its own group, kept by the game with a 1 in 2 chance: `HunterDecorFilter`): racks of drying meat (Meat Blocks), hides on frames, sleds with cargo, woodpiles, barrels, lean-tos with icicles, fishing holes, totems, lantern posts, banners, skull and antler posts, inuksuks, bone piles, heaps of skulls, butcher's blocks, Frostbloom patches.
