Beds stand singly or in pairs either side of a nightstand (a 2-wide double can't be centred in an odd room). Fires get a smoke vent through the roof with a collar of packed ice. **17 designs**: 6 homes with their stories (**The Trapper's Hut**: a loner's bed, snares, a fur rack; **The Family Igloo**: the parents' beds either side of a nightstand, the children's along the wall by their chest, a kitchen corner; **The Elder's Igloo**: bookshelves, a lectern under a blue lamp, skulls of old hunts; **The Twin Hunters**: everything in mirrored pairs; **The Scout's Igloo**: a wide window of clear ice at the back, maps; **The Apprentice's Igloo**: targets against a hay bale, a fletching table), the work igloos (**The Storehouse**: barrels two high, chests, hung meat, packed ice; **The Cookhouse**: the fire under its vent, smokers, stew pots, meat smoking round the fire; **The Barracks**: three domes, 12 beds in two rows with candle nightstands, the spear alcove; **The Workshop**: fletching table, grindstone, loom, anvil, a work table under its lamp), two meeting halls with no beds (**The Round Hall**: the fire in the middle with benches round it, his banners and the hall's trophy at the back, skulls on posts, lanterns by the way in; **The Long Hall**: three domes, a long table and benches, a fire in each end dome), three extras of mine (**The Ice-Fishing Hut**: a hole of open water in the floor; **The Tannery**: soaking vats, hides on the walls; **The Snow Angel's Shrine**: a statue of the Ice Necromancer of snow and ice, wings of blue ice, a blue lamp for a halo, an altar), and **the Huntsman's lodge** (his great dome, its way in 3 wide and 4 tall under his moose skull with his banners outside, his bedroom dome on the west with a chest, the armoury on the east, his seat at the back facing the door with its arms full blocks and his antlers mounted inside the room (they used to replace wall blocks: "holes in the house where the fences are"), the fire and benches, his table of roasted meat and his smoker, 2 chests and a couple of barrels in the hall) and **the Alpha Moose's barn** (plank floors, no dirt, lanterns at the back over the feed barrels and on the stall's fence, the beam lanterns hung low on chains, a chest downstairs and one in the hay loft, its corral beside it with bone posts whose skulls face out). **56 decorations** (`igloo_designs/decor_catalog.py`, `decor_new.py`): the villages' 41 (the sleds now a plank deck with trapdoor runners and a stair prow, the kayak's hull top slabs, the ice blocks on the ground, the smoking rack's stones full blocks: nothing on a bottom slab), 7 lights for a ring round a village (antler lamp, stone brazier, snow lantern, bone lamp, lantern arch, ice pillar, skull lamp) and 8 new ideas (an igloo going up, a trophy board, a training dummy, an ice well, a Snow Angel statue, a cairn cache, a hide tent, palm antlers). Templates `data/dross/structures/hunter_igloo/<id>.nbt` (the ground at layer 1, the doorway at the south) and `hunter_decor/<id>.nbt`; the generated table `boss/huntsman/village/HunterIglooDesigns` (each igloo's size, doorway and darkest floor cell; each decoration's size and group); placed by `boss/huntsman/village/HunterIgloos`. **`/dross showcase hunter_igloos`** (`command/HunterIglooShowcase`): rows ahead of you, every plot facing you with a numbered sign (igloos "No. N", decorations "D N"): the homes; the work igloos and halls; the extras, the lodge and the barn; then the decorations, 10 to a row; one plot a tick; [go] buttons to each doorway and an [overview]. Previews `boss-previews/rimefall/huntsman/igloos/` (`<id>.png`: outside, inside with the roof cut, from above; `decorations.png`; `all_igloos.png`; `index.html`). Headless tests (`command/HunterIglooShowcaseGameTest`): every template at its table's size; the whole showcase built: beds whole, chests open, nothing on a bottom slab, nothing melting, every igloo's darkest floor cell lit. **Next** (step 2): done October 20, see "Step 2: the villages built from the hand-designed igloos" in the hunter villages above.
    - **Round 2 of the igloos, the crops and the farms** [built October 20, from the user's look in-game the same day; not yet looked at in-game] (the user kept every igloo and every decoration: "the rest of the decorations is good"; "I don't mind the extra igloos and stuff"):
      - **Skulls face the right way**: every skull was backwards (the user: "the skulls ... are facing [the wrong] way", the lodge's too): Minecraft turns a standing skull by the placer's own yaw and a banner by it turned round, so the same rotation number faces opposite ways; skulls now use their own table (`villagekit/local.py` `SKULL_ROT16`, `{skull_in}`, `{skull_front}`...). Inside they face into the room, the lodge's and the corral's out. (The old village showcase keeps its backwards skulls until it's rebuilt in step 2.)
      - **Guards**: armor stands in a skeleton's skull, glacier-blue leather (`plan.GUARD_BLUE` 0x3A6FB0) and a stone sword: two in the Barracks in place of its two spears, four in the Long Hall in place of its skull posts, facing the table (the user: "skulls face inwards with leather armor that is blue maybe with stone sword"). Templates now carry entities (`plan.Igloo(stands=...)`, `villagekit/writer.armor_stand`); `HunterIgloos.place` puts them in through `VillageAnimals`, and a showcase plot built again loses the stands of the one before.
      - **2 x 2 doorways** on the Round Hall, the Long Hall and the shrine (the user's; a tunnel can now be an even number wide: `Tunnel(extra=1)`, its second column to the doorway's west, so they sit half a block off the halls' middle).
      - **The Snow Angel's Shrine** is three domes joined (the user: "the shrine house 3 igloos ... should not be connected to any other igloo to show their importance"): the statue, the altar and a light-blue aisle in the great middle dome, the Hall of the Fallen on the west (bookshelves, a lectern, skulls), the Offerings Chapel on the east (barrels, a chest, meat, candles on bones); its own kind, `shrine`. In a village it will stand alone (step 2).
      - **The Huntsman's triple bed** (three beds side by side, a lantern on a barrel at each end; the user: "the hunter should just have triple bed instead of the double bed stuff with something between").
      - **The barn's lanterns** hang from the middle of three beams on two links of chain (the user: "not have such long chains and be centered on the middle").
      - **Placeholders noted in the files**: the antler post, antler gate, palm antlers, antler lamp, the moose heads over the lodge's and barn's doors and the Rimehorn skull are blocks until the user's own moose-skull model (the user: "they will be replaced with a real model of the moose skull that I will create"), and every lightning rod is a spear until a real spear model and item exist ("keep the rods as is"); `# PLACEHOLDER` comments marked each one (all replaced on October 22, 2026: the mounted skulls and the Planted Moonsilver Spear: stage 3, in the fight's notes above).
      - **Rimefall's crops** (`boss/huntsman/village/HunterCrops`; the user: "make it a custom plant for the biome, think of a couple ... a berry farm outside ... another crop with its own igloo"; Snow Lichen over kale; "don't worry about food": none can be eaten yet): **Frostroot** (`dross:frostroots`, planted with the item `dross:frostroot`: pale ice-blue roots under spiky frosted leaves) and **Snow Lichen** (`dross:snow_lichen`, planted with its clumps: a pale crust branching up like coral), crops on farmland that need light 9 to grow but never pop off in the dark (`HardyCropBlock`: a template puts them down before the game lights their lanterns); the **Rimeberry Bush** (`dross:rimeberry_bush`, planted with `dross:rimeberries`: the user, "2 blocks tall and have berries collectable for both blocks"): a sprout, then two blocks tall, one age in both halves, ripe from age 2, picked from either half (both halves' berries, 4-6 when fully ripe, then back to age 1), broken as one, no thorns, on dirt (Hoarfrost too), farmland or snow (`RimeberryBushBlock`); the **Frostroot Crate** and **Rimeberry Basket** (nine into a block and back: the user's "compacted root ... for decorations ... alongside the meat"). They compost; Rimeberries breed Rimehorns and Snow Lichen breeds moose (above). Textures by `tools/huntsman/crops.py` (preview `boss-previews/rimefall/huntsman/igloos/crops.png`). Creative tab: the crate and basket at the end of the blocks, the three in a new last group, crops.
      - **The farms** (`igloo_designs/farms.py`, No. 18-20; ages mixed per cell so the rows look grown): **18 The Root House** (two domes: four long beds of Frostroot either side of a water channel, three lanterns hung over it, crates, compost bins, barrels), **19 The Lichen House** (one dome: two rings of Snow Lichen round a pool, a lantern over each corner of the walk), **20 The Rimeberry Patch** (outdoors: three hedges of two-block bushes on Hoarfrost, windbreaks round three sides (the user: "for the windbreaks I kind of like it, make sure to include those a lot"), lantern posts, the pickers' baskets and barrels). Under a roof every crop is lit 9 or more (`plan.CROP_LIGHT`, checked). Crates and baskets also stand in the Storehouse and the Cookhouse, and two new decorations hold them (D57 Harvest sled, D58 Harvest heap).
      - **How often each igloo comes up in a village** (the user approved it October 20; built in step 2): a regular village (15-20 igloos) always has 1 meeting hall (the Round 60% / the Long 40%), 1 Cookhouse and 1 Storehouse; the shrine in 1 village of 3, standing alone; 1-2 Rimeberry Patches; every other igloo rolled: homes 55 (Family 14, Trapper 9, Twin Hunters 9, Scout 8, Apprentice 8, Elder 7 (only one)), work 22 (Workshop 7, Barracks 6 (only one), Tannery 5, another Storehouse 4), food 23 (Root House 9, Lichen House 7, Ice-Fishing Hut 7). The Huntsman's village (25-30): his lodge and barn, both halls, the shrine, 2 Barracks, a Cookhouse, a Storehouse, 2 Rimeberry Patches, the rest rolled the same way.
      - Headless tests: the igloo showcase test also counts each igloo's guards (in their skull, dyed leather and stone sword) and its crops (none popped off); `boss/huntsman/village/HunterCropsGameTest`: Frostroot and Snow Lichen stay in a sealed dark box where a carrot pops off, a crop gives what plants it (more when grown), the bush grows up into its second block, is picked from its upper half for 4-6 berries and drops back to age 1, breaks as one, can't grow into a block and won't stand on stone, Rimeberries breed Rimehorns, Snow Lichen breeds a bull and a cow (never two bulls).
    - **His and the Alpha Moose's animations** [built October 19, on show only: their fight comes later; reworked twice the same day from the user's looks in-game (below); the latest not yet looked at in-game] (`tools/rimefall/mobs.py` with three modules in `tools/rimefall/rimemobs/`: `alpha_moose.py`, `huntsman.py` (on foot), `huntsman_rider.py` (in the saddle), their shared skeleton `hunter_rig.py`, his hands worked out by IK in `hunt_ik.py` and his shooting in `archery.py`; `tools/rimefall/wildhunt.py` draws the two together). The gallery's models unchanged, their parts on bones (`d06_huntsman.py` routes the rider's hips, chest, head, cape, arms, hands, legs and spear, the moose's bridle to its head and reins to its neck); props shown only while he uses them: a second bow (`bow_held`, its string's halves `string_top`/`string_bot` turned and stretched to his fingers), an arrow on the string and in his hand (`arrow_nock`, `arrow_hand`; three at once for the arrow rain, `arrow_nock3`, `arrow_hand3`), the horn in his left hand (`horn_held`), the bola in his right (`bola_held`: two cords with weights of ice), the spear standing free (`spear_free`: in the snow, in its cup on the saddle, or falling), a quiver at his right hip on foot (`quiver_hip`; in the saddle his cloak lies there, and he takes arrows from the saddle's quiver, `SADDLE_QUIVER`). The things the props stand in for are hidden meanwhile (`hunter_rig.STANDS_FOR`: the bow on his back, the horn on his belt, the spear in his hand).
      - **The user's notes and what was done** (October 19, two looks in-game): the riding thrust went through the moose's antlers (now his arm is drawn back and the spear driven forward and down past the moose's right side, under the antlers, and brought back out to his right and up; mobs.py checks every riding animation against the moose's idle, gallop and charge from any moment of their loops: `check_rider`); the bow looked unnatural and he wanted a real arrow (now: the bow off his back, his bow arm straight, an arrow from the quiver nocked with the bow held out in front of his chest while he faces the target, then side-on as he swings the bow out and draws to his chin, the string following his fingers; a real arrow flies in the game, harmless on show); "the arm holding the bow should be straight", the arrow rain "grabs 3 arrows at a time" (four loosings of three); the horn looked wrong, then was held too short for its sound (now at his lips for a 2 s call, the mouthpiece at his mouth by IK; the placeholder sound a 2.5 s goat horn); the deaths (his: the spear slips at once and topples forward like a felled pole, clear of him, he staggers, catches himself on a knee, cries out and falls back onto his back; the Alpha Moose's own: it rears with a last bellow, crashes onto its knees and rolls onto its side, resting on its fur, not its stirrups: it floated); his cloak clipped into the moose (now it keeps lying over the rump whatever his hips and chest do: `_keep_cape`), his hip quiver went through it (gone in the saddle), the wolf pelt on the back of his hood cut into his mantle when he turned his head (now shorter, the rest of the pelt on his shoulders); in the leap off the moose he slid sideways as he landed (the game now shows a move of over a block at once: `HuntMob.lerpTo`); the backstep shot: a leap back, a shot in mid-air, the landing (his arm went through his body: mobs.py now checks his arms against his body too).
      - **The user's notes of October 20 and what was done** (step 4 of the plan, after their play test): the spear a plain pole with a small collar and its spike (no grip, hilt or butt piece); the quiver further out to his side, its top all ice blue like the fletching; the cloak given more shape (pleated) and kept off the moose at a gallop (its top now stays where it lies on the saddle when he bobs and leans, not only its angle: `huntsman_rider._keep_cape`; `check_rider` checks it); the quiver strapped into the saddle's rolled fur, its arrows staggered so nothing flickers; the bowstring off his hood and antlers (the bow canted the other way, `hunt_ik.CANT`; the spot his drawing fist comes back to, `FACE_ANCHOR`, out past his hood's side; the string never drawn past his chin, and the bow kept on the line through it when his straight arm can't reach a full draw); his bow arm straight (fully in the arrow rain, nearly in the shots, on foot and riding: his elbows were modelled bent and the IK could open them only 4 degrees past that, so they never got straight); his drawing elbow and every other IK'd elbow bent outward by a pole (`hunt_ik.pole_elbow`, the two-bone IK way), an open stance at full draw (`archery.DRAWN_FACE`) and the hand's ways round his chest, so his arms don't fold into his chest or hood; the bola visible in his hand and whirled clear of him, and the snare throw removed (on foot he throws the bola too, `bola_throw`); the leap strike's spear raised over his right shoulder, point forward and down (it was held straight up, through his head); his death's arms keyed in front of him (rolled the wrong way, they crossed through his chest); his spear no longer dips into the snow when he lands or walks. mobs.py now also checks the bowstring, the bola and the spear against his body.
      - **Alpha Moose** (`alpha_moose`, 20 bones, about 5.3 blocks to its antler tips, 6.8 long, in its tack, nobody on it): `idle`, `walk`, `run` (its gallop, also under him in stage 1), `charge` (head low, antlers forward), `ram`, `back_kick` (its weight onto its front legs, both hind legs kicking out behind), `antler_toss` (head under you, then flung up), `stomp` (a little rear, both front hooves down), `bellow` (neck out and up, head shaking: the roar's cone 0.55-1.75 s), `crack_stamp` (three paws at the snow, then a stamp: the cracks start at 1.55 s), `rear` (the split: up on its hind legs, front hooves pawing, 2.4 s), `eat` (kneeling, like the wild moose), `death` (4.6 s, its last pose stays: rears with a bellow at 0.5 s, down on its knees, over onto its side by 2.85 s, a few kicks, its head lifted once and laid down). Hits: ram 0.45 s, kick 0.5, toss 0.55, stomp 0.62.
      - **The Huntsman on foot** (`huntsman`, 5.6 blocks to his antlers): `idle`, `walk`, `run` (spear low and forward), `spear_combo` (thrust, thrust, sweep: hits 0.38, 0.78, 1.28 s), `thrust_sweep` (0.4, 0.88), `leap_strike` (a crouch, a leap about 1.6 blocks up, the spear driven down as he lands: 1.02), `throw_spear` (javelin-style, the spear leaves his hand at 0.55) and `catch_spear` (it's back in his fist at 0.25), `aimed_shot` (2.9 s: the glint at 1.2, the release 1.7), `backstep_shoot` (2.75 s: the spear planted, the bow out, a leap 3 blocks back and 1.6 up, the shot at its top at 1.32, the landing at 1.62, the spear yanked back to his hand on its rope; its meta `shift` moves him 3 blocks back: since October 23 along with the leap, and 2.4 s long), `arrow_rain` (4.2 s: four loosings of three arrows at 1.0, 1.75, 2.5, 3.25), `bola_throw` (1.5 s: the bola whirled high over his right shoulder and thrown at 0.95; it replaced the snare throw, October 20), `horn_blow` (3.7 s: the call 0.85-2.9), `land` (out of the leap's crouch, from the moose), `death` (4.6 s, stays down; the spear slips at 0.04, lands at 0.85; his cry 2.15, he hits the snow 3.2).
      - **In the saddle** (`huntsman_rider`; the model's floor is the moose's ground, so in the game he sits at the moose's own position and turns with it; his whole skeleton hangs from a bone `carry`): `ride_idle`, `ride_gallop` (his own part of riding the gallop), `ride_thrust` (1.3 s, the hit at 0.62), `ride_shoot` (2.9 s, the circling hunt's two shots out to his left at 1.15 and 2.05), `ride_rain` (4.2 s, four loosings of three), `ride_horn` (3.7 s), `ride_bola` (whirled overhead, thrown at 0.95), `ride_leap` (1.4 s since October 23, the split: he rides the moose's rear up, springs off to its left at 0.85 and lands crouched with his boots on the snow 46 units out at 1.26: in the game he then gets off there and plays `land` on foot; it was 1.0 s, off at 0.45, before the moose was up), and `carry_<name>` for each of the moose's animations: the saddle's motion (its body bone's, root and all), played on its own animation controller so he rides along with whatever the moose does (rears, kicks, kneels, gallops), except while he leaps off. Since October 23 the game draws the two as one model while he rides, `wild_hunt` (see "Fixes from the user's play test of October 23" above).
      - Checked like the animals: nothing under the floor, his arms never deeper into his body than they sit at rest, and the rider's spear, bow and arrows never into the moose's head, antlers, ears, neck or body (and, sitting still, the moose's head, antlers and ears never into him while it makes its own moves). The moments the game acts on (each arrow's tick, where it leaves the bow and which way it flies; named moments like hits and falls; the backstep's shift; how far out the leap lands) are generated into `boss/huntsman/HuntCues.java` (kept per model in `tools/rimefall/rimemobs/hunt_cues.json`). Previews `boss-previews/rimefall/mobs/alpha_moose/`, `huntsman/`, `huntsman_rider/` (filmstrips, a GIF of every animation; `wild_hunt.png` and `wild_hunt_*.gif`: him riding the moose through each of his animations, him sitting still through each of the moose's moves, and the split), all on `boss-previews/rimefall/mobs/index.html`.
      - **In the game** (Boss area, `boss/huntsman/`: `HuntMob` (their GeckoLib animation picked by name and synced; on show: frozen, no AI, only `/kill` hurts them, never pushed, leashed or despawned; a move goes back to standing 1 s after it ends, a death stays down; his shots loose real arrows from where the model's leave the bow (`HuntCues`), harmless on show (no damage: they glance off a player); a move of over a block is shown at once, not slid (`lerpTo`); an animation that carries him (the backstep) moves him along with its leap (`glide`)), `Huntsman` (in an `AlphaMoose`'s saddle, `isMounted`, he plays his riding animations, and since October 23 isn't drawn himself: the moose wears the two together, `wild_hunt`, and plays his animation and the saddle's `carry_<name>` on its own controllers; at the end of `ride_leap` he gets off, onto his feet `HuntCues.LEAP_OUT` blocks to the moose's left, and plays `land`), `AlphaMoose` (he sits at its feet' position and turns with it), `HuntAnims` (every animation, its length and whether its last pose stays), `HuntCues` (generated), `HuntSoundCues` (the sounds at their animations' moments, in the fight and on show: the user's picks, "Sounds" below), `HuntsmanEvents`; `client/HuntModel`, `HuntRenderer` (cut-out, the glow full-bright, the ice blended), `HuntsmanClientEvents`; entity types `dross:huntsman` (1.1 x 4.0 since October 23; 1.2 x 4.4) and `dross:alpha_moose` (2.8 x 4.6) in `registry/ModEntities`). **`/dross showcase boss_designs huntsman`** (`command/HuntsmanShowcase`): the Huntsman riding the Alpha Moose, the Huntsman on foot and the moose alone in a row ahead of you with signs, and every animation as a chat button (`... huntsman <animation>`: everything near that has it plays it; the moose carrying him gallops with his `ride_gallop`, and he rides along with its `run` or `charge`; when the moose carrying him rears or dies he leaps off first (the split: the moose starts as he springs, he lands on foot beside it); [reset] puts the three back). Headless test: `command/HuntsmanShowcaseGameTest` (every animation in its file at the length the mod thinks, the models and textures in the mod, him in the saddle at the moose's position wearing his riding animations, each one playing only its own, a move going back to standing, no harm, the split (off at the end of his leap, on foot 2.875 blocks to the moose's left, landing, the moose rearing), his aimed shot loosing a real arrow, the backstep leaving him 3 blocks back, every death staying down).
    - **Sounds** [built October 23; the user's picks the same day; not yet heard in a fight] (`tools/huntsman/sounds.py`, the Rat King's synthesizer: all original): made in 2-3 versions each for the user to pick from, now 65 sounds in 28 events. **The user's picks** (each event plays only its picks; the other versions stay in the showcase): his war cry when he spots you, a howl (see below; then his horn, 1.9 s later: `HuntsmanTuning.WAR_CRY_TICKS`; his goals wait meanwhile, `HuntMob.holding`); the bow's shot **3** (a deep twang); the arrow rain coming down **2** (a heavier storm, at the first arrow down); a frost arrow's hit **1**; the bola whirled overhead **1** and its throw and snare **2** (cut in two, `bola_release` as he lets go and `bola_snare` when it hits); the Alpha Moose's ice spikes **1**, cracking stamp **1**, stomp **2**, ram (and the antler toss) **2**, back kick **2**, footsteps **1**, gallop **2** (one stride's sound at a time while it runs or charges), death **2**, hurt **1**, idle **1 and 2**, its call as it rears **1**, bellow **3 and 1**. **His horn stays the goat horn** (the user: "the one we have is fine"; the three horn options were removed). **His voice remade to sound human**, twice (the user: it "just sounds like a moose ... sound more human", then "I was hoping more human-ish"): now made with `tools/huntsman/voicekit.py`, a voice synthesizer of its own (the vocal folds' puffs of air period by period, Rosenberg pulses with jitter, shimmer and breath in each puff, through five formant resonators gliding between a big man's vowels, Klatt's way; an "h" breathed through the coming vowel, glottal stops, rasp, creak as a voice dies; meant for his villagers' voices too): grunts (hup!, **hah!** picked), hurt (ugh! with a croak, agh! and a hiss through his teeth, **oof** picked) and death (**aaaagh... into a croak, his last breath** picked, nngh... aaah, two gasps and a long sigh). **The war cry** (the user: "more wolfiness", then of three pure wolf howls: "more human as well, not entirely wolf"): three howls of a man with a wolf in it (`half_howl`: his voicekit voice from his chest up into a softer head voice, 30% of `voicekit.howl`'s smooth wolf tone under it): aw-ooooo; ow, ow, ooooo; his howl with the pack answering far off (those two wolves pure); the old man's howl kept as version 4, **the user's pick** ("4 for the war cry is fine"). **The rest of his voice on voicekit too** (the user, of the new grunts, hurt and death: "great, change the rest of his voice to match"): idle (all three, the user's picks: the frosty breath and a hum, sniffing out your trail then a growl in his throat, the low hunting hum), his "hmph" as he lands; the spear's swing and throw are only air now (his grunt with them is the effort sound, played at the blow). Unchanged sounds aren't written again (`tools/huntsman/sound_hashes.json`: the Ogg encoder makes different bytes each run). Skipped by the user, playing version 1 for now: his footsteps, landing, the spear's hit and throw, the bow's draw; the spear's swing plays 1 for a thrust and 2 for a sweep. Where they play: `HuntSoundCues` (at animation moments, lined up so a whoosh, a thump or the stamp lands on the moment), `HuntMob` (his arrows' twang, once a volley), `HuntFight` (the war cry, the rain, a frost hit, the ice spikes; the cracks' running glass sounds are gone), `Bola`, `HuntSpear`, `FootGoal`, `Huntsman` and `AlphaMoose` (idle, hurt, steps; frozen on show they make no idle sounds). In `assets/dross/sounds/huntsman/` and `alpha_moose/`; each event `entity.huntsman.<name>` / `entity.alpha_moose.<name>` and each version on its own as `<event>.v<n>`; the generated list `boss/huntsman/HuntsmanSounds` (with each event's picks). **`/dross showcase boss_sounds huntsman`** (`command/HuntsmanSoundShowcase`) lists them in chat with a numbered button per version; the page `boss-previews/rimefall/huntsman/sounds/index.html` plays every version, the picks in green.
    - **Showcases and eggs**: `/dross showcase rimefall_mobs` (male, female and baby Rimehorn, moose and calf, the Pond Skater, with animation buttons), `/dross showcase boss_designs huntsman` (him riding and the two apart, every animation), spawn eggs (Huntsman, Rimehorn, Moose, Pond Skater; an egg used on an adult makes a baby, like vanilla's). The statue gallery keeps only the designs that aren't real mobs yet.
    - **The animals' skeletons and animations** [built: part 2, October 18; previews only, in the game from part 3] (`tools/rimefall/mobs.py`, the modules in `tools/rimefall/rimemobs/`, the shared code `rimekit/mobkit.py`; see the tool's README): each animal is the gallery design's look unchanged, its parts routed to bones (`shapes.Parts`: the design calls `s.part("head")` and so on; a statue puts every part on its one bone), legs planted with the golem kit's IK (an ankle bone keeps each hoof or blade flat; a leg whose middle can't lie flat sinks a little, so each gait has its own crouch), recorded at 20 frames a second and checked: nothing under the ground (0.6 units' give) and no part into another deeper than it sits in the rest pose. Files: `geo/entity/<id>/<id>.geo.json`, `animations/entity/<id>/<id>.animation.json` (`animation.<id>.<name>`), `textures/entity/<id>/<variant>.png` (+ `_ice` for the Rimehorn's horns and the Skater's blades); previews `boss-previews/rimefall/mobs/<id>/` (a filmstrip of every animation and a GIF of each) and `boss-previews/rimefall/mobs/index.html` (every GIF on one page). A face hidden inside another box is only dropped when both are on the same bone, and nothing counts as under the floor (feet lift).
      - **Rimehorn** (`rimehorn`, textures `male` (7A) and `female` (7B); 16 bones: body, the snow patch (its own bone: males and calves hide it), neck, head, ears, tail, each leg's upper leg, shin and foot): `idle` (6 s: breathing, a slow look round, ear flicks, a tail swish, a snort), `walk` (a four-beat walk, about 1.1 blocks a second at its natural pace), `run` (a gallop, about 5.4), `rush` (the charge: the gallop with its head low and the great horn levelled, ears back, tail up, about 6.5), `eat` (a loop: the front of the body tipped down 7 degrees, the head lowered to the snow, two sweeps of the horn, then nibbling), `flick` (1.3 s: head down, then the horn flicked up hard, the hit at 0.45 s). Its legs are pillars, straight when it stands, so it stands 1 unit sunk, walks 2, gallops 5. A calf: the whole animal at 0.42 with its head at 1.45 (the renderer's job).
      - **Moose** (`moose`, one texture `moose`; 20 bones; about 3.8 blocks tall, 4.8 long; the gallery's Alpha Moose body at 0.44 of full size, no tack, no face plate, plain dark eyes): `idle`, `walk` (about 1.1 blocks a second), `run` (a trot, diagonal pairs, about 4.5), `charge` (a gallop, head low and antlers forward, about 7.2), `attack` (1.1 s: a lunging ram, the hit at 0.45 s, the push forward rather than up), `eat` (6 s, once: it folds its front legs back and drops onto its front knees, grazes, and gets up; a real moose's way of reaching the ground). **Bulls carry antlers; cows (the mothers) and calves don't** (the `antlers` bone hidden; like real moose). A calf: 0.45 with its head at 1.4.
      - **Pond Skater** (`pondskater`; 25 bones: body, head, feelers, the raised front legs in two pieces, the middle and hind legs in four each, the last holding the blade): `idle`, `walk` (off the ice: a stepping gait in diagonal pairs), `skate` (0.9 s: the middle legs row back together like oars and the body surges, the hind blades gliding), `attack` (1.1 s: it rears up, the front legs grab, the beak stabs down, the hit at 0.5 s). Its blades plant flat on the ice, pointing where the gallery model's do.
    - **The animals in the game** [built: part 3, October 19; then the user's first look in-game the same day (below); not yet tested in-game since] (Boss area, `boss/rimefall/`: `Rimehorn`, `Moose`, `PondSkater` and its `PondSkaterPart`, `RimefallAnimals` (attributes, spawn rules, the showcase's freezing), `RimefallAnims` (which animation plays: an action the server started, synced with a counter; or the walk or run, sped up or slowed to the real speed so the feet don't slide; or idle), `RimefallAnimal` (what the showcase drives), every number in `RimefallMobTuning`; `client/RimefallMobModel` (hides the bones one doesn't show, scales a calf's head up after the animations, every frame) and `client/RimefallMobRenderer` (a calf drawn small; the Rimehorn's clear ice horns blended over it), `client/RimefallMobClientEvents`; entity types `dross:rimehorn` (creature, 3.4 x 4.2), `dross:moose` (creature, 2.0 x 3.3), `dross:pond_skater` (monster, 1.8 x 2.3) in `registry/ModEntities`):
      - **Rimehorn**: 40 health, armour 4, speed 0.2, 70% knockback resistance; neutral (anger 20-39 s, like a polar bear); hurting one (a calf too) calls its pack within 16 blocks (`HurtByTargetGoal.setAlertOthers`). Up close the **horn flick** (1.3 s, the hit at 0.45 s, 3.2 blocks past the front of its body: 6 damage, its victim thrown up 1.05 and back 0.45); from 5.5 to 12 blocks the **rush**, a quick dash (the user: "make a quick dash, make the rush a little faster"): a 0.25 s wind-up, then a straight run at 0.54 blocks a tick (about 11 a second; 0.42 before) for up to 8 blocks, its animation a low bounding dash at that speed (`rush`, 0.46 s a stride); the first thing in its way takes 5 and is thrown; 3.5 s before the next; at once when an arrow (anything thrown) hits it from afar. Bred from a male and a female fed **Frostbloom** or **Rimeberries** (the user, October 20: "you can use rimeberry bush for rimehorn") (`canMate` wants opposite sexes); they walk up to each other and mate within their width and 1.5 blocks (`MateGoal`, `MATE_REACH_PAST_WIDTH`: vanilla's breeding wants 3 blocks, which two Rimehorns pushing against each other never reach, the user's "they struggle to make a calf"); the calf takes a random sex, grows up in 35 minutes (`setBaby`) and remembers its breeder (`Breeder`): **it never attacks them** (`canAttack`, `isAngryAt`, `setTarget` all refuse them, so a pack's call can't turn it on them). A pack: its first 2 males, the rest females (vanilla's group data counts them); an egg's is a male or a female at random (`/summon dross:rimehorn ~ ~ ~ {Male:1b}` for a male, `{Male:0b}` a female). Wild ones are only Rimefall's few herds (below). **Its hitboxes** (the user: "the front of his face is off"): its main hitbox is a 3.4-block square round its middle, so three more (`RimefallBodyPart`, like the Pond Skater's legs; measured from its model, turned with its body, a calf's at 0.42) cover its face and horn (about 4 blocks in front), its neck and chest and its rump; hitting any hurts it. **The chase** (the user saw them run sideways, and a herd follow them across the biome): it runs straight at you, head on, when the way is clear (`boss/BossChase`, as the bosses do; the pathfinder only round things); its anger runs out after 20-39 s even while it chases (vanilla's neutral mobs stay angry for as long as they keep their target), it gives up 24 blocks from where the chase began and walks back (`RIMEHORN_CHASE_LEASH`), and a calf runs off when hurt (its herd does the fighting). Grazes now and then (8 s). No drops; a little experience. Original sounds, all breath and chest (no calls: the user found it sounded too like the moose): deep rumbles, heavy wet chuffs through its nostrils, a frosty snort, a deep "hmmph", a gruff roar when hurt, very deep footfalls, the flick's heave, the rush's roar and thundering feet.
      - **Moose**: 50 health, armour 2, speed 0.22; neutral; **a mother** (a cow with a calf within 16 blocks) goes for any player within 6 blocks of her or her calf (like a polar bear; 12 from her before), but **never chases further than 10 blocks from her calf** (`MOTHER_LEASH`: she gives up, walks back to it until she's within 4 blocks of it (`MOTHER_BACK_WITHIN`; until October 20 the end of her chase cancelled her way back, and a headless test once saw her wander 13 blocks off; until October 24 a random wander could still take over on her way back and lead her off past the leash, now she doesn't wander while she's going back) and doesn't start again for 3 s; the user: "the range of attack for a moose mother is too far, it leaves the calf"); one you hurt is angry like any moose, but **a mother keeps to her calf whatever she chases** (the user, October 25, 2026: "she charges all the way away from her calf ... like 50 blocks away during her charge"; until then a hurt mother chased like any moose, 24 blocks from wherever each chase began): she remembers her calf while it's within 64 blocks (`MOTHER_REMEMBERS`; she'd only find it within 16) and gives up any chase 10 blocks from it, then walks back (`Moose.keepToCalf`; test `RimefallAnimalsGameTest.hurtMooseMotherKeepsToHerCalf`). Giving up a chase now stops the target goal that picked her target too (`Moose.dropTarget`; the Rimehorn's leash the same): Minecraft's target goals remember their target and set it again the next tick, so a moose or Rimehorn that "gave up" went on after you, a little further each tick; a lone moose never goes for you; a calf runs when hurt and calls its mother. It **charges** (its head-down gallop, 1.9 times its speed) and **rams** within 2.4 blocks of its front (1.1 s, the hit at 0.45 s: a bull 9 damage, a cow 6, the user: "make the male moose do more damage than the female"; 7 for both before; thrown back 1.6 and up only 0.3). Like the Rimehorn it charges straight at you when the way is clear, its anger runs out while it chases and it gives up 24 blocks from where the chase began (`MOOSE_CHASE_LEASH`), and its head and rump are hitboxes of their own (it's nearly 5 blocks long). Natural moose: 3 in 10 are a mother with her calf beside her, the rest a lone bull or cow (half each); bred since October 20 from a bull and a cow fed **Snow Lichen** or **Frostbloom** (the user: "the snow lichen for the [moose] ... we have a flower ... we can use that for both"; a spawn egg used on one still makes a calf). **Its coat** (the user, October 19: "only moose that spawned in Rimefall should have white"): a moose that spawns in Rimefall (naturally, from an egg or `/summon` there, or in a hunter village) wears the frosty coat (`moose.png`); one anywhere else (the Silverwood Forest, a spawn egg in the Overworld) the same coat with no frost on it (`moose_plain.png`: `tools/rimefall/rimemobs/moose.py`'s plain variants, `d06_huntsman.materials` with `plain`); saved as `Snowy`, its calf takes its mother's (`Moose.isSnowy`; checked by `RimefallAnimalsGameTest.mooseSnowOnlyInRimefall`). Kneels to graze now and then (6 s). No drops. Original sounds, a voice (nothing of the Rimehorn's rumble): a cow's long wavering nasal wail, a bull's croaky "oh-wah", a nasal honk, a snuffle and lip smacks, a bleating bray when hurt, light woody hoof clops, the charge's rising nasal bellow, the ram's croak and the hollow clack of its antlers.
      - **Pond Skater**: no frost on it any more (the user, October 19: "remove the snow on the pond skater's model"; `d08_pondskater.materials`), and no white hairs either (October 20: "you haven't removed the white from their textures": the white specks were its shell's painted hairs); **20% smaller** than its gallery design (October 20: "decrease the skater size by around 20%, it seems too big"; `RimefallMobTuning.SKATER_SCALE` 0.8: drawn at 0.8, its hitbox 1.44 x 1.84, its legs' hitboxes and spots, its reach and its strides all scaled; the previews show the design's full size); 16 health, 3 damage, a spider's speed 0.3; **on ice** (`#minecraft:ice`) its speed is 2.6 times that (a transient modifier, on while the block under its feet is ice; it skates there); hostile (to players, the Frostfolk, and since October 24, 2026 Rimefall's big game too: the Huntsman, his Alpha
        Moose, the moose and the Rimehorns, which fight back, the Rimehorns as a herd; the user: "make sure Pond Skaters
        are hostile to the Frostfolk, the Huntsman and the moose and Rimehorns as well"; `PondSkater.isPrey`); it rears
        up and stabs within 2.2 blocks of its body (1.1 s, the hit at 0.5 s). **Six leg hitboxes** (`PondSkaterPart`, 0.7 x 0.9, like the Ender Dragon's parts: ids after its own, moved every tick, turned with its body): the middle legs' about 3.6 blocks out each side, the hind legs' back and out, the front hooks'; hitting one hurts it. Original sounds: chittering clicks, a dry rasp, a buzz, tapping legs, an icy glide, the stab's hiss.
      - **Spawning** (`tools/terrain/worldgen.py`, `SPAWN_OVERRIDES["rimefall"]` and `SPAWN_REPLACEMENTS["silverwood_forest"]`; Dimension's): Rimefall: only its animals (the user: "the skaters, the rimehorns and the moose should be the only mobs spawning there"); no zombies, skeletons, endermen, witches, bats or vanilla animals (so no Necromancy souls or Deathforged books from Rimefall). Its list: **moose** (alone, the only creature, a creature chance of 0.008 a chunk, about what they had) and **Pond Skaters** (monsters, 1-2, on ice in the dark, even plain ice, which vanilla keeps for polar bears; but **only on a big frozen lake, 1 at most or 2 on about half the lakes (`RimefallLake.skaterCap`, fixed by its seed), or on any other patch of ice at least 20 blocks across (frozen pond or not), 1 at most**: the user saw 10 on one small pond, "one that's 10 blocks shouldn't even get one", then (October 19) "don't force 2 to always be in a large lake, just make it so 2 could possibly spawn" and "a little more common for smaller ice patches like 20 blocks or larger"; `RimefallAreas.skaterMayAppear`, measuring a patch's open ice in loaded chunks; then (October 20, after seeing 7 on one pond: skaters that wandered off their pond no longer counted for it, and a pond with ice at several heights counted each as its own) **never more than 3 within 48 blocks** (`SKATERS_PER_AREA`), a skater up to 16 blocks off its lake or patch still counts for it (`SKATER_WANDER_MARGIN`), and a natural spawn with room happens only **15% of the time** (`SKATER_SPAWN_CHANCE`, `RimefallAnimals.skaterRolls`: "they should have a random spawn rate"); it counts the Pond Skaters by class, since a search by entity type also returns their leg hitboxes: that crashed the game when one first spawned on a lake, October 19). **Rimehorns aren't in the list**: each Rimefall patch gets **a few herds** as its land is made (the user's answer: "a few per area"; below), so a herd that's gone stays gone (they still breed). **Moose in Silverwood Forest too** (the user: "in the main Dross forest but with less probability"): weight 2 among its animals (about half Rimefall's chance), on Silvermoss, podzol or moss (`dross:moose_spawnable_on`), never in the hub's 48-block safe zone. Checked by `DrossSpawnsGameTest` (`checkRimefallLists`, `checkSilverwoodLists`; the Gravecrags are now the base lists' reference).
      - **Spawn eggs** (the Dross tab's spawn eggs group, after the Plague Ravager's): Rimehorn, Moose, Pond Skater (colours in `DrossColors`, Boss).
      - **Showcases** (World site's `command/`): `/dross showcase rimefall_mobs` (`RimefallMobShowcase`): a male, a female and a calf Rimehorn, a bull, a cow and a calf moose and a Pond Skater in a row 10 blocks ahead, frozen on packed-ice pedestals facing you, a post and sign in front of each; controls `idle|walk|run|charge|eat|attack` (each animal plays its own: "attack" the flick, the ram or the stab; "charge" the rush or the charge; "run" the Pond Skater's skating); right-click one: its next animation. `/dross showcase mob_sounds rimefall` (`RimefallMobSoundShowcase`): every sound with a [play] button.
      - **Sounds** (`tools/rimefall/sounds.py`, synthesized with the Rat King's `ratkit/synth.py`; 43 files in 20 events, `assets/dross/sounds/rimehorn|moose|pondskater/`, their subtitles, the generated `boss/rimefall/RimefallMobSounds`, the page `boss-previews/rimefall/mobs/sounds/index.html` playing every version). Remade on October 19 so the Rimehorn and the moose sound nothing alike (the Rimehorn's are mostly deep: 20-66% of their energy under 150 Hz; the moose's almost none). The user picks which versions to keep.
      - **The statue gallery** now holds only the designs that aren't real animals yet: 10 (the Rimehorns' and the Pond Skater's statues left it: `GALLERY = False` in their design modules).
      - **Headless tests** (`RimefallAnimalsGameTest`): a natural pack of 7 has 2 males; two males can't mate, a male and a female can, and their calf grows up in 35 minutes and remembers its breeder; a male and a female in love a few blocks apart make a calf on their own (`rimehornsBreedOnTheirOwn`); hurting one turns its pack on you, but never the one you bred; the flick throws mostly up; the rush hits from afar; a mother moose goes for a player near her calf, a lone moose doesn't; a mother gives up a chase 10 blocks from her calf (`mooseMotherKeepsToHerCalf`); a bull's ram hurts more than a cow's (`mooseBullHitsHarder`); the Rimehorn's face and the moose's head sit in front of them as hitboxes that hurt them (`rimehornAndMooseHeadsAreHitboxes`); a Rimehorn's anger runs out mid-chase and it gives up past its leash (`rimehornGivesUpTheChase`); the ram throws mostly back; the Pond Skater is 2.6 times faster on packed ice and back to a spider's speed off it; its six leg hitboxes sit where its legs are and hitting one hurts it; a Pond Skater may spawn on a frozen pond 31 across and on a patch of packed ice 21 across, not on a pond 9 across or a patch 15 across, and not on the big pond once one is there, even when it has wandered onto the rock beside it; none on the patch with 3 others 31 blocks off; a natural spawn with room about 15% of the time (`pondSkatersNeedABigPond`).
    - **Rimefall's big lakes and herds** [built October 19, not yet tested in-game] (Dimension area, `dimension/rimefall/`; every number in `RimefallTuning`; the user's requests: "add a large ice lake for the biome, guaranteed", "each rimefall should have 1 big lake", "like 100 blocks across", with open water holes (the user's pick of four looks: no islands or ice ridges); the herds "a few per area"):
      - **The plan** (`RimefallAreas`, the boss-site planner's per-patch use `BossSites.RIMEFALL_AREA`, step 12): every Rimefall patch gets one `RimefallArea`: its **lake** and its **herds' spots**, from the terrain's noise alone, the same every time for the same seed. The lake goes in the patch's flattest roomy spot (of its deepest spots, 24 apart, deep enough for the lake, its 16-block shore and 8 more: the lowest average cut and fill, its ice at the lowest quarter of the ground's heights inside it, never cutting more than 12 blocks or needing a dam over 4); a patch with no room for the big one (about 100 across: a radius of 50, its edge wobbling with 2-, 3- and 5-lobed waves) gets a small one (about 64 across), and as a last resort the small one cutting up to 24 and damming up to 8, so every patch with room for a lake has one. Its 4-6 **holes** (1.6-3.4 blocks round, wobbly) lie within 0.7 of the radius, 10 apart. The herds: 2, or 3 a quarter of the time; in a patch under 120,000 blocks² 1, or 2 a quarter of the time (`herdCount`, fixed by the seed; the user, October 19: "only 2-3 per biome, 2 being 75% and 3 being 25% ... smaller ones should have 1 or 2 max"; 3, or 4 in a big patch, before), at the patch's deepest spots 96 apart, at least 40 past the lake's edge. A patch so small that its lake leaves no such spot still gets one herd, at the spot furthest from the lake (at least 22 past its edge: `HERD_LAKE_CLEARANCE_LAST`; found October 19 on a seed whose 140-block patch had none).
      - **The lake** (structure `dross:rimefall_lake`, `RimefallLakeStructure`, one piece `RimefallLakePiece`; placed by the planner's `dross:boss_site` placement in its middle's chunk, built chunk by chunk in the "lakes" step): ground above the ice's level cut away; a sheet of **ice** at its level over **dark water** 2 blocks deep at the edge, 7 in the middle, on a bed of gravel with clay patches; the **holes** open water you can fall into, their top water in an invisible light block of level 0 (waterlogged), which Minecraft never freezes over; the **shore** (16 blocks) lowered smoothly from the land's height to the ice's at the water's edge (no cliffs), dips beside the water raised to the ice's level 3 blocks out and then in steps of 1, so the water never gets out; each column keeps its own top blocks (snow, Hoarfrost, packed ice). Nothing grows on it: Rimefall's trees, ice spikes, boulders, ponds, Frostblooms and snow drifts keep 6 blocks off its edge (`dross:not_on_rimefall_lake`, `NotOnRimefallLakeFilter`).
      - **The herds** (feature `dross:rimehorn_herd`, `RimehornHerdFeature`, in Rimefall's last step before the tidy-up): the chunk holding a herd's spot gets 6-8 Rimehorns round it (each tries 15 spots within 6 blocks, then 40 anywhere in the chunk, on Rimefall's ground with room for them; with only 10 tries near the spot a wooded seed once stood up 3), as a natural pack (the first 2 males; now and then a calf).
      - **`/dross goto lake`** (`command/GotoLake`): the shore of the nearest lake (from you in the Dross, from the hub elsewhere), on the side facing you, facing the lake.
      - **Headless tests** (`RimefallLakeGameTest`): Rimefall's 9 nearest patches each planned (a lake in every one with room, its shore inside the patch, 4-6 holes, 2-3 herds (1-2 in a small patch) well apart and off the lake, the same when planned again); the nearest lake generated (ice or open water over water in every lake column, nothing standing on it, the holes don't freeze, a solid wall round the water, Pond Skaters until its cap (1 or 2) are on it); a herd standing at the nearest herd spot.

## Terrain v1 [built, branch `terrain-v1`]
The user's request and answers (October 2026), kept as a record. Built in stages on branch `terrain-v1`, each checked headlessly and committed; the user play-tested it twice and their feedback is built in. The full report, every choice made and the in-game checklist are in `TERRAIN_REPORT.md`; the design now lives in The plan (steps 1, 4, 5 and 8) and the Areas table.

**The request:** replace the superflat Dross with real terrain (Twilight Forest feel, own character): rolling land, rocky cliffs and outcrops where they fit, a mountain area. Surface only (no caves, carvers or aquifers). Always night, electric blue identity: blue-tinted fog and sky, blue accents (colours from `DrossColors`). Every biome gets its own original plants, ground cover and blocks. Keep the current mob spawn rules in every biome. Tuning numbers (hill height, cliff frequency, mountain height, tree density, ore amounts...) are labeled constants.

**The user's answers:**
- **Biomes about 400-800 blocks across** (Nether Scar: smaller patches). **Small ponds only** (dark water in Silverwood, murky in Blightwood, frozen in Rimefall); no seas or rivers.
- Six biomes: **Silverwood Forest** `dross:silverwood_forest` (the main biome, dense and dark, silverwood trees with Stormvine; it surrounds the hub), **Cinder Wastes** `dross:cinder_wastes` (volcanic: basalt, magma, ash, small lava pools; future home of the Fire Necromancer), **Blightwood** `dross:blightwood` (rotten and fungal: giant decaying mushrooms, mycelium, sickly fog; future home of the Plague Necromancer), **Rimefall** `dross:rimefall` (frozen: packed ice, snow, frost-covered dead trees; future home of the Ice Necromancer), **Nether Scar** `dross:nether_scar` (patches where the Nether bleeds through: netherrack, crimson and warped fungi, soul sand), **The Gravecrags** `dross:gravecrags` (the mountain area: high rocky peaks and cliffs, peaks about y 230-260; future home of the Earth General).
- The hub (the Shattered Spire at 0,0) sits properly on the terrain: footprint levelled, edges blended like a staircase (at most 1 block per step), the 48-block safe zone still works, arrivals land correctly, never in the Gravecrags.
- **The two survivors** (never call them guards, in code, names or text): **the Blind Oracle** (skin 8, the **mentor**) and **the Ragged Seeker** (skin 3, the **student**), spawned at the hub. They walk around together, in and around the spire and out of its front gate, never more than **20 blocks outside its walls**. Exactly like the trader, they can be hit and knocked back but never die (at half health they heal and pop back to their spot), and hostile mobs ignore them. No trades.
- `/dross goto biome <name>` teleports you to the nearest example of each Dross biome (`silverwood_forest`, `cinder_wastes`, `blightwood`, `rimefall`, `nether_scar`, `gravecrags`).
- **Later (October 17, 2026): the Nether Scar biome was removed** at the user's request ("can we remove the nether scar as a biome as well"). Its land went to the biomes round it (each place gets the biome its heat and rot say; no other border moved), `/dross goto biome nether_scar` is gone, and its four blocks (Scarred Netherrack, Scar Roots, Soulwick, Scar Vent) are kept as building blocks only. The answers above are kept as a record.
- Top-down previews of a generated area (height and biome maps) in `terrain-previews/`.

**Blocks** (all original textures, drawn by `tools/terrain/`): Duskstone (the Dross's base stone), Duskstone Coal Ore, Duskstone Gold Ore (drops gold nuggets like nether gold ore), Stormite Ore (Stormite Shard: glowing blue crystal, for special arrows and the Great Mystic Bow later), Moonsilver Ore (Raw Moonsilver, Moonsilver Ingot: a pale metal for anti-undead gear later); silverwood log, wood, stripped log, stripped wood, planks, leaves, sapling; Stormvine (glowing electric blue vine on silverwood trees, decoration only); Silvermoss, Glimmer Fern, Wispbloom (Silverwood Forest); Ash, Ash Layer, Charred Log, Emberweed, Cinder Vent (Cinder Wastes); Sporeloam, Blightcap Block, Rotting Blightcap, Blightcap Stem, Gloomcap, Sporepod (Blightwood); Hoarfrost, Frostbitten Log, Frostbloom, Icicle (Rimefall); Scarred Netherrack, Scar Roots, Soulwick, Scar Vent (Nether Scar; kept as building blocks since the biome was removed on October 17, 2026); Cragstone, Storm Lichen, Crag Thistle (Gravecrags). Creative tab: the blocks go at the end of group 1 (blocks); the ore materials form a new group 7, "Materials", after the enchanted books.

**Round 2: the user played it (October 12) and asked for:**
- **Nether Scar:** the glowing cracks look too busy: calmer, irregular cracks, in patches mixed with plain netherrack. Fog and the survivors' skins: good. Rimefall: fine as it is. No Gravecrags plateaus.
- **Both survivors are "he"** (the Blind Oracle too) in lines, code comments and docs.
- **Blightwood blends in** with the rest of the Dross: recolour it (only the look, the same amount of mushrooms) to poison and decay, "dead and decay, not just dead": **poison green** first, a couple of **decaying reds**, some **beiges**.
- **The Cinder Colossus gets a lair in the Cinder Wastes** (boss area): a round **domed cavern with a skylight**, about 40 blocks across, carved into high ground, ceiling about 22-25 blocks up (its lava lobs peak about 14 blocks above the floor), entered by a tunnel that slopes down from the surface and then runs straight in. **Where:** from the hub at 0,0, find the nearest Cinder Wastes in **three directions**; of those three, use the one **furthest from 0,0**. The Colossus **rises when a player walks in**; after it's beaten it rises again for the next player who walks in **10 minutes** later (a stand-in: the user will add a "boss revive" feature later). `/dross goto lair colossus` goes there (rule 9).
- **Cinder Wastes spawns:** no zombies, skeletons or other mobs there for now; the Colossus's **stone golems** spawn instead, and **lava golems** with a **1-in-5 chance near lava**. Keep them sparse: two more Cinder-only mobs will come later.

**Round 3: the user's second play test (October 12-13) asked for:**
- **The lair must feel volcanic** (it looked like "a regular hole in the ground surrounded by fences"): a volcano outside with lava outside it, a big cave-mouth entrance, an obsidian floor with magma, obsidian spikes hanging from the ceiling, lava below the battle floor, no lava ring at the edge.
- **The fight:** a roar that throws players back about 15 blocks at the first hit and every stage change; 4 minions on entering Cracking and 4 lava blobs on entering the Lava Core; the charge must close the gap from far away and run to where the player was; the user's move-chance table (no rock throws in the Lava Core); against Necromancy minions, summon half as many golems as risen undead and slam more when swarmed; fire behind every charge from Cracking on (half as long in Cracking). The user dropped: players-first targeting, backhanding minions, contact fire, the roar hitting minions.
- **Lava golems** about 1 in 5 of all the golems, still only near lava.

**Who owns what (additions to the table above):** Dimension (`dimension-builder`): the terrain (noise settings, density functions, the `dross:hub_falloff` density function type, biomes, surface rules, features and their Java classes, tree decorators), all the blocks and items above with their assets, tags, recipes and loot, `tools/terrain/`, `block-previews/`, `terrain-previews/`, the Dross sky/fog/light (`dimension/client`), and the biome-finding logic behind `/dross goto biome`. Portal: the hub fitted onto the terrain, plus a small hub-area API for the survivors. World site: `GroundFit` learning the Dross ground through block tags, and wiring `/dross goto biome`. Villager: the survivors. Enchantments: Necromancy never raises undead from hitting a survivor. Boss (round 2): the Colossus lair (site search, carving, the rise and 10-minute respawn) and the stone/lava golems as wild Cinder Wastes mobs. World site (round 2): `/dross goto lair colossus`. Dimension (round 2): the Cinder Wastes spawn list, the Blightwood recolour, the calmer Nether Scar cracks.

## Build order
Original build: 1. Dimension → 2. Portal → 3. World site → 4. Villager → 5. Enchantments → 6. Quest. Run `mod-tester` after each step.

**Status:** everything in "The plan" is **built and tested in-game**, including the whole early-game build below (October 2026). Two additions came after that test (they build, but weren't separately confirmed in-game): the trader sells the Guide Book to players who have "Enter the Dross", and he leaves a boat or minecart before teleporting home. Later additions, also not yet confirmed in-game: the Rift Chapel replaces the netherite hut in new worlds; a tribute held during your first right-click is taken straight after his speech; and players who finished the quest but have no Rift Key (and haven't entered the Dross) are told he can forge another. Then: the risen undead pack AI, the five-level Necromancy (with the one-time remap of old items), the Deathforged I–V anvil rules, and the chapel placed next to the village houses with a 3-wide path (the chapel itself was tested in-game; its new placement wasn't yet). Most recently (not yet confirmed in-game): the 10 trader skins and 10 shrine designs with `/dross showcase trader_skins` and `/dross showcase portal_shrines`, and the user's picks from them: the trader wears skin 2 (The Archivist), new worlds get the Fallen Cathedral as the Overworld portal site, placed 3,000–10,000 blocks from spawn on nearly flat ground with a staircase blend and whole-tree removal, and the Shattered Spire as the Dross hub. These passed headless checks in fresh worlds. After that: the trader's key-forging line points to his books, and the Weathered Letter is a 4-page story (both confirmed in-game). Then, also confirmed in-game: chat messages giving the site's and the trader's locations, `/dross goto cathedral` refusing before the site exists, risen undead dropping a target that died (the one-hit-kill bug), and Dross enchanted books showing only in the Dross creative tab. Most recently: GeckoLib and the Cinder Colossus showcase (step 8). Round 1's five designs were looked at in-game by the user, who picked No. 3 as the base; the user looked at round 2 (four variations of No. 3 with the three stages: dormant, erupted, lava core) and asked for round 3 (volcanoes and spikes grown into the back, darker, lava in the craters, a lean before the eruption); from round 3 the user picked 3G Saddleback and asked for changes (3H's horns, bigger back spikes, eruptions along the volcanoes, a deeper lean, a smoother walk), which were checked by a scripted in-game run but haven't been looked at by the user yet. Then the Cinder Colossus fight (step 8: three stages, six moves, two kinds of minion, temporary lava pools and fire trail, a custom boss bar, the statue death and "Set in Stone", the spawn egg, and the logic for `/dross boss summon colossus` and `/dross boss arena colossus`): it builds and passes its headless checks, but hasn't been tested in-game yet. Then the Cinder Colossus trophies (five candidate designs, the drop: design 1 for now), the stone and lava golem spawn eggs, and the creative tab grouped by kind: they build and pass their headless checks, but haven't been tested in-game yet. Then the `/dross` commands were renamed so each says what kind of thing it's about and which one (rule 9: `goto`, `showcase <kind> [<boss>]`, `boss <action> <boss>`, `trader`, `quest`; the old names are gone) and `/dross showcase trophies colossus` was added: they build and pass the headless checks, but haven't been tested in-game yet. Then, since the user liked all five trophy designs, right-clicking a placed trophy turns it to the next pose (1 > 2 > 3 > 4 > 5 > 1): it builds and passes its headless check, but hasn't been tested in-game yet. Then, after a play test, the Colossus takes less ranged damage the further the fight goes (50 / 35 / 15%) and its minions are 15% faster: they build and pass the headless checks, but haven't been tested in-game yet. Then **Terrain v1** (October 2026, branch `terrain-v1`; `TERRAIN_REPORT.md`): real terrain with six biomes, 38 blocks, features and ores, the hub fitted onto the land, the two survivors, wild golems in the Cinder Wastes, the Cinder Colossus's volcano lair and its fight rework, and `/dross goto biome` / `goto lair colossus`. It passes the headless checks (81 GameTests) and the user play-tested it twice (their feedback is built in); the parts listed in "Verify over time" still need a full in-game check. Then the **Rat King** (step 9, October 2026, worktree branch `rat-king-designs`): his design (round 1, then Gnawrick in seven builds; the user picked 1C), the two rats, 77 original sounds, three spawn eggs, and his fight (asleep, the sweep, the poison shot and cloud, burrowing with 7 rats, the Rage, the silly death and "Down the Drain", nine boss bar designs: the user picked 9); after a play test, the fixes the user asked for (his dirt hole hidden, a steady poison cloud, both bosses facing their target, his pop-up straight up, pickaxes doing 2x on the Colossus's shell, the Colossus's Lava Core roar earlier), the **Rage Wand** (step 10), and his five trophies (the user's picks from ten).
